# Brain Paper — Research Coordination

## Project Context
Mouse widefield closed-loop PI controller vs open-loop analysis.
Controller uses kernel average of a cortical region as output; PI controller drives input.
Two mice: AL_0033 (9 sessions), AL_0039 (4 sessions) = 13 controller sessions, Jan–Apr 2025. (Verified 2026-06-16 from loaded cache m1–m13.)

**Core question:** Does the closed-loop controller reduce neural variability and error compared to open-loop?
**Secondary questions:** Do motion, frequency-band power, and wide-brain activity explain trial-to-trial variability? Do these differ between OL and CL?

**For tasks and priorities → `TASKS.md`**
**For completed findings and paper claims → `FINDINGS.md`**
**For meeting decisions → `MEETINGS.md`**

---

## Change Log

### 2026-07-21 — ⚠ CORRECTION: the delta "survives magnitude control" claim was TRIAL-POOLING INFLATION; session-level relative-δ is NULL
**Changed/Found:** Built the OL-vs-CL factor panels (entry below) with a **per-session** slope (within-session Pearson r of RMSE on the factor, median across sessions, signed-rank OL vs CL) — the session-as-unit test the manuscript motion paragraph already uses. That exposed a problem with the `cl_rmse_factor_windows.m` decomposition's headline. Re-running delta at the session level, full RMSE and late-window (1–3 s) both: **abs-delta OL r≈+0.25 / CL r≈+0.36–0.48 (real within-session), but RELATIVE-δ (power-independent) OL r≈−0.03…−0.07 / CL r≈+0.01…+0.07 — essentially NULL in both loops.** So the earlier claim that "~55–60% of delta survives magnitude control (rel-δ R²=0.26 late)" was **trial-pooling inflation**: pooling 513 trials across 9–13 sessions lets between-session structure (high-delta sessions ARE high-RMSE sessions) masquerade as a within-trial effect. At the correct unit (session), the power-independent delta effect does not clearly survive; the within-session delta→RMSE relationship is almost entirely the signal-power (amplitude) confound, exactly the failure mode of the retracted impulse var/δ claim. Pre-stim-only abs-delta is also weak (OL +0.07 n.s., CL +0.18, diff n.s.).
**Why:** The decomposition's own logged "Next" flagged the pseudoreplication risk ("trial-level unit, no session random effect — add session-clustered check"). Doing that check is what caught it. Between-session confounding is precisely what a trial-pooled partial-R² cannot see.
**Next:** (1) The delta panel is still valid as a DESCRIPTIVE result (within sessions, both OL and CL residual error rise with delta-band amplitude; CL slope ≥ OL, so unlike motion the controller does NOT decouple it) — but it must NOT be captioned as a power-independent brain-state effect. (2) Re-caption/retire the `cl_rmse_factor_windows.m` "magnitude-robust" language; the partial-R² composite should carry a session-level or mixed-effects companion before ANY number from it goes in text. (3) The one genuinely power-independent, session-level-significant decoupling result remains **motion** (OL rises, CL flat). (4) Decide with AL whether delta enters Fig 4 as an honest "controller cannot reject slow-wave disturbance (amplitude)" panel or is dropped.

Per-session standardized slope (within-session Pearson r) + Wilcoxon signed-rank OL-vs-CL annotated. **Results (median within-session r, signrank p): Initial deviation [positive control, 13 sess/1470 tr] OL +0.49 / CL +0.66, p=0.068 — both loops rise steeply (circular by design, onset sample inside window; CL even steeper as controller ties onset to early error). Motion [9 sess/990 tr] OL +0.31 / CL −0.00, p=0.074 — the clean DECOUPLING (OL rises, CL flat). Delta [13 sess/1470 tr] OL +0.26 / CL +0.48, p=0.0012 — both rise, CL STEEPER (opposite of motion; see correction entry above — largely amplitude confound).** Exports → `paper/images/figure4/factor_olcl_{initdev,motion,delta}.pdf`. Code-analyzer clean (bar the cosmetic `deal` mlint warning).
**Why:** User wants the individual factor→error OL-vs-CL panels folded into Fig 4 (motion currently islanded as `fig:figure_motion`); they will assemble panels in Illustrator, so each is a standalone PDF in a matched style. Init-dev kept as an explicit positive control (user decision).
**Next:** (1) ⚠ The annotated motion p=0.074 (per-session Pearson-r signrank) DISAGREES with the manuscript's stated **p=0.008** (motion paragraph) — different per-session statistic (their z-RMSE regression via the canonical motion-significance computation vs my Pearson r). Do NOT ship two different p-values for the same panel; reconcile to the manuscript's method before assembly, or drop the stat annotation and let the rigorous script supply it. (2) Delta panel interpretation is under the correction above. (3) Motion panel here (energy, full RMSE) supersedes the islanded figQp visually — confirm with AL which motion panel is canonical. (4) LaTeX untouched pending AL's approval of the panel set.

### 2026-07-21 — `cl_rmse_factor_windows.m`: delta power replaces pre-trial std; early/late RMSE decomposition — a real, magnitude-robust delta effect that is window-specific
**Changed/Found:** New `controller-analysis/cl_rmse_factor_windows.m` (Figure-4 candidate). Replaces the `cl_mse_factors.m` X3 (pre-trial dF/F std) with **delta-band power** — `log10` of 1–4 Hz band power over **−2 s → stim end** (5.03 s, 176-sample Hann periodogram off `pwcDfk_l`, linear-detrended). X1 (init dev) and X2 (motion energy) held **identical** to `cl_mse_factors` for comparability. Outcome RMSE **split** into early **0–1 s** (transient) and late **1–3 s** (disturbance rejection). Partial (unique) R² per factor × window, 2000-trial bootstrap CIs. 513 CL trials / 9 sessions. **Results — full model R² 0.62 (early) / 0.44 (late). Partial R²: Init-dev 0.209 EARLY, ~0.000 LATE; Motion ~0.000 both; Delta 0.290 EARLY, 0.425 LATE.** The two magnitude-carrying factors **cleanly separate by window** — initial deviation is the early descent-to-ref, delta is the ongoing disturbance that dominates the settled window. Motion contributes nothing in either window (reconfirms `cl_mse_factors`). **Confound robustness (delta unique R², holding init-dev + motion): absolute 0.290/0.425 → relative-δ (band÷total, power-independent) 0.146/0.261 → pre-stim-only window 0.140/0.255.** So **~55–60% of delta's contribution survives magnitude control** in BOTH the relative-ratio and pre-stim-window tests — this is NOT the pure signal-power artifact the impulse-side variance/δ claim turned out to be. The pre-stim-only result also makes it a genuine *predictive* (pre-onset) state, not just a within-trial coincidence. Two activity-type exemplars auto-selected by slow-fraction confirm the user's visual read: slow-wave-dominated (m10 tr12, f_slow=0.87) vs fast-delta-dominated (m4 tr21, f_slow=0.01). Fig → `paper/images/figure4/cl_rmse_factor_windows.png` (6-panel: 2 exemplars + spectra + collinearity + windowed partial-R² + delta-robustness); cache → `data/cl_rmse_factor_windows.mat`. Code-analyzer clean.
**Why:** User inspected the `cl_mse_exemplars` figure and correctly judged pre-trial std too crude — it conflates short-duration delta waves with long large-amplitude slow waves. Delta power resolves the activity spectrally; the early/late split tests whether each factor acts on the transient or the hold; and the confound panel answers whether "delta" is real state or the same signal-power artifact retracted on the impulse side 2026-07-01.
**Next:** (1) This is the strongest Fig-4 state result to date and is largely magnitude-robust — promote to a paper panel (it already uses `paperFig`/`paperStyle`). (2) Add the **OL arm** — script is CL-only; the OL-vs-CL delta contribution difference is the decoupling test Fig 4 needs (does the controller flatten delta's late-window contribution?). (3) Single-mouse caveat unresolved: 513 trials but trial-level unit, no session/animal random effect — the R² CIs are trial-bootstrap, so pseudoreplicated; add a session-clustered or mixed-effects check before the headline number goes in text. (4) Consider splitting X3 into slow (0.4–1 Hz) vs delta (1–4 Hz) sub-bands — corr(log-delta, log-slow)=0.35 so they are only loosely coupled and may contribute differently to the late window. (5) `corr(log-delta, relative-delta)=0.66`, so relative-δ is a materially different regressor, not a rescale — its 0.26 late-window R² is the referee-proof number to quote.

### 2026-07-21 — `cl_mse_exemplars.m`: isolating trials make the error-factor contributions (and the confound) visible
**Changed/Found:** New `controller-analysis/cl_mse_exemplars.m` — companion to `cl_mse_factors.m`, reusing its three predictor definitions and windows verbatim so the two are directly comparable. Selects **isolating exemplars** (high on one factor, both others below median; thresholds auto-relax 85→70th pct if starved), which is only feasible because the predictors are near-uncorrelated. 4 rows × 3 trials: reference (all-low) + one row per factor. Each panel draws the dF/F trace over −2→+3 s, the ref line, the **shaded 0–3 s RMSE window**, the **X1 onset sample as a marker inside that shading**, the X3 pre-trial bracket, and the motion trace on a right axis. Ran on 513 pooled CL trials / 9 motion-bearing sessions. Code-analyzer clean. → `paper/images/figure4/cl_mse_exemplars.png` (PNG — diagnostic, not a designated paper panel).
**Found — the exemplars reproduce the partial-R² ordering and expose *why* each number comes out as it does.** Pool RMSE mean 2.32, median 1.99, sd 1.11. **(1) Initial deviation** (z +1.1…+1.7, e.g. m4 tr103, m6 tr8, m13 tr24): RMSE 1.9–2.8. The trace starts ~+5 %ΔF/F and the controller drags it down to ref over ~1 s — so the "error" in the shaded window **is the descent from the onset marker**. X1 does not predict the error; X1 is the *first sample of* the error. The circularity that was an arithmetic argument this morning is now directly visible in the panel. **(2) Motion** (z +2.0…+3.7, m6 tr2, m2 tr52, m11 tr6): motion trace is visibly spiky, but the dF/F trace tracks ref like the reference row and **RMSE is 1.4–1.8 — BELOW the pool mean of 2.32.** Strong motion trials are not high-error trials, exactly matching partial R² = 0.000. **(3) Pre-trial std** (z +3.5…+4.0, m4 tr19, m2 tr85, m2 tr66): RMSE 3.8–4.7, far above pool mean — but the trace oscillates ±10 % **both before AND after onset, without interruption at t=0.** So "pre-trial std predicts RMSE" is really "this is a high-amplitude-fluctuation trial throughout"; the pre-window and the outcome window are two samples of the same elevated signal magnitude. That is the same-signal confound rendered visually.
**Why:** User asked to see trials where the contributions of the three params to error can be shown. Isolating exemplars were chosen over top-error trials because the latter would conflate all three factors.
**Next:** This figure is the honest version of the Fig-4 state-dependence story and is a strong candidate panel *as a control* — it shows the reader why the naive R² decomposition overstates state-dependence. If it is promoted: re-export via `paperFig` at panel size, and add the OL arm (currently CL-only, inherited from `cl_mse_factors`). The natural follow-up is a fourth row using the **relative δ ratio** as the state — the admissible power-independent predictor — to test whether ANY state factor survives once magnitude is controlled.

### 2026-07-21 — Recovered `cl_mse_factors.m` results (never logged) — and the R² sits almost entirely on the two confounded predictors
**Changed/Found:** Located the error-attribution setup: `controller-analysis/cl_mse_factors.m` (added 2026-06-02, commit `297671f`; last touched `6080273`; wired into `run_all.m:32`). Hierarchical regression + variance partitioning of **CL** trial RMSE (`er_wcDfk`) on three predictors: **X1** = `abs(wcDfk(:,c0) - ref)`, `c0=36` (onset deviation); **X2** = `mean(wcmotion(:, ws:we).^2, 2)`, 2 s pre → trial end (motion energy); **X3** = `std(wcDfk(:, c0-35:c0-1), 0, 2)`, 1 s pre-onset (labelled "pre-trial std"; the header calls it a *proxy for LF/slow variance* — it is **not** a spectral δ measure). **Results were never written to RESEARCH or FINDINGS**, but the three output figures are on disk (`paper/cl_mse_pred_corr.png`, `cl_mse_waterfall_r2.pdf`, `cl_mse_partial_r2.pdf`, all 2026-06-04 15:57), so it did run. **Numbers read off the figures: full model R² = 0.448, unexplained 0.552. Hierarchical — X1 alone 0.137, +X2 motion ΔR² = 0.001, +X3 pre-trial std ΔR² = 0.311. Partial (unique) R² — X1 0.070, X2 motion 0.000, X3 0.311, shared 0.067. Predictor correlations all near zero (X1·X2 = 0.03, X1·X3 = 0.18, X2·X3 = −0.06), so collinearity is genuinely low and the hierarchical entry order barely matters.**
**Why:** User asked to look up the prior error-contribution/attribution setup (initial deviation, motion, δ).
**Found — the decomposition is confounded in exactly the pattern of today's Fig-4 audit.** Both predictors that explain anything are magnitudes of the **same `wcDfk` trace as the outcome**: X1's sample `c0=36` sits **inside** the RMSE window (0→+3 s), the identical circularity as `fig_ps_mse`; X3 is same-signal but window-disjoint (1 s pre-onset), the K2-class confound. **X2 (motion) is the only power-independent predictor — and it is the one that explains essentially nothing (ΔR² 0.001, partial 0.000).** So the headline "45% of CL error explained" is largely *signal magnitude predicting signal magnitude*, and the result reads closer to a positive control for the confound than to a brain-state finding. The low inter-predictor correlations do NOT rescue it — motion being uncorrelated with the other two is exactly why it cannot absorb their variance.
**Next:** Do **not** quote R²=0.448 as explained variance without the caveat. Three fixes, in order: (1) replace X3 with the **relative** δ ratio (band ÷ total, already computed at `trial_state_mse.m:108-111`, available as `ncFreqSpec`/`S_norm` in `controllerData.m:34`) — the admissible power-independent version of the "δ" predictor this script only ever proxied; (2) drop or re-window X1 so it stops sitting inside the outcome window; (3) add an **OL arm** — the script is CL-only (`er_wcDfk`/`wcDfk` throughout), so it cannot support the OL-vs-CL decoupling contrast that motivates Fig 4. Also note it exports loose PDFs to `paper/` root via the CWD-dependent `paper_root` resolver already flagged as a bug in TASKS.md.

### 2026-07-21 — Shared-TF 3-way test: amplitude-linearity FAILS; per-pair γ is UNIDENTIFIED; hard ceiling R²≈0.68
**Changed/Found:** Extended `tf_fit.fit_shared` with (a) per-amplitude residual weighting `_shared_weights` (`none`/`amp`/`rms`) and (b) a static input nonlinearity `f(a)=(a/a_ref)^γ`, `a_ref`=min amp (anchoring at a_ref breaks the h↔f scale degeneracy and makes h = the response AT amp 1.0). γ bounded [0.1,3.0], seeded 1.0 (neutral). SSE/R² kept UNWEIGHTED so variants stay comparable. CLI: `tf_fit.py shared` (weighted, γ free) / `shared linear` (weighted, γ=1) / `shared raw` (legacy). Ran three variants:
| variant | weighting | γ | amp1.0 R² | amp2.0 R² |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A `..._raw` | none | 1 | −0.24 | +0.57 |
| B `..._linw` | ÷(a/a_ref) | 1 | +0.30 | −0.34 |
| C `..._shared` | ÷(a/a_ref) | free | +0.57 | −0.11 |
**Found (three results, in order of importance):**
1. **Amplitude-linearity genuinely fails — it is NOT a weighting artifact.** With γ=1 the error budget is CONSERVED and merely MOVES between amplitudes: unweighted LS spends accuracy on amp 2.0 (0.57) and abandons amp 1.0 (−0.24); weighting equally flips it (0.30 / −0.34). Fixing the weighting relocated the failure instead of removing it. (This retires the earlier caveat that the 0.518 gap might be mostly LS high-amp dominance — that concern was real but not the explanation.)
2. **A hard ceiling exists for ANY "one waveform + amplitude scaling" model.** On the 113 pairs well fit at both amps, the amp1→amp2 waveform **shape cosine** (scale removed) is median **0.858** (10–90%: 0.554–0.966) — similar but NOT identical shapes — and the BEST possible per-pair scalar rescale achieves only median R² **0.681**. No shared-h model can beat that, vs 0.96 for independent per-amp fits. So the response waveform itself changes somewhat with power; shared dynamics is approximately, not exactly, right.
3. **Per-pair γ is UNIDENTIFIED and must be global.** Variant C's γ: median 0.474 but IQR [0.100, 0.883] with **17.7% railing at the 0.1 lower bound**. Three independent γ estimates disagree — **0.266** (waveform-optimal rescale k, median k=1.203, all well-fit pairs, model-free), **0.474** (variant C per-pair), **0.573** (on-site focal gain ratio 1.49). All ≪1 → saturation is real, but two amplitudes do not pin its magnitude. Physically γ belongs to laser/opsin transduction, i.e. ONE global constant, not a per-connection parameter; fitting it per pair let it absorb noise. Note off-diagonal pairs scale less (k=1.20) than on-site focal ones (1.49).
**Why:** user asked to (1) re-run the shared fit with 1/a weighting and (2) add the static input nonlinearity.
**Next:** refit with a **single GLOBAL γ** (per-pair h, one shared γ). Do NOT profile γ by re-running the full 52×52 per γ (~15 min each); γ is estimable model-free from the per-pair optimal rescale k (no TF fit needed), so: estimate global γ from the k distribution on well-fit pairs → refit `fit_shared` with that γ FIXED → compare against the R²≈0.68 ceiling. Also worth reporting the ceiling itself as the honest limit of any amplitude-scaling model for this data.

### 2026-07-21 — ⚠ Fig 4 candidate-panel AUDIT: 4 of 5 candidates are signal-power confounded, 2 algebraically circular
**Changed/Found:** Audited every candidate Fig-4 (state-dependence) panel against the 2026-07-01 signal-power retraction, which had only ever been applied to the IMPULSE stream — never to the controller stream. Results: **(1) `motion_analysis.m` `fig_ps_mse`** — x = `abs(dk.ncDfk(:,c0_g2) - ref)` where `c0_g2 = 36` is the ONSET sample, y = `er_ncDfk` = `norm(dFk(0:+3s) - ref)/sqrt(N)`. The x-sample sits *inside* the y-window — it is literally the first term of the y sum, so a positive slope follows from autocorrelation alone with zero brain-state structure. **(2) `trial_state_mse.m`** — x = `var(pncDfk_l(:, 106:210))` = var(dFk, 0→+3 s), y = RMSE(dFk, 0→+3 s) over the *identical* window. Since `RMSE² = var + (mean−ref)²`, the correlation is near-algebraic, not empirical. Worse, its `corr` runs on **20-trial block means** (`blockSize=20`, sorted along the variance axis before binning), which inflates r and deflates p. **(3) retired `prestim_variance.m` K2/K2m** — same-signal but window-DISJOINT (x = var over −3→0 s), so the mildest of the set; still vulnerable to gain/hemodynamic drift raising both. **K2w is severe** (x window −3→+3 s fully contains y). **(4) retired `spectral_mse_sort.m` fig_J/J2** — rows sorted by RMSE, coloured by ABSOLUTE band power of the same `dFk` over an overlapping window; by Parseval total band power *is* the segment variance, so the colour gradient is an arithmetic identity. **(5) `figQp` (motion quartiles) is the ONLY clean panel** — motion energy is an independent behavioural measurement, not a magnitude of `dFk`. Two power-independent assets already exist in code but are on no axis: the normalized δ ratio (`trial_state_mse.m:108-111`) and `ncFreqSpec`/`S_norm` (`controllerData.m:34`, which `spectral_mse_sort.m:38-44` explicitly DEPRIORITISED in favour of absolute power). PAPER.md Fig-4 block + candidate table updated with the verdicts.
**Why:** The Fig-4 candidate list was assembled BEFORE the 2026-07-01 retraction and was never re-read in its light. The retraction's logic (a state regressor that is a magnitude of the same signal as the outcome is entangled with it) transfers directly to the controller, where the outcome RMSE is itself a magnitude measure of `dFk`. Building Fig 4 from these panels would have shipped the exact confound the impulse stream already retracted — to a referee who will have read both figures.
**Next:** Fig 4 must be rebuilt from (a) `figQp` motion, (b) Stage 5 `s_lvl→o_act`, and (c) a *relative* spectral state (the normalized δ ratio, which is already computed and merely unplotted) — NOT from the retired K2/J panels. Re-run `trial_state_mse.m` with `varWinMode='pre2trial'` if a variance panel is still wanted, since that at least restores window-disjointness. Decide whether `fig_ps_mse` is deleted or kept as a supplementary "why we don't use this" control.

### 2026-07-21 — ⚠ Stage 5: only 1 of its 3 reported state→outcome pairs is confound-free; the "actuator gain" headline is not identified
**Changed/Found:** Re-read `ctrl_state_dependence.m:78-84` against the same confound criterion. **`s_lvl→o_act` is CLEAN**: `s_lvl = mean(G(:,bwin))` is built from `Uflat(gridIdx,:)` with `gridIdx` drawn strictly inside `contra_mask` and further filtered to unaffected pixels (`Su`), so Global reads **zero ipsi pixels**, while `o_act = mean(A(:,sswin))` is the ipsi site — cross-signal AND window-disjoint (−1→0 s vs +1→+3 s). This is the defensible Fig-4 headline. **`s_var→o_loc` is same-signal**: `s_var = var(A(:,bwin))` and `o_loc` both derive from the identical ipsi trace `A`, so a high-power trial inflates both. **`s_dst→o_loc` is near-tautological**: `s_dst` is exactly the `G` excursion term that `o_loc` subtracts, over the same `twin` — regressing a quantity on minus itself, biased toward −1. **The consequence for the Stage-5 entry below:** finding (2) reported OL b=0.031 vs CL b=0.033 as "essentially IDENTICAL" and read it mechanistically as a state-dependent *actuator gain* that is a plant property. But a shared signal-power artifact predicts exactly the same thing — a positive slope of equal size in both conditions. So identical-OL-vs-CL does **not** discriminate "genuine plant property" from "shared confound"; that pair is unidentified as stated. Note the OL-vs-CL *difference* is still meaningful even under the confound (it cancels), which is precisely why finding (1)'s decoupling result survives and finding (2)'s mechanistic reading does not.
**Why:** Stage 5's own log entry presented all three findings at equal evidential weight. They are not equal, and the one that got the strongest mechanistic interpretation is the least identified.
**Next:** Either (a) drop the actuator-gain claim, or (b) re-run `s_var→o_loc` with a power-independent state — normalized δ ratio or motion — which would break the shared-magnitude link. Do this BEFORE the cross-session sweep, so the sweep is not spent on a pair that cannot be interpreted. `s_lvl→o_act` remains the pair worth the sweep. Also still open from the Stage-5 entry: robustness of `s_lvl→o_act` to the low-`s_lvl` OL outliers (Theil-Sen).

### 2026-07-21 — 20-trial OL/CL decomposition grids: per-trial Global is NOISY on high-variance trials
**Changed/Found:** Generated two 4×5 trial grids (ad hoc from the Stage-2/3 caches, not a committed script) → `paper/images/predictor_saga/ctrl_ol_trials_grid_m4.png` (20 of 92 OL) and `ctrl_cl_trials_grid_m4.png` (20 of 108 CL); each panel = Actual (black) / Global (orange) / Local (blue) with the residual shaded. **Observations: (a) pre-onset Global tracks Actual tightly in nearly every trial — the predictor validates per-trial, not just on average, and the residual sits ~0 pre-stim; (b) the most convincing trials are those where Global goes UP during stim (OL tr 11/63/73, CL tr 12/74) — the network was heading positive while the laser pulled down, proving Global is not passively echoing the dip; (c) OL Local dips cluster tightly (−2.0 to −5.2%) while CL is wider (−0.8 to −5.0%) with much larger ongoing swings on some trials (CL tr 29 →−15%, tr 40 →−14%).** ⚠️ **(d) On high-variance trials the SINGLE-TRIAL Global becomes wild (CL tr 40, tr 7: orange swinging ±10%).**
**Why:** Global's spontaneous held-out R² is only 0.680, so single-trial predictions carry substantial error and the per-trial Local inherits it. The trial-AVERAGED decomposition (91–94% local) is robust; per-trial Local values are not.
**Next:** This is a concrete attenuation source for the Stage-5 per-trial regressions (noisy regressor/outcome biases slopes toward 0) — another reason the marginal decoupling CI needs the cross-session sweep. Consider an errors-in-variables or trial-binned version of the Stage-5 slopes, and/or weighting trials by Global prediction confidence.

### 2026-07-21 — Stage 5: state dependence — controller decouples state→outcome but NOT state→actuator gain
**Changed/Found:** New `ctrl_state_dependence.m`. Rebuilds ABSOLUTE per-trial Actual/Global for OL (n=92) and CL (n=108) from the Stage 1/2 predictor — necessary because the cached traces are baseline-subtracted, which forces pre-stim level to 0 by construction and makes it useless as a state regressor. State: `s_lvl` = pre-stim absolute Global level, `s_var` = pre-stim Actual variance, `s_dst` = during-trial Global excursion. Outcome: `o_loc` = Local/residual (bs, [0,3]s), `o_act` = final absolute level ([1,3]s). OLS slope + Pearson r; OL-vs-CL slope difference by trial bootstrap (5000, percentile CI). **Results: (1) DECOUPLING — `s_lvl→o_act`: OL b=0.340 (r=0.40, p<0.001) vs CL b=0.084 (r=0.23, p=0.015), a 4× shallower CL slope; bootstrap dSlope CI [−0.501, +0.001] = right at the significance boundary. (2) ACTUATOR GAIN — `s_var→o_loc`: OL b=0.031 (r=0.24, p=0.020), CL b=0.033 (r=0.25, p=0.010) — essentially IDENTICAL, dCI [−0.043,+0.050]; higher pre-stim fluctuation ⇒ LESS negative Local (weaker inhibition). (3) `s_lvl→o_loc`: OL null (r=−0.05, p=0.65), CL weak (r=−0.26, p=0.008), dCI includes 0.** Fig → `paper/images/predictor_saga/ctrl_state_dependence_AL_0033_0226_e2.png`; cache → `data/ctrl_state_dependence_*.mat`.
**Why:** Answers the standing "K2 OL vs CL slope" open question using a principled state estimate (stim-blind Global) instead of crude pre-stim variance. The two findings are complementary and mechanistically coherent: the controller **does** absorb brain state before it reaches the outcome (its job), but **cannot** remove the state-dependence of the laser's own gain — a plant property, identical in OL and CL, which it can only compensate for. Finding (2) is measured in OL where the command is a FIXED step, so it is genuine state-dependent actuator gain, not a controller artifact.
**Next:** The decoupling headline is MARGINAL (CI upper bound +0.001) on one session — needs the cross-session sweep (Stage 1→2 caches on the other 12 sessions) before it becomes a claim. Check robustness of `s_lvl→o_act` to the low-`s_lvl` OL outliers (Theil-Sen / robust regression) since the OL line spans a wider x-range. Still open: the `data.dFk` reconstruction check gating the Stage 4b "PI over-inhibits" headline.

### 2026-07-21 — SHARED TF (amplitude as INPUT, not a fitted per-amp gain) + notebook `tf_grid_explore.ipynb`
**Changed/Found:** Added `tf_fit.fit_shared()` (+ `_fit_order_shared`, `fit_lti_cv_shared`, CLI `python tf_fit.py shared`, cache `data/grid_tf_fits_shared.npz`): **ONE** transfer function per (stim,readout) pair shared across ALL amplitudes, where the response to laser amplitude `a` is `a·h(t)`. **Deliberately NO free per-amp gain** — user's correction: a fitted per-amp scalar would absorb the amplitude dependence as a parameter, when physically the larger drive is what produces the larger response. This is the strict LTI statement (same dynamics AND same sensitivity). Caches shared `tau/A/om/ph`, the per-unit-amplitude `base` h(t), per-amp `yhat`/`gain`, pooled `r2`, per-amp `r2_amp`, and pooled split-half `cvr2`. Kept `fit_all_amps` (independent per-amp) — it is the LTI *test* that justified sharing (τ ratio 0.951 ≈ 1 over 113 pairs).
**Found (in flight):** the shared model's pooled R² runs far below the independent fits (first rows ~0.12–0.41 vs 0.85). **Expected, not a failure:** the shared model FORCES a ×2.0 ratio between amp 1.0 and 2.0 while the measured focal ratio is ~1.4–1.5×, so its systematic residual IS the saturation / departure-from-amplitude-linearity measure. Notebook §4 plots shared-vs-independent per-amp R² so the gap is readable as that quantity.
**Also:** new **`bilateral/grid/tf_grid_explore.ipynb`** (15 cells) — interactive companion to the module set, following the existing notebook-beside-modules pattern (`network_id_explore.ipynb`). Loads the caches and plots inline; documents the full file map + regeneration commands. Imports only backend-safe modules (`tf_fit`, `cross_response`) because `tf_matrix`/`plots`/`bidirectional` call `matplotlib.use("Agg")` at import and would kill inline figures. Sections: reliability (why in-sample R² must never be the gate) → per-pair data+fit inspector → shared-vs-independent amplitude linearity → spatial efferent field (CV-gated, hollow = untrustworthy) → bidirectional sites → standing caveats.
**Why:** user asked for one TF for both amps with the input driving the amplitude, and for a notebook version of the pipeline.
**Next:** shared fit still running (`tf_fit.py shared`) — report pooled/per-amp R² and the shared-vs-independent gap when it lands; decide whether the saturation is better modelled as a static input nonlinearity (e.g. fit `a^γ` or a saturating `f(a)` in front of one LTI h) rather than as residual.

### 2026-07-20 — 2-amp osc refit + `tf_matrix` gate FIXED (in-sample R² → held-out CV-R²)
**Changed/Found:** 2-amp cache refit under `MODEL="osc"`: amp 1.0 order hist [1548,617,539] medR²=0.78 medCV=−2.57; amp 2.0 [1457,633,614] medR²=0.85 medCV=−1.54 (amp-2.0 matches the single-amp cache exactly = consistency check passes). **Fixed a regression the model change introduced:** `tf_matrix.amp_compare` gated the focal-linearity subset on in-sample `r2>=0.4`, but under osc R² no longer separates signal from noise (weak pairs hit R²≈0.81) — the gate admitted **42** diagonal pairs (vs 17 under the real model) and degraded the focal fit to r=0.59. Re-gated on held-out `cvr2>=cvr2_min` (0.2) and dropped the now-meaningless `r2_strong` arg → **n=14, r=0.722, slope=0.834, median ratio=1.49**. τ-stability panel unchanged in logic (already CV-gated) but now passes **113** pairs (was 61) since osc improves CV on real pairs; median τ ratio **0.951** ≈ 1 → decay constants still amplitude-invariant.
**Found:** across every model/gate variant tried today the **median focal gain RATIO is stable at ~1.4–1.5** (1.37 → 1.40 → 1.46 → 1.49) while the through-origin SLOPE swings 0.83–1.25 depending on model and gate. The ratio is the robust statistic; **do not quote the slope as a precise dose-response number.** Sublinear (<×2) either way.
**Why:** completes the osc adoption across both caches and repairs the reliability filter that the more-flexible model silently broke.
**Next:** (1) two visible fit pathologies in `grid_tf_amp_compare.png` — the per-pair R² histogram is now **piled at 1.0** (overfitting signature; R² should probably stop being displayed as a quality metric), and some τ **rail at the 3.0 s upper bound**, where a slow low-frequency mode acts as a trend/DC term over a 0.6 s window rather than real dynamics; consider tightening the τ bound to ~1 s or penalising near-DC modes. (2) gain matrix still UNMASKED — with 2204 pairs at CV-R²≈−2.1 it should be reliability-masked before use (asked, not yet approved). (3) amplitude-invariance still tests τ only; under osc it should test τ AND ω.

### 2026-07-20 — TF model class → DAMPED SINUSOIDS ("osc"): 3× CV-R² on real pairs, but WORSE on noise
**Changed/Found:** New `bilateral/grid/tf_sweep.py` (hyperparameter + model-class sweep, selects on HELD-OUT CV-R², not in-sample R² — the latter is what you maximise to overfit). 150-pair sweep at matched `tmax=0.6`: real ord-2 CV **−0.012**, real ord-3 (old production) **+0.232**, real ord-4 +0.268; osc ord-2 +0.393, **osc ord-3 +0.637**, osc ord-4 +0.662. Adopted **`MODEL="osc"`, order ≤3, delay 0, tmax 0.6, onset_pre 0.10** in `tf_fit.py`: each mode is now a DAMPED SINUSOID `A·exp(−t/τ)·sin(ωt+φ)` = a complex-conjugate pole pair at −1/τ±jω; `MODEL="real"` kept as the ω→0 special case. Order 3 over 4 because +0.025 CV is INSIDE the existing `CV_MARGIN=0.03` (own parsimony rule) at 2.6× runtime; delay stays 0 (0.662 vs 0.621). `tmax=0.35` scored higher but that comparison is INVALID — R²/CV-R² are computed over the fit window itself, so a shorter window is an easier target, not a better model. BIC penalty fixed to 4 params/mode for osc. Caches gained `om`/`ph`/`model`; `tau` now means mode DECAY CONSTANT. `interactive_grid.poles_zeros` rewritten for complex poles (−1/τ±jω) with the zeros taken as roots of the combined numerator over the common denominator, plus complex-safe printing.
**Why the model class, not the knobs:** a sum of DECAYING REAL exponentials cannot oscillate, but the responses visibly dip→rebound→overshoot, so the rebound was structurally unmodellable — a hard R² ceiling no hyperparameter could lift.
**Found — ⚠ the headline medians are misleading in BOTH directions.** Single-amp refit reports median R² 0.29→**0.85** but median CV-R² **−1.13→−1.54 (worse)**. Stratifying by response strength explains it: top-150 pairs medR²=0.992 **medCV=+0.623** (81% CV>0 — reproduces the sweep's +0.637 exactly); top-500 medCV=+0.153; the remaining **2204 weak/noise pairs medR²=0.809 but medCV=−2.105 (only 12% CV>0)**. So osc fits noise *beautifully* in-sample and predicts nothing — MORE overfitting than the real model on the noise majority, because it is more flexible. Median-over-all-pairs R² overstates the gain; median CV-R² hides the genuine 3× improvement on real-signal pairs. Order hist [1457,633,614].
**Next:** (1) **Reliability masking is now MANDATORY, not optional** — any figure or claim must restrict to CV-R²>0 pairs (the viewer's CV-R² dimming already does this visually; the gain matrix still does not). (2) Fitted mode frequencies on CV-R²>0.3 pairs (n=681): median **2.71 Hz**, percentiles [10,25,50,75,90] = [0.79, 1.51, 2.71, 4.59, 7.52] — broadly 1–7 Hz, overlapping `network_id.py`'s 7–8 Hz graph-wave band **only in the top decile**, so this is NOT clean corroboration of the wave model; report as partial/tail overlap only. (3) `tf_matrix` amplitude-invariance check still compares only τ — under osc it should test τ AND ω (frequency invariance is the stronger LTI test).

### 2026-07-21 — DECIDED NOT to port Fig 3's windowed ratio panels (3I/3J) into Fig 5 — insufficient trials
**Changed/Found:** Nothing built. Considered adding sine-wave equivalents of **3I** (`fig_Fr`, OL/CL variance ratio over Pre/Stim/Post) and **3J** (`fig_G2r`, OL/CL RMSE ratio over Pre/0–1 s/1–3 s/Post) as new Fig 5 panels 5I/5J. **Rejected.**
**Why:** Those Fig 3 panels derive their statistics from **13 sessions** — one paired OL-vs-CL ratio point per session, Wilcoxon **signed-rank** across sessions. Fig 5 is a **single session** (2026-07-14), so the unit would have to drop to trials, and trials are **not paired across modes** (n = 51/46/46/57, different trials per mode). Consequences: signed-rank → rank-sum (unpaired), and one session yields a *single* ratio value per window per mode, so 3I/3J's scatter-of-per-session-points has nothing to scatter — error bars would have to be bootstrapped over trials, a materially weaker claim. Two panels that *look* like Fig 3's but rest on much thinner evidence is a referee liability, not an asset. User: "leave it because we dont have enough trials."
**Next:** Revisit only if a second matched-parameter sine session is recorded (that would also restore the cross-session replication claim (a) lost when 2026-07-01/6 was dropped from Fig 5 — see the 07-21 single-session entry). Until then Fig 5's error/variance evidence stays as 5E (RMSE over time), 5F (variance over time) and 5G (trial RMSE distribution + rank-sum).

Shared col1 time base: 5B/5C draw the corner bar with a BLANK XLabel, only 5D labels it (`shortCornerAxes_plot` validates `XLength>0` and always draws both arms, so a Y-only bar isn't available). Added `MODE_SHORT` (now also feeding 5H's ticks). Two formatting fixes found by inspecting the render: **5E's legend sat on top of the traces at 5.2 cm** → removed (mode key is carried by the 5B/5C column titles); **5G had no y-scale and no mode labels** (`paperAxes(ax)` with no args strips every tick) → added a corner Y bar + OL/OL+p/CL/CL+p labels. All 7 panels export clean; mlint clean.
**Found (TWO claim corrections — both go to the manuscript):** (1) **Claim (c) is false as written.** It said CL+prev is "the **only** mode whose error falls within the trial". Per-mode early(0–1 s)→late(1–4 s) RMSE: **OL 4.553→4.472 (−0.080, ALSO falls)**, OL+prev 4.802→4.892 (+0.090), CL 3.867→3.946 (+0.080), **CL+prev 4.009→3.668 (−0.341)**. The computation is identical to the one behind the original claim (CL+prev 16.07→13.45 MSE = 4.009→3.668 RMSE) — the "only" was simply never checked against the other three modes. Corrected to the *margin* claim (CL+prev falls ~4× more than any other mode). (2) **Preview's error direction FLIPS under RMSE.** Mean MSE said preview raised OL error (20.2→23.7); mean RMSE says it marginally lowered it (4.10→4.04). Cause: mean-MSE is outlier-dominated (squaring amplifies the ~10 clipped motion trials) while sqrt compresses them — so `mean(RMSE) ≠ sqrt(mean(MSE))`. Neither direction is reportable; the rank test (p=0.32, invariant) is. **p-values reproduced exactly as predicted** — OL vs CL 7.709e-4 (recorded 0.00077), OL vs OL+prev 0.317 (recorded 0.32) — confirming the monotone-transform argument. Variance numbers are in variance units and are unchanged (OL 17.93→OL+prev 23.20 matches the recorded 17.9→23.2).
**Why:** User: "sine_ff_plots_combined.m do this one" + RMSE consistency with Fig 3 + inputs not visible on the single-trial figures.

### 2026-07-20 — Bidirectional CORRECTED: 0–200 ms window blended a BIPHASIC response → 42/52 (was 21/52)
**Changed/Found:** The earlier 21/52 result used a single 0–200 ms mean, which is **wrong for this data**: the response is BIPHASIC (excitatory peak ~70 ms, suppression trough ~140–240 ms), so a 0–200 ms mean blends the lobes and reports whichever dominates rather than the drive. Verified directly: stim(−1.5,−2)→self reads **+1.27%** over 0–200 ms although its true peak is **+3.52% @71 ms** (0–100 ms = +2.61%, 100–250 ms = −0.36%). `bidirectional.py` now tests TWO windows separately — **early 0–100 ms** (excitatory lobe) and **late 100–250 ms** (suppression lobe) — FDR across all pairs × windows (5408 tests, 873 sig, 807 also ≥0.5%); a site is drivable in a direction if EITHER window qualifies. **Result: 42/52 bidirectional** (up-only 2, down-only 7, neither 1). Added `--min-driver-ml` to exclude near-midline drivers. **Bug fixed:** `classify` used `nS = eff.shape[0]`, which became the WINDOW count once eff went 3-D — only 2 readouts were being classified (result read "2/52").
**Found (two prior flags REFUTED + one real structure):** (1) the "left-hemisphere site acts as inhibitory driver" anomaly is **NOT** midline beam spread — excluding |ML|≤0.5 drivers barely moved it (21→18) and the top down-drivers were still lateral left sites (−1.5,−2/−3/−4). It is genuine **lateral/surround suppression**: stim(−1.5,−2) EXCITES itself (+3.5%, correct excitatory polarity) while SUPPRESSING distant (−1.5,+2) (−1.99% @142 ms). (2) Readout (−1.5,+2) is excited by (−3.5,−1) (+8.26%) and suppressed by (−1.5,−2) — so **bidirectional control is achievable from the EXCITATORY opsin alone via network topology**, not only by using both opsins. (3) **0/42 use the same driver for + and −** → none are the trivial one-biphasic-response case; all 42 use genuinely different actuators. BUT only **2/42** get both directions within the SAME window: for the rest, UP comes from the early lobe and DOWN from the late lobe → **the two actuation directions have different latencies (~70 ms vs ~140–240 ms)**, which a closed-loop controller must treat as asymmetric actuation delay.
**Why:** user asked to identify grid points adequately affected in both directions; the first pass was window-confounded.
**Next:** highest dynamic range is left anterior-medial ((−1.5,+2)/(−1.5,+1)/(−0.5,+1), up to ~8% ΔF/F). The broad excitatory field still carries the single-wavelength hemodynamic confound → the focal DOWN drive remains the more trustworthy direction. Consider a latency-resolved version (per-window driver maps) before using this to pick controller actuation sites.

### 2026-07-20 — Prediction plots added; SELECT's L1 SHRINKAGE may bias the capture/leak split
**Changed/Found:** `run_ols.py` — added `plot_spont_prediction` (held-out spontaneous actual vs full-grid OLS vs sparse SELECT, + predicted-vs-actual scatter) and `plot_trialpred` (per-trial ipsi prediction + residual, 5 trials/amp, per side; `ols_trialpred_{left,right}.png`). Engine now returns `pred_by_amp`/`resid_by_amp` (single-trial) and the held-out spont prediction traces. **The scatter shows clear regression-to-mean: SELECT systematically UNDER-predicts magnitude (L1 shrinkage, `select_wlasso` does NOT debias — nor does the MATLAB original).** Since Global IS the SELECT prediction, shrinkage biases Global toward 0 → **leak under-estimated, capture over-estimated**. So excit-85%/inhib-63% are upper bounds on capture.
**Why:** Wanted to see the predictor actually predicting rather than trust the scalar R²; the shrinkage is only visible in the scatter.
**Next:** Test with a DEBIASED refit (plain OLS on the SELECT-selected pixels, keeping the sparsity but dropping the L1 shrinkage) and see whether the excit-confined/inhib-broad asymmetry survives. If it does, the claim is safe; if the excit leak grows a lot, it was shrinkage. Flag upstream — the MATLAB §17d has the same bias.

### 2026-07-20 — TRIALPRED: single-trial variability dwarfs the evoked effect (~5-10x)
**Changed/Found:** In `ols_trialpred_*.png`, single-trial ipsi traces span ±5–10 %ΔF/F while the trial-averaged evoked effect is only ~0.5–1 %. The per-trial stim-blind prediction TRACKS per-trial ongoing state well (red follows grey, including a −10 % excursion at 1.0 V), and residualizing visibly shrinks the spread (e.g. 2.5 V: actual ±6 → residual ±3) — the SNR gain the method buys. But the residual amp-average (~−0.5 %) is still several-fold smaller than the single-trial residual spread (±2–3 %).
**Why:** Needed before stage 3 to know what the state-dependence test is working with.
**Next:** Stage 3 will be POWER-LIMITED at ~50 trials/amp given that spread — plan to pool amps (z-within-amp, as the MATLAB §17c does) rather than test per-amp, and expect small effect sizes. Don't over-read a null.

### 2026-07-20 — SELECT replaces NATIVE in the AL_0048 port; excit local effect is CONFINED, inhib LEAKS
**Changed/Found:** `bilateral/impulse/impulse_ols.py` — swapped the stim-blind model from the (upstream-RETIRED 2026-07-18) §17b NATIVE KKT projection to **§17d [STIMBLIND-SELECT]**: ported `cd_lasso` + `select_wlasso` (sparse spont OLS on stim-UNAFFECTED px with an L1 penalty that GROWS toward the ipsi site → few predictors, all FAR from it; `l1frac=0.15`, `penNear=2.0`, `penFar=0.2`). No dip-blinding, so Global is an HONEST LEAK. Result on AL_0048 (peak-based, the valid readout): **excit capture 85% / leak 15%; inhib capture 63% / leak 37%** (nActive only 5–8 px; predR² 0.64–0.73). The inhib leak is **dose-graded** (Global peak −0.46→−0.79 over amps) → genuine stim propagation into far contralateral pixels, not predictor noise.
**Why:** NATIVE cancelled the dip BY CONSTRUCTION, so its ~100% capture (what my 2026-07-20 first port reported as medLocal≈91–101%) was guaranteed, not informative. SELECT's capture/leak split is the meaningful measurement — and it changes the interpretation.
**Next:** The excit-confined vs inhib-broad asymmetry is the headline dual-opsin claim; it needs (a) a second session, (b) a check that it survives varying `select_l1frac`/`penNear/penFar` (only 5–8 predictors survive — leak may depend on WHICH few), (c) reconciliation with AL_0033 [CP-STIMAFF] ("~95% of contra co-suppresses"), which predicts exactly this broad inhibitory propagation.

### 2026-07-20 — SELECT readout: peak-based capture/leak; flag amps where the dip-window mean has the wrong sign
**Changed/Found:** `run_ols.py` now prints capture/leak on BOTH the signed peak and the dip-window mean, marking with `!` any amp where `sign(meanActual) != side sign`. The mean-based ratio is unusable at two kinds of amp: near-threshold ones where Actual≈0 (inhib 1.5 V → cap −137%, leak 237%) and excit amps where the 0–300 ms mean cancels the fast transient against its undershoot (excit 1.5 V → 6% by mean vs 107% by peak).
**Why:** Ratios to a near-zero or sign-flipped denominator are meaningless; without the flag they read as real numbers and would have been interpreted.
**Next:** Consider a per-side dip window (excit transient is much faster than the inhib trough) derived data-driven like S15 SETTLETIME, instead of the shared 0.300 s — that would make the mean-based (exactly additive) capture usable on the excit side too. Exclude inhib 1.5 V and excit 0.5 V from interpretation until then.

### 2026-07-20 — Bidirectionally drivable grid sites: 21/52 (new `bilateral/grid/bidirectional.py`)
**Changed/Found:** New `bidirectional.py` — identifies readout sites adequately driven BOTH up and down by at least one of the 52 photostim sites. Deliberately uses EMPIRICAL trial data (not the TF fits, which are mid-revision): per-trial dF/F, each trial baselined to its own pre-onset mean, fixed-window mean over **0–200 ms** (project `peak_mode=3` convention, NOT peak → avoids argmax selection bias); one-sample t-test per pair; **BH-FDR across all 2704 pairs BEFORE any max is taken** (asking "is there ANY of 52 drivers" is a 52-way selection that manufactures winners from noise); then a practical floor |eff| ≥ 0.5% dF/F. **Result: 497/2704 pairs FDR-sig (462 also ≥0.5%); 21/52 readouts BIDIRECTIONAL, 6 up-only, 20 down-only, 5 neither.** Bidirectional sites cluster in the LEFT (excitatory) hemisphere + near-midline anterior region; best dynamic range 5.22% dF/F at (−0.5,+1). Right hemisphere is largely DOWN-only (inhibitory opsin, no excitatory reach). Fig `grid_png/grid_bidirectional.png` (3 panels: bidirectional map + best-up + best-down driver), cache `data/grid_bidirectional.npz`.
**Why:** user asked how to identify grid points adequately affected in both directions (bidirectional control authority — the point of the dual-opsin prep).
**Next:** ⚠ TWO flags before trusting this. (1) **Best DOWN driver for most readouts is (−0.5,−1) — a LEFT/excitatory-hemisphere site**, which shouldn't inhibit. Near-midline sites were already noted as "dominated by the opposite field" (tf_fit prototype); likely beam spread across the midline or a registration error at ML=±0.5. Verify before claiming left-hemisphere sites can inhibit. (2) UP drivers may be inflated by the broad, near-cortex-wide excitatory field, which is exactly where the single-wavelength hemodynamic confound lives. Consider re-running excluding |ML|≤0.5 stim sites.

### 2026-07-21 — Stage 4b: optimal vs PI — PI over-inhibits ~1.5% past ref, optimal cuts steady RMSE ~88%
**Changed/Found:** New `ctrl_optimal_control.m` (Objective 2). Framing per user: adaptation is NOT folded into the plant — it is a **disturbance the controller exists to fight**, so `y = G*u + d` with `d` MEASURED from the PI trials themselves (`d = y_PI − H*u_PI`). Optimal and PI then face the same plant, same disturbance realization, same actuator ceiling. Built the Markov/Toeplitz convolution matrix `H` from the Stage-4a plant (absorbDelay so the 86 ms delay is in the states) — verified `max|H*u − lsim(u)| = 3.6e-15`. Solved bound-constrained QP `min ||H*u + d − r||² + λ||u||²` s.t. `0 ≤ u ≤ u_max=2.059` (PI's own usage ceiling), both non-causal (clairvoyant) and causal (receding-horizon MPC, Hp=1 s, constant-disturbance prediction). **Results (m4, trial-avg, absolute %dF, ref −5): tracking RMSE steady[1,3s] — PI 1.202, optimal causal 0.146, non-causal 0.000; full[0,3s] — 1.510 / 0.994 / 0.927. Causal optimal improves steady RMSE 87.8% vs PI while using LESS mean laser (1.300 vs 1.563).** PI overshoots to ≈−6.5% and holds there against a −5% setpoint. Disturbance drifts **+1.82%** over the trial (waning inhibition = adaptation); both optima ramp u 1.2→1.5 late to fight it. Causal (0.146) ≈ clairvoyant bound (0.000) ⇒ nearly all achievable gain is reachable CAUSALLY — the PI gap is not about future knowledge. Two bugs fixed pre-result: (a) was comparing baseline-sub `y` against an absolute ref (pre-onset offset biased the error) → now uses `AaAbs`; (b) MPC free-response off-by-one (`Ai=A` gave `Psi(1)=C*A*x` instead of `C*x`), which had been unfairly penalizing the causal controller (steady RMSE 0.618 → 0.146 after fix).
**Why:** Answers "what would optimal control do instead of the PI signal" with a fair, same-budget, same-disturbance counterfactual. The one-sided actuator (0≤u, laser can only inhibit) is what makes this a QP rather than least-squares.
**Next:** ⚠️ **BLOCKER before claiming PI is mistuned:** our `y` is the SVD-reconstructed kernel signal, which matches the real `data.dFk` at only **corr 0.90**. The controller regulated the TRUE `dFk`, so a gain/offset bias in the reconstruction could masquerade as the 1.5% "overshoot". Decisive check: load `data.dFk` (needs `getpixel_dFoF`, skipped in the minimal m4 rebuild) and see whether the real dFk sits at −5 during CL while the reconstruction sits at −6.5. Also: d is estimated along PI's own u trajectory, so it absorbs u-specific model error — a caveat for any u far from u_PI. Then cross-session sweep.

### 2026-07-21 — BUG (user-caught): laser command is a sine CARRIER whose AMPLITUDE is the true input
**Changed/Found:** New `utils/cp_laser_amplitude.m`. The raw `d.inpVals` in early controller sessions is **`A*(1+sin(2*pi*50*t))/2`** — a 50 Hz carrier swinging 0→A, where **A is the true command**; later sessions write the value directly. My first Stage-4a pass used the raw trace as the input, which was wrong twice: (a) the running mean is only **A/2** (0.61 vs true A=1.246), and (b) resampling the 50 Hz carrier to the 35 Hz widefield grid **ALIASED it into a fake ~15 Hz "dither"** that I misread as designed persistent excitation. That fake dither is what drove the nx=5 fit to lightly-damped ~15 Hz resonant poles, and my 6 Hz LP "fix" was just crudely recovering A/2. Correct handling = peak ENVELOPE (running max over one carrier period) at the NATIVE 2 kHz rate. Detection gotcha: a first detector segment spanning several trials read only **3% carrier power** (broadband laser ON/OFF switching swamps the carrier bin) and misdetected 'direct' — fixed by detecting **inside one ON epoch** plus a physical `mean/peak ≈ 0.5` test.
**Why:** User: "the amp was meant to be the true input ... later experiments we converted to using direct value". Getting this wrong corrupts every downstream gain: the plant DC gain was inflated ~2× and the model was fitting an aliasing artifact.
**Next:** Apply `cp_laser_amplitude` anywhere `inpVals` is treated as a command (check `widebrain_arx.m`, `Analysis_openloop_sinusoid.m`, any CL input analysis) — other scripts may carry the same A/2 + aliasing error.

### 2026-07-21 — Stage 4a: LTI plant ID (laser u -> ipsi y) from OL step response
**Changed/Found:** New `ctrl_lti_sysid.m` — SISO LTI actuator model from m4's OL trial-averaged IO, using the corrected amplitude command (`cp_laser_amplitude` → mode=envelope, carrier 50.1 Hz, 46% AC power, period 40 samp). Input `u_OL` is then a **clean constant step of amplitude 1.246**; output `y_OL` = OL Actual (baseline-sub, Stage-2 cache). Model sweep nx=1..5 selected by **AICc** (not raw fit%, which overfits): **nx=2, poles [0.859, 0.364] (both real), DC gain −4.57 %dF per unit u, settling ~0.54 s, fit 88.4% / R²=0.987**, clean monotonic step response, input delay 3 samp (86 ms). Saved `data/ctrl_lti_AL_0033_0226_e2.mat` (A,B,C,D,Ts, u_OL/y_OL, PI's own `u_CL`, budgets **u_max CL-usage=2.059, HW=2.675** — all in amplitude units). Also drew CL single-trial residual fig → `ctrl_cl_pertrial_*.png`, IO fig → `ctrl_lti_iodata_*.png`, raw-carrier fig → `ctrl_lti_rawinput_m4.png`.
**Why:** Stage 4b (Objective 2, best-possible constrained control) needs a plant. Parsimonious nx=2 is the defensible actuator map. Note DC gain halved (−9.03 → −4.57) exactly as expected once u doubled (0.625 → 1.246) — a good consistency check on the amplitude fix.
**Next:** Stage 4b — constrained-optimal control on this LTI: min_u Σ(y−ref)² s.t. 0≤u≤u_max (quadprog, Toeplitz convolution), non-causal (full-horizon) + causal (receding-horizon MPC); compare to the PI controller's CL Actual and its `u_CL`. CAVEAT: the LTI captures drop+gain+settling but NOT the late adaptation (measured y drifts −6→−4.5 by t=3 under a constant step, LTI holds flat). Since Global_OL≈0 that recovery is real actuator/neural adaptation, so a model-based optimal controller won't "see" the disturbance PI fights — decide whether to add a slow adaptation mode before over-interpreting any optimal-vs-PI gap. CONFIRM with user: cost (pure tracking vs +input effort), u_max (CL-usage 2.06 vs HW 2.68).

### 2026-07-21 — Stage 3: deploy stim-blind Global on CL trials (Objective 1) — controller closes 4.5% to ref
**Changed/Found:** New `ctrl_ols_cl_deploy.m` (Stage 3) — reuses the EXACT Stage-2 predictor (`b/mu/sd/muY/Su`, 151 unaff px @ aff_dip_thr=1.33) so OL and CL share one Global, deploys it on the **108 CL trials** (`data.wc`) of m4. Results (baseline-sub, n=108): transient[0,0.5s] Actual −3.46 / Global −0.49 / Local −2.97 = **86% local**; sustained[0,3s] Actual −5.88 / Global −0.50 / Local −5.38 = **91% local / 9% shared** (matches OL's 94/6). ABSOLUTE units: Actual_CL=−5.63 (near ref −5), **Global_CL (no-control counterfactual)=+0.11 (baseline)** → error-from-ref: no-control 5.11% vs controlled 0.63% ⇒ **controller closed 4.49%**. OL-vs-CL: closed-loop holds the inhibition (sustained ~−6, at/below ref) while the open-loop step **drifts back toward baseline (−4.5) late** — CL rejects the recovery OL suffers. `corr(Global_OL,Global_CL)=0.42`. Fig → `paper/images/figure4/ctrl_cl_deploy_AL_0033_0226_e2.png` (3 panels: CL decomp / OL-vs-CL overlay / absolute+ref). Cache → `data/ctrl_ols_cl_deploy_AL_0033_0226_e2.mat`.
**Why:** Delivers Objective 1 ("find ipsi activity such that no closed-loop control was applied"): Global = ipsi-if-laser-off, so on CL trials the whole −5 inhibition is the controller's Local doing (Global sits at baseline). The OL-vs-CL Actual overlay is the controller-efficacy result — CL sustains the setpoint, OL recovers. CAVEAT: `corr(Global_OL,Global_CL)=0.42` is only moderate, but both Globals are near-flat (|Global|≪|Actual|) and OL/CL sample different ongoing-state moments, so the low corr is expected; the real "control-agnostic" evidence is that Global shows NO dip in either condition.
**Next:** Cross-session sweep (Stages 1→3 need Stage-1 caches on the other 12 sessions — only m4 exists now); then Objective 2 (best-possible-control QP benchmark, 0≤u≤u_max, causal + non-causal). Also: workspace was cleared this session — Stage 3 needs `mouse`/`fields`; a minimal m4 rebuild (initialize_data + inline wc/nc, no getpixel/controllerData) is enough since Stage 3 only reads `data.wc` + `d.stimStarts`.

### 2026-07-21 — Stage-2 OL decomposition at aff_dip_thr=1.33: ipsi laser dip is ~90% LOCAL
**Changed/Found:** `ctrl_ols_ol_stimblind.m` run on m4 (AL_0033_0226_e2) with user-chosen `aff_dip_thr=1.33` → **276/427 affected, 151 unaffected** contra px. Global (dense OLS on unaffected px, spont) held-out **R²=0.680** (train 0.888). OL trial-avg decomposition (n=92, baseline-sub): transient[0,0.5s] Actual −3.28 / Global −0.35 / Local −2.93 = **89% local / 11% shared**; sustained[0,3s] Actual −5.33 / Global −0.34 / Local −4.99 = **94% local / 6% shared**; trough @1.51s Actual −6.24. Per-trial Local dip median −3.00 (IQR [−3.58,−2.21]). Fig → `paper/images/figure4/ctrl_ol_stimblind_AL_0033_0226_e2.png`; cache → `data/ctrl_ols_ol_stimblind_AL_0033_0226_e2.mat`.
**Why:** This REVERSES the earlier ~50%-shared number, which was an artifact of the old t-test keeping ~404 "unaffected" px that still included the broad midline dip pixels (they track the ipsi dip → inflate "shared"). The dip-score at −1.33 drops every px that itself dips → 151 genuinely-flat px carry no stim signal → Global stays flat → residual ≈ the whole dip. Honest pure-stim-blind answer: **the ipsi laser-site inhibition is almost entirely LOCAL, not shared with stim-blind contra**. Spont R² fell 0.87→0.68 because the homotopic midline predictors (best for spont) are exactly the dropped ones — expected tradeoff of pure-pixel over KKT.
**Next:** Sweep Stages 1→2 on the other sessions to check the ~90%-local result holds cross-session; then Stage 3 (deploy Global as the "no-controller" counterfactual on CL trials + best-possible-control benchmark).

### 2026-07-20 — Affected-pixel selection: reject adaptive permutation null, keep manual dip threshold
**Changed/Found:** `ctrl_affected_gui.m` — stripped the adaptive/permutation-null selection (Auto-select button, `aff_auto`, pool computation, `nPerm`/`alpha_adx` config, `mode`/`pval` GD fields); GUI is now MANUAL-only (slider on the trial-avg dip score). Reason it was rejected: the permutation null draws pseudo-onsets from laser-off frames, so its null carries the **same trough-min bias** as the real score (null mean −1.43 vs real −1.58, only 0.15 apart) → it "explains away" the real broad contra dip as chance and flagged just **9/427** scattered px, contradicting the visible midline/left-hemisphere dip cluster in the deflection map. Manual slider still ranks pixels correctly by dip depth. Live GUI on m4 (AL_0033_0226_e2): score range [−3.41, −0.01]; default score<−1.0 → 321 affected / 106 unaffected (25% kept). Deflection map shows the WHOLE contra grid sits in a broad blue dip (callosal/network-shared inhibition) — that broad dip is why most pixels flag; cleanest unaffected pocket = ventro-lateral bottom-left cluster.
**Why:** User decision ("keep the manual threshold, adaptive is wrong") — the adaptive test is statistically self-defeating here because the bias it needs to correct also lives in its null. Manual visual tuning against the deflection map is the chosen selection method.
**Next:** User picks the slider threshold in the live GUI; then set `aff_dip_thr` in `ctrl_ols_ol_stimblind.m` to that value and run the Global predictor on the resulting unaffected set.

### 2026-07-20 — STATEDEP: add pre+post prediction-quality control + state↔control collinearity report
**Changed/Found:** `ols_tf_pipeline.m` §17c — strengthened the state-dependence controls per the paper-review discussion. (1) New per-trial `dev_post` = residual energy over the SETTLED tail `postCtrlCols` = last 0.2 s of the trace = **[+0.8, +1.0] s** (bleed_postSec=1.0, past the ~770 ms rebound-settle) — a second stim-free prediction-quality probe matched in length to the pre-onset `dev_pre` [−0.2,0] s. (2) The per-DV table now prints a `p|pre+post` column = `partialcorr(DV, state, [PRE POST])` (both stim-free windows controlled JOINTLY as a 2-covariate partial) alongside the existing `p|pre`, `raw`, and `trust` columns — guards against within-trial non-stationarity of the fit across the onset. (3) New `[STATEDEP CONTROL]` block prints `state↔dev_pre` and `state↔dev_post` Spearman collinearity per state, so a null partial can be read as "no effect" vs "no power left after partialling" (variance/δ are expected to be collinear with prediction quality). `STATEDEP` struct gained `dev_post`, `ctrl_collin_pre/post`, `rho_partial_prepost/p_partial_prepost`. Header + NOTE updated: a real effect must survive raw + p|pre + p|pre+post + trust-half. Code Analyzer: no new issues.
**Why:** User's paper-level design point — pre-trial prediction quality is the control for how much of the residual-state relationship is the STIM effect vs baseline-fit noise. Discussion identified two gaps in the single pre-window control: (a) temporal mismatch (pre-onset window vs peri-stim residual → non-stationarity) and (b) invisible collinearity burden (a state collinear with prediction quality kills partial power silently). Both now addressed and reported.
**Next:** Run §17c and read the grid {3 DVs}×{raw, p|pre, p|pre+post, trust} + the collinearity table; the defensible paper claim is the DV (likely L1DEV/predictability) that survives all four on an admissible state (Motion, Rel-δ) AND is not merely collinearity-starved. Not run here (MATLAB-MCP crash constraint).

**Changed/Found:** New script — the OL stim-blind decomposition, **pure unaffected-pixel selection** (user's chosen approach; NO KKT weight constraint). Dense OLS on the 404 unaffected px → Global held-out spont R²=0.855. Per-trial Local dip median −1.49 (IQR −1.99..−1.21). So on m4 the laser's ipsi effect is ~half genuinely local, ~half a distributed state shift predictable from unaffected contra. ### 2026-07-19 — Stage 2 prototype (OL stim-blind): unaffected-pixel alone gives 42%; KKT constraint gives 100% capture
**Changed/Found:** Prototyped the OL contra→ipsi stim-blind decomposition on m4 (in-memory, not yet scripted). Reverted Stage 1 to **dense OLS** (`fit_mode='ols'`, user wants R²). Findings: (1) **Contra is barely stim-affected** — only 5/427 grid px reach |t|>3 (across-trial transient-deflection t-test), none |t|>4. The focal ipsi inhibition does not strongly cross the midline. Dropping the 5 leaves held-out spont R²=0.876 (was 0.874). (2) **BUT unaffected-pixel prediction still LEAKS 58% of the dip** — OL trial-avg: Actual dip −3.28 (early window; full trough ~−6), Global −1.91, **Local (residual) captures only 42%**. The contra pixels individually pass the unaffected test yet their WEIGHTED prediction dips, because the inhibition is a brain-wide event contra jointly predicts via shared dynamics. This exactly replicates the impulse-side lesson (`ols_tf_pipeline` §17: "network-correlated clean pixels still predict the dip; dropping energy-affected px is insufficient"). (3) **KKT stim-blinding fixes it** — project the spont OLS weights off the contra evoked subspace (K=6 sub-windows of [0,3]s, z-space `b_blind = b0 − G⁻¹D(D'G⁻¹D)⁻¹D'b0`): Global goes FLAT (0% leak), **Local captures 100%** of the dip and tracks Actual almost exactly. Cost: held-out spont R² 0.876→0.718 (the removed directions ARE the stim-predictive info). Figure `ctrl_ol_stimblind_compare_m4.png`.
**Why:** User asked to repeat the impulse stim-blind analysis on OL controller trials: predict ipsi from stim-UNAFFECTED contra px, residual should capture most of the stim effect. The pure-pixel version doesn't meet "most" (42%); the KKT constraint (impulse §17b NATIVE, ported) does (100%).
**Next:** DECISION PENDING (user): build Stage 2 script with the **KKT stim-blind predictor** (recommended — only path meeting the "capture most" requirement) vs pure pixel-dropping (42%). Caveats to carry: (a) the trial-avg 100% is partly BY CONSTRUCTION (KKT zeroes the evoked direction) — the real value is per-trial denoising (Global removes ongoing state from Local) + the counterfactual reading for CL (Stage 3); (b) mild circularity — D built from the OL evoked, tested on the same OL trials → validate with held-out trials / CV; (c) tune K (fewer = less R² cost). Then Stage 3 = deploy on CL for the controller Global/Local + best-possible-control benchmark.

### 2026-07-19 — Stage 1 RESOLVED + validated: SVD-rolling Actual at the data-derived laser spot; held-out R²=0.87
**Changed/Found:** Reworked `ctrl_ols_spont.m` and ran it clean on m4. Three fixes resolved the mode-0 disconnect: (1) **Actual = SVD raw-kernel fluorescence + rolling baseline** — rebuild `F_raw = mI + kernel·U·V` and pass it through the SAME `horizon`-sample (40 s) rolling baseline getpixel mode-0 uses (helper `local_svd_rolling_dfk`, warm-up→NaN). This lifts `corr(Actual, data.dFk)` from 0.06 (mean-image baseline) to **0.90** — the entire disconnect was the baseline METHOD, not the pixel. (2) **Data-derived laser site, not `params.pixel`** — the OL trial-avg dip map (n=92) shows one focal inhibition blob on the RIGHT/ipsi hemisphere, but `params.pixel` [row320 col380] sits on the dip RIM ~45 px from the true center; `cp_find_stim_site` on OL onsets gives the laser spot **[row 315 col 427]** (depth −1.87%), cached `cp_stim_site_ctrl_*`. Retarget keeps `corr(Actual,data.dFk)=0.90` (spot↔rim corr 0.96) while giving a cleaner/larger Local. (3) **Hemisphere confirmed functionally** (user's request): OL dip is focal on the right → ipsi=right, contra(predictor)=left; matches the ROI split. Removed the invalid `data.dFk`-based convention auto-detect. Held-out contra→ipsi **R²=0.874, ρ=0.936** (train 0.971, gap 0.10; 99,399 laser-off frames after dropping 1,399 warm-up). Predictor cached `ctrl_ols_spont_<sess>.mat`. Code-analyzer clean. Figures: `ctrl_orient_check_m4.png`, `ctrl_dipmap_m4.png`, `ctrl_ols_spont_<sess>.png`.
**Why:** User directed the target onto the SVD-rolling reconstruction ("dfk can be computed from svd by ipsi kernel + rolling mean, ignore warm-up") and to confirm the hemisphere via OL dips at the laser spot. This makes Actual both SVD-predictable AND tied to the paper's `data.dFk`, and puts it at the site of the true local effect.
**Next:** Stage 2 = OL TF affected-pixel detection + per-pixel plant `h_j(t)` for time-varying CL de-bleeding. Then Stage 3 = deploy on CL for Global/Local + the best-possible-control benchmark. Also: transpose note — SVD `U` is stored in the raw-transposed orientation, so the native index and mode-0's transposed-raw index coincide (proven by the 0.90 corr); `cp_find_stim_site` works in the native frame with no transpose. Run remaining 11 sessions once Stage 2/3 shape is fixed.

### 2026-07-19 — ⚠ Sharpened: mode-0 vs mode-1 dF/F disconnect was the BASELINE, not a broken pipeline
**Changed/Found:** Supersedes the concern in the prior entry that mode-0 `data.dFk` might be frame-misaligned/wrong-channel. With the baseline method matched (rolling vs mean-image), SVD-rolling reconstruction correlates **0.90** with mode-0 `data.dFk` at the same site (and 0.91 at the rim). So mode-0 is NOT broken — the 0.06/0.10 correlations seen earlier were entirely the mean-image (DC) vs 40 s-rolling (high-pass) baseline difference. The `data.dFk` pipeline is sound.
**Why:** The earlier entry flagged a potential paper-wide problem in the controller signal. This retracts that alarm: mode-0 and the SVD agree once compared like-for-like.
**Next:** No action on mode-0. Keep using the SVD-rolling reconstruction for the decomposition (needed for contra-predictability); it faithfully matches the paper signal.

### 2026-07-18 — KEY FINDING: `data.dFk` (mode-0 raw-frame rolling-baseline) is NOT reconstructible from the widefield SVD
**Changed/Found:** Ran `ctrl_ols_spont.m` on m4 (AL_0033 2025-02-26 e2). The contra→ipsi OLS fit was excellent (held-out R²=0.978) but the built-in guard fired: `corr(rebuilt kernel trace, data.dFk)` ≈ 0 for BOTH pixel conventions. Diagnosed: **`load_sessions.m` L126 calls `getpixel_dFoF(d, mode=0, ...)`** → `data.dFk` is computed from **raw binary frame images** (`C:\Users\aditya\Documents\projects\data\<mn>\<td>\<en>`, every 2nd frame) with a **40 s rolling-window baseline** (`w = params.horizon-1`), NOT the SVD. Evidence: (1) `dFk(1:10)=[58017 40854 31682 …]` = rolling-baseline warm-up garbage over the first ~1399 samples (the `ones(1,w)` pad), inflating std to 267 while the body is normal (median 0.24, 99% within ±20); (2) reconstructing the SAME pixel/kernel from the raw blue SVD gives **corr(SVD, data.dFk)=0.06**, only **0.10** after a ±20-frame lag scan; (3) raw-vs-hemo-corrected SVD at that pixel corr=0.82 (correction is NOT the culprit); (4) U bases identical (max|ΔU|=0 over first modes). ⇒ mode-0 and the SVD are genuinely different pipelines; the SVD predictor structurally cannot target `data.dFk`.
**Why:** The whole contra→ipsi Actual/Global/Local decomposition is SVD-based (both here and impulse-side `ols_tf_pipeline.m`). If Actual = mode-0 `data.dFk`, contra explains only ~10% of it → Global useless, Local≈Actual, no decomposition. The port only works if Actual is defined on the SVD pipeline.
**Next:** (a) **DECISION PENDING (user):** define Actual = SVD ipsi-kernel trace (mode-1), decoupling from the paper's mode-0 `data.dFk` — recommended and the only workable path. Verified sensible: the SVD kernel trace at the getpixel-correct box shows a clean stim dip (OL trough −5.4% @1.5 s, CL −6.2% @2.3 s) and contra predicts it at held-out **R²=0.923**. (b) The convention auto-detect in `ctrl_ols_spont.m` is invalid (compared vs a signal uncorrelated with either SVD box) — replace with the fixed getpixel rule box B = `U(row=pixel(2), col=pixel(1))`; the script's R²=0.978 was the WRONG box A (a valid SVD location, not the controller pixel). (c) ⚠ **SEPARATE CONCERN for the paper:** mode-0 vs mode-1 dF/F at the SAME pixel correlating only 0.10 means one may be subtly wrong (mode-0's every-2nd-frame raw read could be frame-phase-misaligned or the wrong channel). The paper's controller RMSE is all built on mode-0 `data.dFk`. Out of scope now — do not lose this.

### 2026-07-18 — STATEDEP: three candidate DVs (DIPmean / GAIN / L1DEV) reported side by side
**Changed/Found:** `ols_tf_pipeline.m` §17c — the state test now runs on THREE DVs instead of one, all on the same dip window `dc` and all z-scored within amp: **(1) DIPmean** = signed mean residual minus the amp mean (the original DV, directional); **(2) GAIN** = template gain `<r,mu>/<mu,mu>` against the amp-mean residual template — shape-aware (uses the whole window profile, not just its mean) so better SNR, still signed; **(3) L1DEV** = `mean|r - mu|`, the UNSIGNED deviation from the template = per-trial *unpredictability*, which cannot cancel a deep-then-shallow mixture the way a signed mean can. The partial-Spearman table (partial | raw | trust-only, × 4 states) is printed once per DV; `rhoP/pvP/rhoR/rhoT/pvT` became `[4 states × 3 DVs]` and `STATEDEP` gained `GAINz`, `L1DEVz`, `dvLabel`. Fig B still plots DIPmean (column 1, now explicitly indexed). Added a `[DV-PRIMACY]` printout telling the reader to compare the two ADMISSIBLE rows (Motion, Rel-δ) across the three columns. No statistical machinery changed (no MC correction / stratified permutation / bootstrap — user declined).
**Why:** User chose "add template-gain, then decide" on DV primacy. The 2026-07-01 A2 result was that at the true laser focus the SIGNED effect collapses while the unsigned L1 deviation survives ⇒ the defensible claim may be PREDICTABILITY rather than response size — but the script only computed the signed mean-dip, so that comparison could not actually be made. Now it can, from one run.
**Next:** Run and compare the Motion / Rel-δ rows across DIPmean vs GAIN vs L1DEV. If the signed DVs collapse and L1DEV survives, promote L1DEV (and §17c2's dispersion result) to the paper's primary claim and demote the signed mean to a supporting negative. Not run here (MATLAB-MCP crash constraint).

It now builds a per-session distance-weighted penalty (`selD = hypot(grC-py_prim, grR-px_prim)` → normalized → `gammaS = 1./(penNear*(1-d) + penFar*d)`) and fits `select_wlasso(Gz, cz, Sset, gammaS, cfg.select_l1frac, lamR)` per amp, exactly mirroring §17d. Distance is taken in ARRAY coords — the display-orientation transform is an isometry, so the metric is identical to the main script's display-coord version (avoids needing `Tor`, which is built later in the function). Held-out spont R² is now computed directly (`1 - sum((yte-(muYc+Zte*bfull)).^2)/sstot`) since `native_project` no longer returns it; added `nActA` (#active predictors per amp) to the returned struct. `cfg` gained `select_l1frac/select_penNear/select_penFar`. **`native_project` deleted** (both callers gone). ### 2026-07-18 — Retire §17b STIMBLIND-NATIVE (+§17bV); SELECT is the sole stim-blind model
**Changed/Found:** `ols_tf_pipeline.m` — removed 211 lines: the NATIVE `native_nblind` sweep, the NATIVE per-amp KKT fit, its three figures (figNB1/2/3), the `STIMBLIND_NATIVE` struct, and the whole §17bV [NATIVE-VALIDATION] section. The **shared, model-agnostic prep** that SELECT/STATEDEP/§18 depend on was NOT removed — it stayed in place and was re-headed **§17a [SHARED-OPS]** (per-amp z-evoked `evZc/aAc/dcc/rcc/scc/Sc/dGc/b0c`, biphasic basis `gDip/gReb`, honest pre/post pred-R² designs `Zpre/ypre/Zpost/ypost/r2f`, layout `nrN/ncN`). §17d SELECT promoted to `*** PRIMARY ***` and its two NATIVE cross-references stripped: the "SELECT vs NATIVE dip capture" printout, and Fig 1 panel 3 which compared capture curves — that panel now shows **per-amp capture vs leak** for SELECT alone. §17c/§17c2 STATEDEP repointed `bUseN` → `bUseS` (SELECT per-amp sparse weights) and its Fig A check-overlay `STIMBLIND_NATIVE.LocalDip` → `STIMBLIND_SELECT.LocalDip`. Four stale header comments (§1 config ×2, §16 FARPIX, §17) updated. Code Analyzer: no new issues; no live NATIVE symbol remains (verified by grep).
**Why:** User: "retire stim blind native, route the state dep to use stimblind select — state dep is what we use for paper." NATIVE cancelled the dip by an exact KKT constraint, which guarantees ~100% dip capture and therefore makes the capture number uninformative; SELECT has to *earn* its residual by choosing few, far-from-ipsi predictors, so its leak is an honest readout. The paper's residual must come from the honest model.
**Next:** Re-run §17–§17c2 and confirm the state-dep numbers on SELECT residuals — they WILL differ from the NATIVE-based ones, since SELECT leaks and so the per-trial Local dip is smaller. Sanity-check the §17c Fig A overlay lines up with §17d's per-amp LocalDip. Not run here (MATLAB-MCP crash constraint).

### 2026-07-18 — BUG in `contra_prediction_controller.m`: "spontaneous" frames include 3 s of laser-ON data
**Changed/Found:** Found while porting, not yet fixed in the scaffold. `ols_tf_pipeline.m` §6 starts each spontaneous gap at `onset + settle_s` (2 s), which is correct for IMPULSE data because the stim is instantaneous. On CONTROLLER data the laser is driven for the **entire trial** — `controllerData.m` slices `d.inpVals` over `i2 : i2 + dur*2000`, i.e. `dur` (=3) seconds at 2 kHz. `contra_prediction_controller.m` `[CTRL-CP-CFG]` sets `settle_s = 1.0` and passes it to `cp_hemi_predictor` as the post-onset settle, so its "spontaneous" training set begins 1 s after onset and swallows ~2 s of laser-ON frames per trial. That trains the predictor **on exactly the stim-driven coupling it must be blind to** — the failure mode already documented on the impulse side (Global absorbs the stim effect, Local comes out near-empty). New script uses `onset + trial_dur + settle_s` and additionally harvests the pre-first-trial baseline.
**Why:** The whole Actual/Global/Local decomposition is only meaningful if Global is fit on genuinely stim-free data; this silently violated that and would have produced a confidently wrong, near-empty Local.
**Next:** Fix `contra_prediction_controller.m` (`settle_s` → `trial_dur + settle_s`) OR retire it if the direct-pixel lineage fully supersedes the RRR one — decide once Stage 1 has run. Its `[CTRL-CP-TRIAL]`/`[CTRL-CP-STATE]` results, if any were ever generated, are invalid.

### 2026-07-18 — Found: `initialize_data`'s cached `d.svd` is preprocessed differently from `loadUVt`
**Changed/Found:** `utils/initialize_data.m` (L91–107) populates `d.svd` by reading `blue/svdTemporalComponents.npy` directly at `nSV = 2000` — **raw, no hemodynamic correction, no detrend**. `loadUVt` (used by the whole impulse pipeline) instead prefers `corr/svdTemporalComponents_corr.npy` and, when that is absent, applies `detrendAndFilt` to the uncorrected components. So a controller-side predictor built on `d.svd` would sit on materially different preprocessing from the impulse-side method it is meant to replicate. `ctrl_ols_spont.m` therefore calls `loadUVt` rather than using the cached `d.svd`, and records which branch was taken in the saved struct (`used_corr`) plus a warning when uncorrected.
**Why:** Cross-pipeline comparability. Global/Local numbers from controller vs impulse sessions are only comparable if both hemispheres' regressors went through the same preprocessing; otherwise any difference is confounded by detrending/hemo correction rather than biology.
**Next:** Note that `contra_prediction_controller.m` uses `d.svd` (L77) and so inherits this mismatch — fold into the fix/retire decision above. Also decide whether the 5 uncorrected sessions (m6 m7 m8 m11 m12) get pooled with the 7 corrected ones or analysed separately.
