### 2026-07-18 — Design: port the stim-blind predictor to controller trials via per-pixel TF de-bleeding (not fixed KKT blinding)
**Changed/Found:** Design decision, no code yet. Read `ols_tf_pipeline.m` §17/§17b to establish how blinding actually works: NATIVE trains an unconstrained spontaneous contra→ipsi OLS, then KKT-projects the weights off a **per-amp, trial-averaged evoked subspace D** (`native_nblind` sub-windows spanning onset→settle), so the prediction is exactly zero along stim-driven directions. **That construction cannot transfer to closed loop as-is:** D is built from a trial-average at a fixed amplitude, and the CL laser command is time-varying and different every trial, so there is no per-amp evoked direction to project off. **Chosen alternative — use §10T2 as a plant model, not just a detector.** §10T2 already fits a TF to *every* contra pixel at a fixed canonical order; that is a per-pixel laser→pixel impulse response `h_j(t)`. In CL the commanded laser `u(t)` is known, so each contra regressor can be de-bled in a time-varying, amplitude-correct way as `z_j_clean(t) = z_j(t) − (h_j * u)(t)` before the spontaneous predictor is applied. Global (the counterfactual no-control ipsi) then comes from de-bled contra. Fixed-D KKT blinding, built from the OL step trials, is retained as the cheap fallback/cross-check since it reuses `native_project` verbatim.
**Why:** User is redirecting the controller-side residual work off the old `contra_prediction.m` RRR model onto `ols_tf_pipeline.m`, with the train/detect/apply split = spontaneous / open-loop / closed-loop. Detection transfers cleanly (OL fixed-amp steps ≈ impulse); the *blinding* is the part that does not, and it is the step that determines whether Local is real or an artifact.
**Next:** Validate on OL step trials FIRST (known-good regime — if Local doesn't recover the known step response there, the port is wrong) before touching CL. Also settle the DC/baseline question: for the Actual/Global/Local decomposition the trial DC must be KEPT (the offset between Actual at ref=−5 and Global at its spontaneous level IS the controller's effect), which is the opposite of the queued `[CTRL-CP-TRIAL]` baseline-align item — that one applies only to the predictability DV (r2/resE). Do not blanket-apply either rule.

### 2026-07-17 — Add §8c [KERNELPAPER]: paper-level smooth ipsi→contra sparse-kernel panels
**Changed/Found:** `ols_tf_pipeline.m` — new section **§8c [KERNELPAPER]** (after §8b) plus a base-MATLAB helper `kfield`. Publication version of §8b's kernel map: picks **5 ipsi targets evenly spaced along the ipsi hemisphere's principal axis** (SVD of the ipsi-mask coords → 1-D projection → `linspace(prctile 8, prctile 92, 5)` snapped to the nearest ipsi pixel, deduped), and for each fits the **spont-trained sparse contra kernel** via the existing `ols_refit` Lasso (fit on non-stim frames — unchanged model, just static + multi-target). Rendering replaces §8b's discrete sized dots with a **smooth continuous weight field**: `kfield` scatter-interpolates the sparse grid weights onto the full display grid (`scatteredInterpolant`, `'natural'`/`'none'` so it stays inside the hull) then applies a **normalized (edge-safe) Gaussian convolution restricted to the contra mask** — `conv2(V,K)./conv2(mask,K)` — so the field neither decays at the hemisphere border nor bleeds across the midline. Overlaid on the anatomical background (diverging blue-white-red, `KP_alpha=0.85`), with a **COMMON symmetric colour scale across all 5 panels** + one shared colorbar so weights stay quantitatively comparable; green pentagram = that panel's ipsi target, black + = laser site. Knobs `KP_sigma=6`, `KP_alpha`, `KP_export` (default false → vector PDF to `dataDir` when true). `tiledlayout` 1×5, 6–7 pt fonts, cm-sized figure. No Image Processing Toolbox dependency (`scatteredInterpolant`+`conv2` only). Code Analyzer: only the benign constant-`false` `KP_export` unreachable note.
**Why:** User asked for the previously-requested "ipsi region and their corresponding contra sparse kernel" as a **paper-level smooth figure**, and chose (via routing Q): plain spont Lasso kernel, smoothed weight field, 5 evenly-spaced targets fit on spont data. §8b existed but is a diagnostic (dot scatter, ad-hoc 3×3 lattice targets, per-panel colour scale) — not publication-ready.
**Next:** User runs §8c and eyeballs it; tune `KP_sigma` (smoothing) / `KP_alpha`, and whether the common colour scale washes out weaker targets (per-panel normalisation is a one-line switch). Flip `KP_export=true` when happy. ### 2026-07-17 — Dip-cap: gate on the EARLY-window fitted dip (ignore late natural dips)
**Changed/Found:** `ols_tf_pipeline.m` §10T — new knob `tf_dipCapSec = 0.4` caps the window in which the gated dip is searched. The fit still spans the full `tf_winSec` (needs the tail to shape the rebound), but a fit trough LATER in the window is natural drift / a second dip, not the stim response, so it must not gatekeep. Implementation (LIVE knob, no refit on change): the fit loop now caches the per-pixel **fitted impulse responses** `YItf` [nG×nWin×nA] (computed via `proto_sim` from the fitted `sys`); the gated dip `DIPgate = min(YItf(:,1:capN,:))` with `capN = round(tf_dipCapSec*Fs)+1` is derived FRESH each run (like `preThr`), so tweaking the cap re-thresholds off the cache. Gate is now `-DIPgate > preThr/tf_sens` (was `-DIPtf`, full-window). `DIPtf`/`TRtf`/`REBtf` kept full-window for display. Slider `tfmap_sens` gates on `DB.DIPgate`. Inspector `tfmap_detail`: computes the capped dip from its live fit, draws a **grey dashed cap line** + **red marker at the gated dip**, `preOK = dipCap < sflr`, title `gate dip(≤Nms)=X vs floor Y | full trough Z`. `DBT` carries `DIPgate/capN/dipCapSec`; `AFFECT_TF` carries `dipGate/dipCapSec`. Cache algo `biphasic_stmcb_v2 → v3` (adds `YItf`) → **one refit on next run** to populate, free thereafter. Config printout shows the dip-cap. Code Analyzer: no new issues.
**Why:** User: "the TF-fit trace may have a dip after the window where [the] first dip must be ... which does not correspond to stim-driven dip; cap the threshold-comparison window to only where [the] first dip should occur." The stim inhibition trough is early (~114 ms); `min` over the full 600 ms window could lock onto a later natural trough and mis-gate a pixel as affected.
**Next:** User re-run — FIRST run refits (builds `YItf`, ~one-time), later runs load cache. Inspector shows the cap line + gated-dip marker so it's visually unambiguous. Tune `tf_dipCapSec` (live) if the stim dip in some regions lands later than 400 ms. Not run here (MATLAB-MCP crash constraint).

### 2026-07-17 — Gate the fit's own dip (ch.dip) directly; drop the raw-onset-sample offset
**Changed/Found:** `ols_tf_pipeline.m` §10T — corrected the previous turn's gate. It tested `DIPfittf = ch.dip + r1`, adding the RAW smoothed onset sample `r1` back to the fitted dip to re-reference it to the pre-stim baseline. That re-injected raw-trace noise into a gate that should read only the fit, and made the inspector inconsistent (fit drawn dipping to `ch.dip`, but verdict computed on `ch.dip+r1`). Since the fit is zeroed at onset, `ch.dip` (=`DIPtf`) IS the denoised post-onset drop, and `preThr` is already the fluctuation about the MEAN pre-stim activity (`base` is per-trial DC-removed) — so they are directly comparable. Gate is now simply `g_pre = (-DIPtf > preThr/tf_sens)`. Removed the `DIPfittf` block, the `DIPfit` fields from `DBT` + `AFFECT_TF`. Slider `tfmap_sens`: `-DB.DIP` vs floor. Inspector `tfmap_detail`: `preOK = ch.dip < sflr` (now the fit trough as DRAWN is exactly what's gated — visually consistent), title reads `gate: fit dip X vs floor Y (raw trough Z)`; `DIPraw` kept for reference display only. Updated stale comments (baseline block + `DIPrawtf` line). Cache key UNCHANGED (`ch.dip`/`DIPtf` already cached) → re-run re-thresholds, no refit. Code Analyzer: no new issues.
**Why:** User clarified the previous explanation was wrong: the fit is good, so gatekeep on the TF-fit's dip as shown in the inspector; the floor is already about the mean pre-stim activity (DC-removed base), so no raw-onset term is needed. The `+r1` I added pulled the raw trace back into the gate — exactly what the fit is meant to avoid.
**Next:** User re-run (loads cached fits, re-thresholds) — inspector's drawn fit trough vs the floor line now equals the gate verdict. Tune with `tf_sens`/`tf_kSD`/`tf_kRange`. Not run here (MATLAB-MCP crash constraint).

### 2026-07-17 — Baseline gate now tests the FITTED trough, not the raw trial-mean min
**Changed/Found:** `ols_tf_pipeline.m` §10T — the baseline significance gate (`g_pre`) was comparing `DIPrawtf = min(mu(:,wa))` (the RAW trial-mean trough) against `preThr`. That's the wrong trace: `min(mu)` is a noise-biased extremum — it grabs the single most-negative sample in the window, exactly the kind of excursion the floor is designed to reject — so a noisy pixel could pass on one bad sample. Now the gate tests the DENOISED **fitted** trough. New `DIPfittf(g,a) = DIPtf + r1`, where `DIPtf=ch.dip` is the fitted impulse-response trough (onset-referenced, r1 subtracted before fitting) and `r1` = the smoothed onset-sample of the trial mean (recomputed fresh from `muMap`, so `DIPfittf` needs NO refit and the cache stays valid). This puts the fitted trough on the same pre-stim-baseline footing as `preThr`. Gate is now `g_pre = (-DIPfittf > preThr/tf_sens)`. Propagated to: slider `tfmap_sens` (`DIPraw`→`DIPfit`), DBT (`DIPfit` field + `smoothN`), inspector `tfmap_detail` (now smooths `r` with `tf_smoothN` to match the batch fit, verdict `preOK = dipFit<sflr`, title reports `fit trough` + raw for reference), and `AFFECT_TF` (`dipFit` field). `DIPrawtf` kept for display/reference only. Code Analyzer: no new issues.
**Why:** User: "check if gate 3 is being applied to the correct trace, it should be applied to the fitted response." Correct — the whole point of the biphasic TF fit is a denoised depth estimate; gating on the raw min defeats it and reintroduces the noise-extremum false positives the floor exists to kill. The inspector previously also didn't smooth `r`, so its live fit could disagree with the map; now it matches.
**Next:** User re-run (loads cached fits, re-derives `DIPfittf` + re-thresholds, no refit) — affected counts should drop slightly and be more robust (noisy single-sample dips no longer pass). Click a pixel: the title's `fit trough` vs `floor` is now the actual gate test. Not run here (MATLAB-MCP crash constraint).

### 2026-07-17 — Baseline floor uses max ABS deviation (either direction), not just downward
**Changed/Found:** `ols_tf_pipeline.m` §10T — the range term of the baseline floor was `preNeg = max(-min(base),0)` (worst DOWNWARD excursion only). Renamed `preNeg`→`preRange` and changed it to `max(max(base,[],2), -min(base,[],2))` = the MAX ABSOLUTE deviation about the mean, i.e. the pixel's worst swing in EITHER direction. Rationale: `base` is per-trial DC-removed then trial-averaged (mean ≈ 0), so a pixel that spontaneously swings +2 up is exactly that noisy, and a −2 stim "dip" is therefore NOT significant for it — the floor must reflect the full swing envelope, not just the downward half. `preThr = max(tf_kSD·preStd, tf_kRange·preRange)` unchanged in form. Updated the `AFFECT_TF` field (`preNeg`→`preRange`), the `tf_kRange` knob comment, and the header prose. No `DB.preNeg` reader existed (inspector uses `preThr` only), so no other call sites. Cache key unchanged (floor computed fresh, not cached) → re-run re-thresholds, no refit. Code Analyzer: no new issues.
**Why:** User: "for inhibition floor we should take absolute deviation … floor should be represented by max of min or max activity about the mean." A one-sided (downward) floor under-counts the true fluctuation of pixels that swing up spontaneously, letting their noise-scale downward dips pass; the symmetric abs-deviation floor is the correct significance envelope.
**Next:** User re-run — the `baseline` gate should now REJECT more marginal dips at pixels with large upward baseline swings (stricter, more honest). If it becomes too strict, lower `tf_kRange` (or raise `tf_sens`). Not run here (MATLAB-MCP crash constraint).

### 2026-07-17 — Baseline gate now pools pre-stim + post-response activity
**Changed/Found:** `ols_tf_pipeline.m` §10T — the significance floor (`preStd`/`preNeg` → `preThr`) now measures the pixel's own fluctuation over TWO stim-free windows, not just pre-stim: (1) the PRE window (`tf_preSec=4` s before onset, clamped per amp to `min(onF)-1` so no trial drops) and (2) a POST window (new: `tf_usePost=true`, starts `tf_postStartSec=0.8` s after onset — past the response — for up to `tf_postSec=4` s). The post window is auto-clamped to the nearest NEXT stim onset (union of all amps + 0V, `allOn`) minus a 3-frame guard so it never overlaps another stim. Each window is per-trial DC-removed, trial-AVERAGED (same √nT footing as `DIPrawtf`), then the two averaged traces are POOLED into `base` before `std`/worst-min. Labels updated: gate print `pre-stim`→`baseline`, inspector floor/verdict `pre-stim`→`baseline`. `AFFECT_TF` now records `preSec/usePost/postStartSec/postSec`. Cache key unchanged (`biphasic_stmcb_v2`; floor computed fresh, not cached) → re-run re-thresholds off the cache, no refit. Code Analyzer: no new issues.
**Why:** User: "we should also include the post stim (after 800ms) activity here as baseline." The settled post-response tail is another sample of the pixel's genuine fluctuation; pooling it with the pre-stim window gives a more robust, less pre-window-length-limited floor (some amps have short pre gaps), so the inhibition-significance bar reflects the pixel's true noise on both sides of the response.
**Next:** User re-run (loads cached fits, re-thresholds) — the `baseline` gate count should be stable/robust vs the pre-only version; if the post window is too short at some amps (dense trial spacing) it auto-drops to pre-only for that amp. Tune via `tf_postStartSec`/`tf_postSec`. Not run here (MATLAB-MCP crash constraint).

### 2026-07-17 — REPAIR#2: pre-stim floor was on the wrong footing (raw vs averaged) + live slider
**Changed/Found:** `ols_tf_pipeline.m` §10T — pre-stim gate STILL rejected everything. Root cause: a footing mismatch — `DIPrawtf` is the trough of the **trial-AVERAGED** evoked (noise down by √nT), but `preStd`/`preNeg` were computed from **raw single-trial** samples (full noise), so the gate demanded the averaged response beat ~√nT× the single-trial fluctuation (an impossibly high bar). Fix: measure the pre-stim floor on the **trial-AVERAGED** pre-stim trace — per-trial DC-remove, then `mean(...,3)` across trials, then SD + worst-min on that averaged trace — so trough and floor are on the same footing. Also clamp the pre-stim window per amp to whatever every trial actually has (`pn=min(preN4, min(onF)-1)`) instead of dropping trials, and mark an amp `inf` (excluded) only if it truly has <4 pre-stim frames. Slider: was only firing on release (and with an empty map looked dead) — added an `addlistener(hSens,'ContinuousValueChange',…)` so it re-thresholds LIVE while dragging. Cache key unchanged (only thresholding/floor changed, not fits) → re-run re-thresholds off the cache, no refit. Code Analyzer: no errors.
**Why:** User: "is the slider doing anything, nothing got detected, gate 3 still seems harsh." The harshness was the raw-vs-averaged footing (the √nT factor), not the multiplier; the slider looked dead only because the map was empty at every sens.
**Next:** User re-run — the per-gate print should now show a non-zero `pre-stim` count and a non-empty AFFECTED map; the slider should visibly grow/shrink the black pixels as you drag. If now too permissive, raise `tf_kSD`/`tf_kRange` or lower `tf_sens`. Not run here (MATLAB-MCP crash constraint).

### 2026-07-17 — REPAIR: selection criterion returned empty — dropped self-defeating VAF-vs-null gate
**Changed/Found:** `ols_tf_pipeline.m` §10T — after the stmcb+sweep fit-quality upgrade, `affected_tf` went EMPTY. Diagnosis: two of the three AND-gates broke. (a) The `VAFtf > vafGate` (0V-null) gate self-defeats now that the fit is flexible — a stmcb+AIC-sweep fit to 0V NOISE scores nearly as high a VAF as a fit to signal, so the null gate rose to ≈ the real VAF and nothing beats its own noise ceiling. (b) `tf_kSD=4` compared the trial-AVERAGED trough against 4× the SINGLE-sample pre-stim SD — too strict (averaging already shrank the trough's noise). Fix: **VAF-vs-null is no longer a detection gate** (kept only for display; an OPTIONAL absolute floor `tf_useVAF`/`tf_vafMin=40`, default OFF). Detection is now **PRIMARY = net inhibition (DIPtf<0) AND raw trough beats the pre-stim floor** (`-DIPrawtf > preThr/tf_sens`), plus optional VAF/rebound. Lowered `tf_kSD` 4→2.5. Added **per-gate pass-count prints** (`dip<0 N | pre-stim N | VAF … -> AFFECTED N`) so an empty result is never a mystery. Slider (`tfmap_sens`) + inspector (`tfmap_detail` verdict, title label `gate`→`0Vnull`) updated to match; DBT carries `useVAF/vafMin`. Cache key UNCHANGED (`biphasic_stmcb_v2`) — only thresholding changed, not the fits, so the existing cache is still valid and a re-run just re-thresholds (fast, no refit). Code Analyzer: no errors.
**Why:** User: "the fits look great … what is our current selection criterion, we need to repair it cause nothing is affected right now." The flexible fit that fixed fit quality is exactly what killed the VAF-beats-null test; the pixel-own pre-stim amplitude test is the discriminator that survives (signal amplitude vs the pixel's own fluctuation), which is also what the user asked for last turn.
**Next:** User re-run (loads cached fits, re-thresholds) — the per-gate counts will show what's binding; expect a non-empty affected map. Tune `tf_kSD`/`tf_kRange` on the pre-stim floor and drag `tf_sens`; turn `tf_useVAF=true` only if garbage-shaped fits sneak in. Not run here (MATLAB-MCP crash constraint).

### 2026-07-17 — TF detector: stmcb fit (fixes poor fits) + pre-stim significance gate + 600 ms window
**Changed/Found:** `ols_tf_pipeline.m` §10T, three coordinated changes. (1) **Poor fits fixed** — the fit core moved from raw **Prony** to **Steiglitz-McBride** (`stmcb`, Signal Processing Toolbox — CONFIRMED installed): Prony fits the noisy impulse-response recursion and gives bad poles; stmcb is Prony-seeded OUTPUT-ERROR, iterating with `filter()` to minimise the actual output error — far better on noisy trial-averages, and it never touches sssim so still can't crash. New wrapper `ba_fit(h,na,nb)` = stmcb with a `prony_fit` fallback in try/catch; `proto_fitTF`/`proto_fitTF_fix` now call it. Added light pre-fit smoothing (`tf_smoothN=3`, ~85 ms movmean) to both the pixel fits and the 0V-null. (2) **Pre-stim significance gate** (new, user-requested) — each pixel's inhibition trough must be DEEPER than its own pre-stim fluctuation measured over **4 s before stim** (`tf_preSec=4`): `preStd` (pooled SD) and `preNeg` (mean across trials of each trial's worst/most-negative excursion, DC-removed); the RAW trough `DIPrawtf=min(mu over window)` must satisfy `-DIPrawtf > preThr` where `preThr=max(tf_kSD·preStd, tf_kRange·preNeg)` (`tf_kSD=4`, `tf_kRange=1`). So a "dip" within normal fluctuation is rejected regardless of fit quality — directly targets the noisy-pixel false positives. This is now the 3rd AND-gate in `affected_tf` (with VAF>0V-null and fitted dip<0), all scaled by the live `tf_sens` slider. (3) **Window 800→600 ms** (`tf_winSec=0.6`). Selector: `tfmap_sens` applies the pre-stim gate live; `tfmap_detail` draws the pre-stim depth floor (green dotted) + reports trough vs floor; DBT/AFFECT_TF carry `preThr/DIPraw/preStd/preNeg`. Cache algo `biphasic_prony_v1`→`biphasic_stmcb_v2` (+`smoothN` in key; `DIPrawtf` saved). Code Analyzer: no errors (benign inline-if + constant-toggle-unreachable + dead-helper notes only).

### 2026-07-17 — TF fit: fixed 800 ms window + AIC order-sweep (up to 3p2z) to capture strong rebound
**Changed/Found:** `ols_tf_pipeline.m` §10T — (1) fit window is now a **FIXED 0–800 ms** (`tf_winSec=0.8`, same for every amp), replacing the per-amp landmark window. (2) **Enabled the AIC order sweep** (`tf_sweep=true`, ceiling `tf_maxP=3`/`tf_maxZ=2`) as the default, so each pixel's order is chosen per-pixel up to 3p2z. Motivation: at the strongest amp (4.3 V) ALL pixels fit poorly with 2p1z — a dip-then-strong-rebound has 3 timescales (onset rise, inhibition decay, rebound), and a single complex-pole pair couples the dip & rebound timescales so 2 poles can't represent a fast inhibition + independent slow/strong rebound; the 3rd pole gives the rebound its own slow mode. AIC keeps simple pixels low-order (guards the over-fit the user saw when 4p was FORCED earlier). (3) Made the **0V-null VAF gate use the SAME procedure** (sweep when `tf_sweep`) — a swept fit grabs more VAF from noise, so the gate must rise to match, else the extra freedom over-detects. Inspector (`tfmap_detail`) + DBT updated to sweep too (`DB.sweep/maxP/maxZ`). Cache auto-invalidated (winEnds + sweep flag in key). Code Analyzer: no errors.
**Why:** User: "lets fix fit window to 800ms" + "some regions have longer and stronger rebound after inhibition how to allow for that?" + "all of 4.3 are poorly fit". Fixed window per instruction; the sweep is the minimal way to let the model carry a variable-strength rebound without blanket high-order over-fitting.
**Next:** User re-run — sweep fits nG×nA pixels over ~6 candidate orders (slower first pass, ~tens of s, cached). Check 4.3 V fits are now good (click pixels: fitted red-dashed should track the dip AND the rebound), and that the extra freedom didn't balloon the affected count (the matched null gate should absorb it). If rebound still runs past 800 ms in places, widen `tf_winSec`; if fits look over-flexible, drop `tf_maxP` to 2 or set `tf_sweep=false`. Not run here (MATLAB-MCP crash constraint).

### 2026-07-17 — BUGFIX: §10T3 pixel inspector crashed on click (row/column concat)
**Changed/Found:** `ols_tf_pipeline.m` `tfmap_detail` — clicking a pixel threw a dimension error. Cause: `muMap(p,:,ai)` returns a `[1×Wb]` ROW (indexing drops the trailing singleton amp dim, so `squeeze` is a no-op), so my earlier "fix" that removed the `.'` left `mu` as a row; then `dat=[mu; r(:)]` vertically concatenated a `1×Wb` row with a `numel(wa)×1` column → error. Fix: force column explicitly (`mu = DB.muMap(p,:,ai); mu = mu(:)`) and likewise `xw = DB.rel(wa); xw = xw(:)/Fs`. Code Analyzer: no errors.
**Why:** The prior entry's "fixed an inspector orientation bug" was itself wrong — I removed the `.'` on the mistaken belief that `squeeze` of that slice yields a column; it yields a row, so the concat broke. Forcing `(:)` is orientation-proof regardless of how the slice/index shapes come out.
**Next:** User re-run + click a pixel to confirm the TF-fit overlay renders. Not run here (MATLAB-MCP crash constraint).

### 2026-07-17 — DETECTOR REDESIGN #2: RMS-vs-0V scrapped → pre-stim-referenced cluster t-test
**Changed/Found:** `ols_tf_pipeline.m` — replaced the (one-day-old) RMS-vs-0V-null §10T detector; it failed both ways: (a) NOISY pixels tagged — RMS of a trial-average normalises by the WRONG noise (0V-block variance), but stim blocks are heteroscedastic, so a pixel merely noisier during stim trips the 0V null; (b) LOW-AMP responses missed — RMS weights every bin equally, so a brief small dip is a tiny fraction of windowed energy and drowns. New §10T `[AFFECT-CLUST]`: baseline is each trial's **own pre-stim mean** (`blkA{a}` = per-trial evoked − its pre-onset mean, captured in the §10 build loop); per-bin **one-sample t across trials** `T(g,t)=mean/(sd/√nT)`; a **temporal cluster** = run of ≥`det_kmin`(3) consecutive bins with `|T|>det_zthr`(2.5) in the 0–`det_maxSec`(1.0)s window, mass=Σ|T|; **null** by trial **sign-flipping** (variance is flip-invariant → only the mean changes, so `Bmat*S/nT` is one matmul; `det_nPerm`=500), max-cluster mass pooled across pixels (t-normalised → ~pixel-independent) per amp; **affected(g,a)=clMass > prctile(nullMass, clusPrc)**. This normalises by THIS amp's own trial variance (kills the noisy-pixel FPs) and rewards trial CONSISTENCY not energy (a small reliable dip → large t → survives). New helper `local_maxcluster` (vectorised-over-pixels run-length max-cluster). §10T3 selector rewired: slider is now the **cluster null percentile** (`clusPrc`, 50–99.9), `tfmap_sens` re-thresholds instantly from cached `nullMass`, DBT carries `clMass/nullMass/massThr/Tmap/muMap/seMap/clC0/clC1/detCols/zthr/kmin` (dropped all Rreal/Rnull fields), `tfmap_detail` now a 2-panel inspector (top: pre-stim trial-mean ±SEM + detection-window + winning-cluster shading; bottom: per-bin t with ±zthr lines + cluster highlight). `AFFECT_TF`/gate/`select_refit_btn` prints → `clusPrc`. Cache key `rms_vs_0Vnull_v1`→`prestim_clusterT_v1` (stores Tmap/clMass/clC0/clC1/nullMass/muMap/seMap). Code Analyzer: no errors.
**Why:** User: "using 0v as baseline is not working its worse than tf fitting, cause now noisy pixels are being tagged as affected and low amp responses are not picked up" + "suggest using pre-stim activity as baseline". Chosen after laying out options (AskUserQuestion → "t-contrast + cluster"). The heteroscedasticity between 0V and stim blocks is the root cause of the FPs; normalising by the stim condition's OWN trial variance (a t-statistic) is the fix, and cluster-over-time supplies the temporal consistency RMS discarded — which is also what rescues low-amp. Pre-stim per-trial baseline (not 0V) de-drifts without importing the wrong noise, and is desirable here since bleed pixels SHOULD be flagged (they must be excluded from §17b).
**Next:** User to re-run `ols_tf_pipeline.m` — first run computes t-maps + sign-flip null (~10–30 s, cached), then the §10T3 selector opens; drag the cluster-null-% slider, spot-check g241 @ 4.3V flags AND that noisy background pixels do NOT, click pixels to read the t-map/cluster inspector, CONFIRM to build §17b/§17d. Tune `det_zthr`/`det_kmin` if clusters are too greedy/timid; watch for a global onset transient lighting every pixel (would mean re-adding a 0V contrast). Downstream `affected[nG×nA]` contract unchanged. Not run here (MATLAB-MCP crash constraint).

### 2026-07-17 — DETECTOR REDESIGN: §10T/§10T2 replaced by model-free RMS-vs-0V-null (recall-first)
**Changed/Found:** `ols_tf_pipeline.m` — ripped out the per-pixel TF-fit shape-matcher (reference-TF reuse, prototype table, order lock, per-amp VAF gate, dip pre-screen, trough-match, `affected_tf=(VAF>gate)&(match<tol)&(dip<0)`) and the whole prototype figure/`AFFECT_TF_PROTO`. New §10T detector: for each pixel×amp, `Rreal(g,a)=RMS over winA{a} of mResp(:,:,a)` (trial-avg excess = amp-mean − 0V-mean); a matched **0V-null** `Rnull{a}[nG×nBoot]` bootstraps `nT_amp(a)` random 0V trials into pseudo-averages (same trial count → same variance reduction), subtracts `m0`, takes the same windowed RMS; **affected iff `Rreal > prctile(Rnull, respPrc)`** (per-pixel, per-amp). Polarity-agnostic (dip/rebound/up-going all count as energy), model-free (no tfest/sim → cannot hit the sssim MEX crash). Sensitivity knob is now `respPrc` (null percentile, default 95; LOWER→more affected). §10T3 selector rewired: slider is now a null-percentile slider (Min 50, Max 99.9), `tfmap_sens` re-thresholds instantly from the cached `Rnull` (no resampling), DBT carries `Rreal/Rnull/Rthr/respPrc/respDir` (dropped VAF/DIP/TR/DEL/MD/vafGate/matchTol/chRef/sysRef/np-nz-nd), `tfmap_detail` inspector now shows the pixel's excess trace + coupling window + ±RMS vs ±null-threshold bands + percentile verdict (no fit). `AFFECT_TF` struct + confirm-gate + `select_refit_btn` prints updated to `respPrc`. Cache key `perpixel_pronyIR_v12_mexfree`→`rms_vs_0Vnull_v1` (stores `Rnull` only; respPrc applied after load so the slider is free). Prony/tfest helpers left defined but now dead (mlint "unused", harmless). Code Analyzer: no errors.
**Why:** User: "look at g241 @ 4.3V, how is it not stim affected?" The 4-ANDed shape gates made the old detector a *shape-matcher*, not a *response-detector* — it rejected genuine responses whose dynamics didn't match the ipsi reference. Approved redesign "A: RMS vs 0V-null". Detection here should favour **recall**: a missed stim pixel leaks stim signal into the stim-blind predictor (§17b), the costly error; a false-positive only removes a predictor. RMS-vs-null is the minimal statistic that asks the right question ("is there more energy here than 0V noise?").
**Next:** User to re-run `ols_tf_pipeline.m` — first run resamples the null (~fast, 200 boot/amp serial), then the §10T3 selector opens; drag the null-percentile slider, spot-check g241 @ 4.3V is now flagged, click a few pixels to confirm the excess-vs-null inspector reads sensibly, CONFIRM to build §17b/§17d on the selection. Downstream `affected[nG×nA]` contract unchanged. Watch for over-recall at low respPrc (whole-field flagged) → raise respPrc. Not run here (MATLAB-MCP crash constraint).

### 2026-07-17 — CRASH FIX (definitive): §10T TF fit rewritten MEX-free (Prony + filter/residue), no more sssim
**Changed/Found:** `ols_tf_pipeline.m` — 3rd crash today (`matlab_crash_dump.38420-1`, 14:56), same `sssim.mexw64` access-violation, this time even at forced 2p1z0d WITH the pole guards. Conclusion: the crash is INSIDE `tfest`'s own optimization (it simulates candidate models every iteration via sssim), so no post-fit guard on the returned model can ever catch it. FIX (user-approved "Prony rewrite"): removed `tfest`/`sim`/`impulse` from the §10T path entirely and replaced with MEX-free equivalents that cannot access-violate — (1) `prony_fit` = fixed-order discrete `B(z)/A(z)` fit by LINEAR least squares (one `\` solve); (2) impulse response via `filter()` (pure recursion — an unstable fit just yields Inf/large → VAF≤-100 → rejected, no crash); (3) the continuous ipsi `best_sys` reference impulse via `residue()` (partial fractions, algebraic). New helpers `prony_fit`, `sys_impulse`; `proto_fitTF`/`proto_fitTF_fix`/`proto_vafch`/`proto_sim`/`proto_chars` rewired to them; fitted "sys" is now a discrete struct `{b,a,ir}`. Deleted `tf_sim_safe` (dead — nothing left to guard). Cache algo key bumped `perpixel_tffit_v11_adaptwin`→`perpixel_pronyIR_v12_mexfree` (invalidates tfest-era caches). Detection logic UNCHANGED (VAF gate + trough-match + dip<0, all read off the impulse-response vector). mlint clean.
**Why:** User: "simplify the script to make a failsafe against crash." The only true failsafe is to never call the crashing MEX; Prony+filter+residue removes System-ID-Toolbox continuous simulation from the whole per-pixel path. Bonus: much faster than hundreds of serial `tfest` calls (the earlier bottleneck), and the guard code is gone.
**Next:** User to re-run `ols_tf_pipeline.m` — should complete with NO crash. Expect fitted numbers (VAF, trough ms) to shift vs the old continuous fit (discrete estimator) — recheck the affected-map + the §10T3 selector look sane; if the `2p1z0d` discrete fit is too rigid, widen via `tf_contraOrder`. `tf_fit.m` still uses `tfest` for the single clean ipsi fit (approved to leave it; low crash risk, and `tf_ok`/compare-filter guards remain) — if it ever crashes, Prony-ify it too and `d2c`/keep `best_sys` continuous via `residue`-only downstream. Not run here (MATLAB-MCP crash constraint).

### 2026-07-17 — TF fit bug: zero the initial condition at stim onset before fitting
**Changed/Found:** `bilateral/grid/tf_fit.py` — the sum-of-exponentials impulse-response model `h(t)=ΣA_i e^{-t/τ_i}` is a ZERO-initial-condition response (system at rest at onset), but the data `H[s,r]` was baselined over the whole 2 s pre-stim window, so slow drift between that baseline and onset left a nonzero value at t=0 the TF structurally can't represent → biased fits. Added `_zero_onset(t,h)` (subtract the mean over the immediate pre-onset window `ONSET_PRE_S=0.10 s`, making `h(0)=0`) and applied it per pair in `fit_all` + `fit_all_amps` (same offset subtracted from the CV split-halves `HA/HB`) and in `prototype`. The onset-zeroed `H` is what gets stored in both caches, so the viewer/matrix display the same shifted data the fit saw (blue trace and orange TF now both start at 0 at onset). Single-amp refit: order hist [1436,513,755]→**[1335,643,726]** (shifts toward order-2), median in-sample R² 0.36→**0.29** — expected + correct: the model can no longer earn "free" R² from a DC offset it can't represent, so R² now reflects fitting the actual dynamics. Verified in the viewer: every trace sits at 0 on the onset line.
**Why:** user identified the fit was starting from a nonzero initial condition and asked to shift the data so activity at stim onset is zero.
**Next:** DONE — 2-amp cache refit + all four figures re-rendered. Conclusions HOLD under onset-zeroing: on-site focal gain slope 1.25→**1.11** (r=0.81, median ratio 1.46, still sublinear/<×2), dominant-τ amp ratio 1.003→**1.038** (still ~1 = amplitude-invariant poles). Exemplars now start at 0 at onset; inhibitory pairs reveal a sharp +transient at onset before the dip (was hidden by the long-baseline offset). No further action.

### 2026-07-17 — Revert TF order 4p→3p (contra pixels over-fit because §10T2 locks them to the ipsi order)
**Changed/Found:** `tf_fit.m` `maxPoles` 4→3; `ols_tf_pipeline.m` §10T `tf_maxPoles` 4→3, `tf_maxZeros` 3→2 (delay stays 0). Reason found by user: the per-pixel contra fits were OVER-FITTING the stim-affected data — new behaviour, absent before today's order bump. Mechanism: §10T2 does NOT search order per pixel; it LOCKS every contra pixel to `ordRef = tf_orderof(best_sys)` (the AIC-selected ipsi order). Raising the ceiling to 4p let AIC spend the extra poles on the CLEAN trial-averaged ipsi trace, and each much-noisier contra pixel was then forced to that same high order → 8-param model chases contra noise → over-fit. 3p still satisfies the original "more than 2p0z" ask (3p2z available) and, as a bonus, lowers the stiff-fit `sssim` crash risk (fewer poles → fewer pathological systems). Window floor + `nPreZ` auto-track the bounds (floor→18, nPreZ→7).
**Why:** User: "the TF being fit now are overfit on the stim affect data, was not the case before, we dont need 4p3z." Correct diagnosis — the overfit is a direct consequence of the ipsi→contra order lock plus the raised ceiling.
**Next:** On re-run, read the `Best: NpNzNd` (tf_fit) and `>> CANONICAL order locked: NpNzNd` (§10T) lines to confirm the ipsi AIC now picks ≤3p and the contra fits look parsimonious again. DEEPER ISSUE to weigh later: locking contra order to the ipsi order is only safe while the ipsi order is modest; if a cleaner-data session pushes the ipsi to 3p and that still over-fits contra, consider letting contra pixels fit a LOWER fixed order than the ipsi (decouple), or per-pixel AIC with a cap. Not run here (MATLAB-MCP crash constraint).

### 2026-07-17 — TF overfitting: parsimony margin in CV order selection + viewer reliability dimming
**Changed/Found:** (1) `tf_fit.py` `fit_lti_cv` — added `CV_MARGIN=0.03`: instead of plain `argmax(cv)`, pick the SIMPLEST order whose CV-R² is within the margin of the best (a higher order must EARN its complexity). `cvr2` now reports the CHOSEN order's CV, not the max. Single-amp refit: order hist [1379,479,846] → **[1436,513,755]** (91 order-3 fits deflated to lower order); median R²/CV-R² essentially unchanged (0.36 / −1.13). (2) `interactive_grid.py` `render` — TF orange line α now graded by CV-R² (`rel_fn=cvr2[X,·]`): fits that don't predict the held-out trial half are drawn faint so noise-fits on weak pairs stop reading as confident. Loaded `cvr2` into the viewer. **Verified independence:** every (s,r) pair is still fit on its OWN impulse response (`for s: for r:` in `fit_all` → `fit_lti_cv(H[s,r], HA[s,r], HB[s,r])`) — no shared/canonical order, no coupling; independence was never the issue.
**Found:** overfitting is concentrated on the WEAK off-diagonal pairs (82.8% of pairs CV-R²<0 = there's no real response to fit, so any model overfits noise); STRONG pairs generalize (order-3 pairs median CV-R² −0.28 & |gain| 0.0096, vs order-1 −2.03 & 0.0044 — CV already prefers order 1 for noise). Sub-sample-τ spike-fitting is NOT a factor (only 2.4% of pairs have a pole faster than 2 samples).
**Why:** user flagged that displayed TF fits looked overfit and asked to confirm per-site independence.
**Next:** refit the 2-amp cache `grid_tf_fits_2amp.npz` with the same margin for consistency + re-render the matrix/exemplar/field figures; consider reliability-masking (CV-R² floor) the gain matrix so noise pairs don't read as coupling.

### 2026-07-17 — Site→site TF matrix on the 2026-07-10 TWO-AMP grid (52×52 per amp) + LTI/linearity check
**Changed/Found:** New `bilateral/grid/tf_matrix.py` + added `fit_all_amps()`/`split_half_means_amp()` to `bilateral/grid/tf_fit.py` (2-amp path: fits a CV-selected low-order LTI TF to every stim-site→readout-site pair at EACH laser amp, caches `data/grid_tf_fits_2amp.npz`). Rebuilt the 2-amp cross-response tensor for **2026-07-10** (the stale `grid_cross_response_2amp.npz` was the old 07-01 [0.25,0.5] session; now [1.0,2.0], 52 sites, 50 trials/site each, H (2,52,52,280)). Results across all 2704 pairs/amp: **(1) gain matrix** reproduces dual-opsin polarity as a block structure — excitatory-hemisphere stim (rows 0–25) → +dF/F, inhibitory (26–51) → −dF/F; coupling is diagonal-local (near-neighbour dominant, distance-decaying), off-hemisphere transmission real but weaker. Median per-pair fit R² = **0.28 (amp1) / 0.38 (amp2)**. **(2) LTI check:** dominant-pole (τ) **amplitude-invariant** — median τ ratio amp2/amp1 = **0.965** over 61 well-fit pairs (on the identity line) → the dynamics don't move with power. **(3) Linearity:** honest estimate is the **on-site focal** (diagonal) responses — median |gain| ratio amp2/amp1 = **1.37**, through-origin slope **1.24**, r=**0.86**, 16/19 sign-consistent — sublinear/saturating (below ×2), matching the known focal dose-response (~1.39). The all-pairs peak-gain slope (0.44) is **regression-diluted garbage** (weak sign-flipping off-diagonal pairs) and is shown only as a faint reference. Figures: `grid_png/grid_tf_gain_matrix.png`, `grid_tf_amp_compare.png`, `grid_tf_exemplars.png`.
**Why:** User asked, on the latest two-amp grid session, to fit TF models from each injection site to each injection site — the causal/connectivity map (README open item) done as per-pair LTI fits, with two amplitudes enabling an LTI/linearity cross-check.
**Next:** split-half **CV-R² is negative** (−1.6/−1.1 median) — order selection is noisy on off-diagonal weak pairs (50-trial halves); consider restricting the matrix/claims to a reliability-masked subset (e.g. in-sample R²≥0.4 or |gain| threshold). If promoted to a panel, apply paperFig/paperStyle and mask weak pairs. Delete temp `_render_amp2.py`.

Also fixed a now-stale hard-coded `'2p1z'` console label → prints the actual `bestNP/bestNZ`.

Applying the impulse params LITERALLY (np up to 4, AIC) free-simulated catastrophically: S1 picked 4p0z → **R²=−8.35** (AIC scores the prediction-error residual, but the panel plots the free-simulation response; for a long step these diverge, unlike a short decaying impulse). User chose **cap poles at 2** from the options → robust. Result: S1 2p1z R²=0.22 (weak/noisy OL step — data-limited, not fit-limited), S2 2p1z R²=0.77, S3 1p0z R²=0.89. Pole-cap also dodges the stiff-fit `sssim` crash the impulse pipeline just had to gate against (see 10:30 entry).

### 2026-07-17 — Fix MATLAB crash #2: sssim (TF simulation) access-violation on stiff 4p3z fits — pole-safety gate
**Changed/Found:** Diagnosed a hard MATLAB crash (`matlab_crash_dump.42640-1`, 10:30). Access violation in `sssim.mexw64` (controllib state-space simulation) down in a BLAS `dcopy` — i.e. in the SIMULATION of a fitted TF, NOT `tfest`. Cause: raising the order to 4p3z lets `tfest` return a pathological system (unstable, or a mode far faster than the sample rate = STIFF) on short/noisy windows; `sim`/`impulse`/`compare` on it access-violates in the solver — UNCATCHABLE by try/catch (it's a MEX segfault, so the existing try/catch in `proto_vafch`/`proto_chars` are useless). Fix: screen every fitted TF with a new pole-safety gate BEFORE any simulation, using only algebraic pole extraction (`pole()` = denominator roots; no sim, can't crash). Reject if unstable (`max(real(p))>0`) or stiff (`max(|p|)*Ts > 2π`, i.e. past ~2× Nyquist). `ols_tf_pipeline.m`: new `tf_sim_safe`, wired into `proto_vafch` (→ VAF=-100 reject), `proto_sim` (→ flat), `proto_chars` (→ NaN struct) — covers per-pixel fits, the inspector, AND the reused ipsi `best_sys`. `tf_fit.m`: new `tf_ok`, filters unsafe candidates out of the sweep BEFORE `compare(allMdls{:})` (which simulates every swept model → prime crash site) and before the LOAO `sim(sys_lo)`, so `best_sys` is guaranteed simulable. Both files mlint-clean.
**Why:** The 4p3z freedom (added earlier today) is worth keeping, but a fitted TF must be proven safe to simulate first. Over-rejection is graceful (a pixel reads "not affected", or an order isn't selected); under-rejection = whole-MATLAB crash. Legitimate dip/rebound poles have `|p|*Ts = O(1) < π`, so the `2π` cap culls only overfit/aliased junk while keeping every real fit.
**Next:** User to re-run (`tf_fit.m` → `ols_tf_pipeline.m`). Watch for `tfest(NpNzNd) rejected: unstable/stiff` prints — if MANY 4p candidates are rejected, the data doesn't support order 4 and we should drop `maxPoles` back to 3. If a crash STILL occurs, the trigger is faster than the pole gate assumes → tighten to `> pi`, or replace `sim`/`impulse` with a `filter()`-based discrete impulse response (fully MEX-free). Not run here (MATLAB-MCP crash constraint).

### 2026-07-17 — Fig 2 session encoding → sequential COLOUR GRADIENT (reverted the monochrome shape/linestyle)
**Changed/Found:** User called the monochrome shape/linestyle look "absurd" → switched Fig 2 session encoding to a **sequential colour gradient**. `utils/paperStyle.m`: added `PS.grad0=[0.09 0.16 0.42]` (dark navy) / `PS.grad1=[0.40 0.76 0.88]` (light cyan) and `PS.sessGrad = @(n) interp1([0 1],[grad0;grad1],linspace(0,1,max(n,2)))` — samples n colours along a single-hue ramp, so it scales to any session count and is ordered by lightness (⇒ colourblind- & greyscale-safe). `PS.sess` now defaults to `PS.sessGrad(3)`; legacy `PS.sess_rgb`/`PS.sess_cb` kept for reference; the `PAPER_CB` toggle no longer swaps palettes (gradient is already CB-safe) and only appends the `_cb` filename tag. Reverted the three panels: **2F** `motion_analysis.m` (points coloured by `gradC=PS.sessGrad(nExp)`, single `o` marker, kept the pooled trend line), **2B** `dose_response.m` (gradient error-bar series + solid fit lines + gradient slope/R² text; both mean±SEM and median±IQR figures), **2D** `step_response.m` (gradient solid lines; KEPT the two-row mean/variance stack — only the colour encoding changed). ⚠ MATLAB function-cache gotcha: `paperStyle` is a function, so `clear paperStyle; rehash` was needed before the new gradient took effect.
**Why:** Three-way solid/dashed/dotted (2D) and o/□/△ (2B/2F) are hard to separate where data overlap; a dark→light single-hue gradient distinguishes ordered sessions by BOTH hue and lightness while staying colourblind-safe, so it reads cleanly without sacrificing accessibility.
**Next:** `_cb` variants of 2B/2D/2F are now redundant (main version is already CB-safe) — stale `_cb` PDFs left in gitignored `paper/images/` are harmless; don't regenerate. ### 2026-07-17 — SELECT model → SPARSE, distance-weighted-FAR-from-ipsi lasso + interactive "Refit" button
**Changed/Found:** `ols_tf_pipeline.m` — replaced §17d's plain ridge-OLS SELECT model with a SPARSE, DISTANCE-WEIGHTED lasso and wired an interactive refit into the §10T3 selector. (1) New model: `min_b ||y_spont - Z_S b||^2 + lam1*sum_i (1/gamma_i)|b_i|` where `gamma_i` grows with distance from the ipsi stim site → L1 penalty is HIGH near ipsi, LOW far → surviving predictors are FEW and FAR from ipsi. Solved via column-rescaling `b=gamma.*a` → uniform `cd_lasso` (new helper `select_wlasso`). Distance = Euclidean to the stim site (`hypot(dspGc-dspSc, dspGr-dspSr)`); penalty ramps `select_penNear=2.0 → select_penFar=0.2`; `select_l1frac=0.15` (fixed — user declined a sparsity UI). (2) §10T3 SELECT-model prep block computes the spont operators (`selGz/selcz`), distance gains (`selGamma`), and per-amp z-evoked (`selEvZ/selAA`) BEFORE the selector so the button can refit. (3) New "Refit SELECT model" button + `select_refit_btn` callback: refits at the CURRENT sensitivity and draws dip/rebound dose curves + a strongest-amp weight map (shows the sparse distal weights) + per-amp traces; reuses one figure. (4) §17d committed model now calls the SAME `select_wlasso` at the confirmed sensitivity, so button-preview and committed model agree. Static mlint clean (no parse/undef errors).
**Why:** User: "refit the no-constraint model every time I change sensitivity" + "add a constraint … best R2 on spont … a sparse model with weights selected farthest away from ipsi." Rationale: predictors far from the stim site are least stim-contaminated, so a sparse distal spont-fit predicts the ipsi site from globally-shared activity — the dip then lands in the residual (Local) without an explicit blinding constraint, and the Global "leak" honestly measures residual contamination. Chosen via AskUserQuestion: distance=ipsi stim site; refit=on a button (not live); no sparsity slider.
**Next:** Run and press "Refit SELECT model" at a few sensitivities — watch the `[SELECT-REFIT] … active px/amp` line. If `select_l1frac=0.15` drives all coeffs to 0 (empty model → Global≈0 → false 100% capture), lower it; if too dense, raise it. Compare SELECT (sparse/distal) vs NATIVE (KKT) dip capture in figSB1 panel 3. §17b NATIVE untouched (still primary; §17c/§18 use it). Not run here (MATLAB-MCP crash constraint). Duplicate SVD recon (prep + §17b) costs a few s — fold into a shared cache later if it bites.

### 2026-07-17 — Add detection SENSITIVITY knob (tf_sens) + live slider on the §10T3 affected map
**Changed/Found:** `ols_tf_pipeline.m` — new `tf_sens` knob (in [0,2], default 1) in the §10T block. Rescales the two soft gates monotonically: `affected_tf = VAF > vafGate*(2-tf_sens) & MD < tf_matchTol*tf_sens & DIP<0`. `tf_sens>1` lowers the VAF bar AND widens the trough-match tolerance → MORE affected px; `<1` stricter; `DIP<0` always required. Applied AFTER the fit cache (moved the `affected_tf=…` line out of the `~tf_loaded` block) so changing sensitivity re-thresholds WITHOUT invalidating the expensive tfest cache. §10T3 map: added a live `uicontrol` slider (0–2) + count readout + new `tfmap_sens` callback that recolors every per-amp panel from the CACHED feature maps (no refit → no tfest → no crash risk); captured the previously-anonymous unaffected-scatter handles (`hUn`) to recolor in place. Slider and baked-in `tf_sens` use the identical rule so they agree exactly.
**Why:** User wanted one intuitive knob on the affected plot to trade sensitivity (more/fewer affected px) interactively. Re-thresholding is pure boolean algebra on already-computed per-pixel features, so it's instant and safe — no need to re-fit any TF. NaN features of un-screened px stay false under any sens (a non-dipping px can't become "affected"), which is correct.
**Next:** User drags the slider to find a good operating point, then sets `tf_sens = <value>` at the top of §10T and re-runs so it propagates to the downstream stim-blind models (§17b/§17d feed off `affected`). Cross-check the chosen sens against the §17d SELECT dip-LEAK: if raising sens (more px excluded) doesn't drop the leak, the extra excluded px weren't the ones carrying stim signal. Not run here (MATLAB-MCP crash constraint).

### 2026-07-17 — Raise TF order freedom to 4p (both ipsi tf_fit + §10T), keep delay pinned to 0
**Changed/Found:** `tf_fit.m` (`maxPoles` 3→4) and `ols_tf_pipeline.m` §10T (`tf_maxPoles` 3→4, `tf_maxZeros` 2→3, `tf_maxDelay` 4→0). Both sweeps already enforce `nz≤np-1` (strictly proper → `h(0)=0`, required by the onset-referencing) and `tf_fit` already had `maxDelay=0`, so delay was already 0 end-to-end — the §10T `tf_maxDelay=4` was stale (it only fed the fallback fit + window floor, since the reused-order path forces `nd=ordRef(3)=0`). Now the ceiling reaches 4p3z0d so AIC can select a genuine dip+rebound biphasic (two timescales need ≥3 poles); 2p0z becomes AIC's *choice*, not a cap.
**Why:** User: "worth exploring tf model freedom for tf (and eventual ipsi tf) to have more than 2p/0z keeping delay constrained to 0." A 2-pole model is one damped mode — it can dip but struggles to also render the slow positive rebound; 3–4 poles give distinct dip/rebound timescales. Delay stays 0 (onset frame IS t=0; no dead-time). Matching the §10T bounds to `tf_fit` keeps `nPreZ` (=9 both), the fallback sweep, and the crash-safe window floor consistent with the richer order §10T inherits from `best_sys`.
**Next:** User to run `tf_fit.m` and report the AIC-selected order (the `>> CANONICAL order locked: …` line) — that's what §10T2 forces on every contra pixel. WATCH the fit-support tension: 4p3z = up to 8 free params, but the adaptive windows are only ~20–25 samples at 35 Hz (~2.5–3 samples/param). AIC self-regulates in `tf_fit` (won't pick 4p3z if it overfits), but §10T2 LOCKS the order, so if `best_sys` locks high, weak/fast contra pixels may fit poorly — the per-amp 0V-null VAF gate still keeps the detector honest (calibrated at the same order+window). Also flows to `contra_prediction.m` via `best_sys`. Not run here (MATLAB-MCP crash constraint).

### 2026-07-17 — Add §17d [STIMBLIND-SELECT]: UNCONSTRAINED companion to NATIVE (dip carried by selection alone)
**Changed/Found:** `ols_tf_pipeline.m` — new section §17d after §17bV, before STAGE 3. Same spont-trained contra→ipsi OLS and same kept pixel set as NATIVE (§17b), but with **no blinding constraint**: it deploys the already-cached UNCONSTRAINED weights `b0c{ai}` (`=dGc\cz(S)`) directly, so `Global = b0'·evZ`, `Local = residual`. Reports the DIP **leak** = `Global dip / Actual dip` (NATIVE forces this to ~0 by KKT; SELECT lets it fall out of pixel selection). Figs: dose curves (dip+reb, Global now = the leak, not ~0) + a SELECT-vs-NATIVE dip-capture panel + per-amp Actual/Global/Local traces. Output struct `STIMBLIND_SELECT` (adds `medDipLeakPct`). No change to §17b/§17c/§18 — those still use NATIVE.
**Why:** User asked to add the model that relies ONLY on the premise that unaffected pixels produce ~zero stim dip, with no constraint. Its purpose is diagnostic: `leak≈0` ⇒ selection alone suffices and the NATIVE constraint is unnecessary (validates the §10T detector); `leak>0` ⇒ selection is imperfect and the constraint is doing real work. Since `Local = Actual − Global` exactly, SELECT dip-capture = 100 − leak. Closes pending task #7 (§17d STIMBLIND-SELECT honest selector).
**Next:** User to run and read the printed `SELECT vs NATIVE median dip capture … constraint adds N pts` line — that gap quantifies detector honesty. If the leak is large at low amps, revisit the §10T per-amp VAF gate / trough-match tolerance (under-selection would let affected px into the predictor). Not run here (MATLAB-MCP crash constraint); user runs it.

### 2026-07-17 — Fix MATLAB crash from too-short adaptive TF windows (tfest access-violation)
**Changed/Found:** `ols_tf_pipeline.m` §10T — the v11 adaptive-window floor `tf_winMinN = round(0.20*Fs)` collapsed to **7 samples** (Fs=35). A 7-sample window is a degenerate input for a fixed 2p/1z/1d `tfest` fit and makes `tfest` **access-violate** (a hard crash, *not* catchable by try/catch). Two fixes: (1) raised the floor to `max(round(0.5*Fs), 2*(tf_maxPoles+tf_maxZeros+1)+tf_maxDelay+4)` ≈ **20 samples (~0.57 s)**, tied to the sweep bounds so it stays safe if Fs/order change; (2) added a crash guard at the top of `proto_fitTF_fix` that returns an empty sys (→ VAF from `proto_vafch`) when `nT < 2*(np+nz+1)+nd+2`, input is non-finite, or `std(r)<eps` — a backstop so no degenerate window ever reaches `tfest`.
**Why:** Adaptivity must only *shorten* windows down to a safe floor, never into the regime where the fit has fewer samples than free parameters. The old fixed window (~0.7 s / 25 samples) never hit this; the per-amp floor did. The floor now exceeds the max `nMin` (16 for a 3p2z2d sweep) for every order in the sweep.
**Next:** User to re-run `ols_tf_pipeline.m` and confirm no crash. If genuinely-fast pixels now get floored to ~0.57 s and load post-response noise into the fit (depressed VAF → false reject), consider the flagged secondary fix: fit contra with `nd=0`/free delay and match trough *latency* rather than inheriting the ipsi `InputDelay`. Do NOT run MATLAB from here (MCP + tfest = uncatchable segfault risk); user runs it.

### 2026-07-16 — §10T2 fit window made ADAPTIVE per amplitude (was one fixed reference window)
**Changed/Found:** `ols_tf_pipeline.m` §10T/§10T2/inspector — replaced the single fixed `tf_win` (reference-amp biphasic span, applied to every pixel×amp) with a PER-AMP adaptive window `winA{a}` = onset→settle from that amp's own §10 primary-pixel landmarks (`inhCols`/`rebCols`), floored to `tf_winMinN=round(0.20*Fs)` samples and capped at `Wb`. So a fast/short response is fit over a short window and a slow one over a long window, instead of dragging every fit across a fixed long window full of post-recovery noise. Ripples handled: (1) **VAF null is now per-amp** — VAF depends on window length (a fixed-order model fits noise more easily in a short window), so `vafGate` became a length-calibrated vector `vafGate(a)` = max(`tf_vafFloor`, 95th pct of the 0V-null VAF over `winA{a}`) (nA×`tf_nBoot` fits; same rng seed per amp so the same 0V segments are compared, isolating the length effect). (2) **Screen** computes `dipD` per amp over `winA{a}` (small loop, windows differ in length). (3) **Per-pixel fit** uses `twl=winA{a}`, onset-referenced. (4) **Accept rule** `affected_tf = VAFtf > reshape(vafGate,1,nA) & MDtf<tf_matchTol & DIPtf<0`. (5) **Inspector** refits on `winA{ai}`, gates on `vafGate(ai)`, draws the ipsi ref at THAT amp (`mAmpP(:,ai)`), x-axis from `rel(winA{ai})`, and prints the window length in the title. `matchOf` (trough time vs `chRef`) is window-independent, unchanged. Cache key → `perpixel_tffit_v11_adaptwin` (keyed on per-amp `winEnds`). `AFFECT_TF`/`AFFECT_TF_PROTO`/DBT now carry `winA` + the per-amp `vafGate`; dropped the stale scalar `vaf95`/`vaf95null`. Grep-verified no dangling `DB.tf_win`/`DB.tms_w`/`DB.rRef`/scalar-`vafGate` refs.
**Why:** User: "make the windows adaptive instead of the fixed one on tf — learn the window then learn on the window" and confirmed PER-AMPLITUDE granularity (robust, reuses §10's landmarks) over per-pixel. A fixed window over-long for a fast pixel loads the fit with post-response noise → depressed VAF → false rejections; a per-amp window matched to the actual response extent is the honest support for the fit.
**Next:** User re-runs (cache recomputes on the new key). Watch the printed per-amp window lengths + `vafGate` per amp (short windows should show a HIGHER null gate — that's the length effect being corrected, not a bug). Runtime: the null now does nA×`tf_nBoot` fits (~6×50) instead of 50; still calibration-only + cached. If per-amp windows look too short/long, tune `tf_winMinN` or the §10 landmark tolerances (`recTolFac`/`setTolFac`, single source in §10). Still open from prior turn: the fixed ipsi InputDelay imposed on contra fits (the "delay makes no sense" secondary suspect for g323). Then §17d.

### 2026-07-16 — FOUND: §10T fit data started at r(1)≠0 while TF pred starts at h(0)=0 → onset-reference fix
**Changed/Found:** `ols_tf_pipeline.m` §10T/§10T2/inspector — user: g323@4.3V is a clear response yet tagged non-affected, and the TF prediction starts at 0 while the fitted data does not — pred and data MUST share the initial condition. **Real fit-corrupting bug.** The fitted TF is causal + strictly proper, so its impulse response is EXACTLY 0 at the impulse instant (h(0)=0), and `proto_fitTF` builds `y=[zeros(nPreZ,1); r]` (assumes 0 before the impulse). But the data was referenced to the **pre-onset trend extrapolated to onset** (`a0 = wA'·x(anchCols)`), which left `r(1) = x(onset) − a0 ≠ 0` — a STEP at the impulse instant that a strictly-proper TF can't represent, so `tfest` distorts poles/zeros/delay chasing it → degraded VAF → clear responses rejected. **Fix:** reference every fitted trace to the value AT the onset frame — `r = x(tf_win) − x(tf_win(1))` — so `r(1)=0` exactly, matching the prediction. Applied consistently to: `rRef`, the §10T prototype fits, the §10T 0V null, the §10T2 dip screen (`Ranch`), the §10T2 per-pixel fit, and both inspector traces (`riW`, wide `exc` now referenced at t=0). Removed the pre-onset-trend anchor machinery (`wA`, `tA_`, `Xa_`); kept `anchCols` only for the screen's pre-onset noise estimate. Cache key → `perpixel_tffit_v10_onsetref`. This reverses the earlier "don't single-sample clamp" stance — deliberately: the anti-clamp reasoning was about the pre-onset baseline, whereas here the strictly-proper prediction structurally starts at 0, so the data must too; the one-sample noise becomes a negligible constant offset on a real dip.
**Why:** User: "TF pred all start at 0 whereas tf data fit does not — pred and actual data MUST start at same initial condition," + g323 (clear response) wrongly rejected. The onset-frame reference is the only way to make `r(1)=0` exactly, and it removes the t=0 step that was corrupting every fit.
**Next:** User re-runs (cache recomputes on the new key); re-check g323@4.3V. **Likely SECONDARY cause still open:** the per-pixel fits inherit `best_sys`'s fixed InputDelay `nd` (the IPSI latency). A contra pixel that responds without that latency keeps the model flat for `nd` samples while its data already dips → poor fit even after the IC fix. This is the same "delay makes no sense" the user flagged for the match. Candidate fix: fit contra pixels with `nd=0` (or free the delay) and match trough LATENCY (`troughMs − delayMs`, delay-invariant) instead of absolute trough time — needs a call before changing, since it ripples into the reference/match. Then §17d.

### 2026-07-16 — §10T reuses tf_fit's canonical ipsi TF (best_sys); delay dropped from the match metric
**Changed/Found:** `ols_tf_pipeline.m` §10T — two user asks. (1) **Stop re-fitting the reference TF.** §10T was re-deriving the ipsi reference (`sysRef`) with its own `tfest` sweep, but `tf_fit.m` already fits the TF to the ipsi impulse response (`best_sys`) — re-fitting is redundant and can yield a DIFFERENT reference. §10T now REUSES `best_sys` when present: `sysRef = best_sys`, `chRef`/`vafRef` via `proto_vafch` against the anchored window, `ordRef` via a new `tf_orderof(sys,Ts)` helper (np=den order, nz=leading-trimmed num order, nd=round(InputDelay/Ts)); the canonical §10T2 order is then adopted straight from `best_sys` (not the mode over prototypes). Falls back to the in-place sweep + a warning if `best_sys` isn't in the workspace (so the standalone workbench still runs). This mirrors the established pattern — `run_all.m:29` already feeds `best_sys` from `tf_fit.m` to `contra_prediction.m`. (2) **Delay dropped from the match.** `matchOf` was `sqrt(mean(([delayMs troughMs]-ref).^2./[40 80].^2))`. But with the fixed canonical order every per-pixel §10T2 fit shares ONE `InputDelay`, so `delayMs` is CONSTANT across pixels — it can't discriminate, it only adds a constant offset to the distance. Now `matchOf = |troughMs - chRef.troughMs| / 80` (trough-time only). `tf_matchTol=0.7` unchanged → accept |Δtrough| < 56 ms. `delayMs` is still computed + stored as an informational map, just not gated. Also updated the stale onset-anchor comment block (it still described the retired GLM template's "DC+ramp nuisance / VIF"); the anchor is now correctly described as a DC shift that gives `proto_fitTF` the zero-start boundary it assumes.
**Why:** User (verbatim): "so lets not re fit tf to ipsi we already do it in tf_fitting, moreover [usi]ng delay makes no sense." Both correct: the ipsi TF is a canonical quantity the impulse pipeline already produces (re-fitting risks two disagreeing references), and a per-pixel-constant delay is not a usable discriminator.
**Next:** For the reuse to fire, run `tf_fit.m` before this script in the same MATLAB session (it leaves `best_sys` in the base workspace); otherwise §10T warns and fits in-place. Caveat to watch: `best_sys` from `tf_fit.m` is fit over ITS window/normalisation, which may differ slightly from §10T's anchored `tf_win`, so the printed `VAF-on-win` can be lower than an in-place fit — that's expected (it measures how well the canonical TF explains this window), not a regression. Confirm `tf_orderof` returns the intended np/nz/nd on the first run (print line shows it). Then §17d [STIMBLIND-SELECT].

### 2026-07-20 — Fixed shared-SVD triviality: OLS must use 500 SVD comps, not 50
**Changed/Found:** `run_ols.py` reloads V at **500 components** (`OLS_N_COMPS=500`) for the OLS, not the 50 that `impulse_config` loads for the dose-response readout. With 50 comps the ~400-node contra grid spans the entire SVD temporal space → any pixel reconstructs EXACTLY → spont R²=1.000 (trivial linear algebra, not coupling). At 500 comps the grid (~400–430 nodes < 500) is a proper subspace → genuine spont R² = 0.951 (excit) / 0.991 (inhib), comparable to AL_0033 (~0.86–0.90). The MATLAB builds its ipsi target the same SVD way (`spat·V/mI`, load_experiments L216) — the only thing keeping it non-trivial there is nSV_load=500.
**Why:** Perfect R² is a red flag; diagnosed it as #grid-pixels ≥ #SVD-components, not a real predictor.
**Next:** If the grid is later grown ≥500 nodes, re-check R² isn't creeping toward 1 (raise OLS_N_COMPS in step).

### 2026-07-20 — Excitatory dose readout must be signed PEAK, not 0–300 ms mean
**Changed/Found:** `run_ols.py` dose-response uses the **signed peak** (max for excit, min for inhib) in the dip window, not the mean. The excitatory local effect is a fast (~60–100 ms) positive transient with a negative undershoot; the 0–300 ms MEAN cancels the two and flips sign across amps (e.g. excit mean: 0.5→−0.33, 1.0→+0.37, 1.5→−0.20). Signed peak is monotone/dose-graded: excit Local peak 0.14→0.92→0.71→1.54→1.99 %ΔF/F; inhib Local peak −0.75→−0.33→−0.00→−0.95→−1.05. Global (stim-blind) stays small on both. (Same peak-vs-energy split the dose-response module already found.)
**Why:** The dip-window mean (peak_mode=3 convention) suits the sustained inhibitory trough but dilutes the fast excitatory transient.
**Next:** Mid-amps (1.0–1.5 V) are near-threshold/noisy; inhib 1.0 V flags 230/432 contra px as bled → fit degrades, Global absorbs part of the dip. Watch whether more reps or a higher bled z-threshold stabilizes the mid-amp decomposition.

### 2026-07-16 — De-duplicated recovery/settle landmarks: §10 is now the single source, §15 SETTLETIME consumes it
**Changed/Found:** `ols_tf_pipeline.m` §10 + §15 — the recovery/settle inhibition windows were computed TWICE from independent code paths: §10 (lines ~314-336) builds `recMs`/`setMs`/`mAmpP`/`inhCols`/`rebCols` from the primary-pixel evoked, and §15 [SETTLETIME] recomputed the SAME trough/recovery/settle from the SAME primary-pixel evoked with the SAME tolerance factors re-declared as `st_tolFac=0.05`/`st_setFac=0.10`. So `recMs≡Lrec` and `setMs≡Lset` by construction — a silent-divergence hazard, since §10T2's inspector shades its windows from `recMs`/`setMs` while §15 reported `Lrec`/`Lset`; tuning a tolerance in one place would desync the displayed vs reported windows with no error. **Fix:** §10 is now the single source — named the factors (`recTolFac`/`setTolFac`) and additionally stores the trough (`troughMs`/`troughDf`) and the per-amp bands (`recTol`/`setTol`). §15 rewritten as a pure CONSUMER: `mAmp=mAmpP; Ltr=troughMs; Dtr=troughDf; Lrec=recMs; Lset=setMs`, recovers the recovery column as `inhCols{ai}(end)`, and now OWNS only what §10 does not — the post-recovery REBOUND peak (`Lreb`/`Dreb`), the REBOUND-SETTLE landmark (`Lrbs`), the dip_win_s audit, and the figure. Deleted §15's duplicate `st_tolFac`/`st_setFac`, its own `y0p`/`tr0p` recompute, and the trough/recovery/settle recompute loop. **Behaviour delta (intended):** §15's empty-`lb` settle case now reports `Lset=setMs=recMs` (settle at recovery) instead of the old `0` — the §10 convention, and more correct. Everything else numerically identical (same evoked, same factors, same landmark formulas). `SETTLE` struct, medians, audit and figure unchanged and verified to still resolve. Grep-confirmed no dangling `st_tolFac`/`st_setFac`/`postC`/`tr0p` refs; the `troughMs`/`settleMs` hits elsewhere are the TF-fit `ch` struct FIELDS (different namespace), not the §10 arrays.
**Why:** User asked (after I flagged it while explaining Stage 1b's purpose) to collapse §15's recovery/settle onto §10's landmarks so there is one source of truth, leaving §15 to own only the rebound landmarks and the audit. The duplication was exactly a maintenance trap: two code paths, duplicated magic constants, guaranteed-equal outputs that could silently diverge under a future edit.
**Next:** none — pure de-duplication, no analysis change. `SETTLE` struct is diagnostic-only (not consumed downstream, grep-confirmed). Runs in §10's existing loop cost (§15 is now cheaper — no second evoked pass).

### 2026-07-16 — ⚠ CORRECTION: `er_ncDfk` was ‖e‖₂ (un-normalised), NOT MSE or RMS → normalised to true RMSE
**Changed/Found:** `utils/controllerData.m` — `er_ncDfk`/`er_wcDfk` were `norm(seg - d.ref)` = **un-normalised L2 norm ‖e‖₂ = RMSE·√N**, not MSE and not RMS. This CORRECTS my earlier entry ("the MSE panels compute RMS") — that was true only of the *windowed* `er_ncDfk_w` (`sqrt(mean(...))` = true RMSE, panel J). So THREE metrics were in play: `er_ncDfk` (E/G/motion/pre-stim) = ‖e‖₂; `er_ncDfk_w` (J) = RMSE; panel H = MAE (`abs(mean error)`). Per user decision (RMSE everywhere) changed to `norm(seg - d.ref)/sqrt(numel(seg))` = sample-normalised RMSE in %ΔF/F, consistent with `er_ncDfk_w`. **Impact: NONE on any conclusion** — `dur=d.params.dur` is 3 for all 13 controller sessions (the only dur=4 session, AL_0034 10-17, is a controller-TUNING grid session, not in the 13), so the √N factor is a CONSTANT (≈√106≈10.3); all distribution shapes, OL/CL ratios, z-scored slopes and p-values are invariant. Only y-axis absolute values + units change. Code-analyzer clean.
**Why:** User chose "root mean squared error everywhere". Honouring that truthfully required normalising at source, not just relabelling — `‖e‖₂` is not RMSE, and (had any session used a different `dur`) pooling raw ‖e‖₂ across sessions would have mixed window-length scales in panel G.

### 2026-07-15 — FOUND: §10T3 inspector drew the GLM fit against a trace it was never fit to (+ template-offset bug)
**Changed/Found:** `ols_tf_pipeline.m` `tfmap_detail` — user: on click, the GLM fit / template-only / evoked-excess curves all start from different initial conditions. **Three distinct causes, only one a bug.** (1) **BUG — template offset:** `fitT = bW(1)*href + mean(fitW - bW(1)*href)` placed the template component at the *mean* nuisance level, so it started at `mean(nuis)` while the full fit started at `nuis(1)` — displaced by exactly the ramp's half-excursion. Fixed to `fitT = bW(1)*href + nuis(1)`, so the two now start together and their gap IS the nuisance contribution. (2) **REAL mismatch — the drawn trace was not the fitted trace:** `riW` (from `mResp`, per-trial pre-onset baselined via `local_periavg`, ALL trials) is what the GLM fit and the t-stat come from, but it was only plotted `if ~haveW` — so whenever the wide view existed the user saw `exc` (raw `Xpct` amp-avg − 0V-avg) instead, computed over only the SUBSET of trials with a full ±2 s of recording either side (`onFa-ctx>=1 & onFa+ctx<=nFall`). Different trial set ⇒ genuinely different average ⇒ the fit was being shown against a curve it was never fit to. `riW` is now always drawn (black, the fitted data), `exc` demoted to grey wide context, and BOTH trial counts (`n=` fitted vs `n=` wide) are in the legend so the gap is visible and diagnosable. The missing per-trial baselining in `exc` costs only a constant, which the anchor removes — benign. (3) **NOT a bug — the fit need not start at 0:** the GLM's DC+ramp is least-squares over the whole window; nothing pins it to the data at t=0. The onset anchor constrains the DATA, not the FIT. All three now documented in the function header.
**Why:** User observed the curves not sharing an initial condition. Worth chasing rather than dismissing: two of the three are expected, but (2) meant the inspector — the tool being used to JUDGE the detector — was misrepresenting it, which would corrupt the paint-and-learn calibration that depends on eyeballing these traces.
**Next:** On click, check the two legend trial counts. If `n` wide ≪ `n` fitted, the grey context is a small-subset average and its divergence from the black fitted trace is expected; if the counts are close but the traces still diverge, that is a real signal worth chasing (suspect `Xpct` vs `mResp` provenance). Then §17d [STIMBLIND-SELECT].

### 2026-07-15 — Clamping retired for an ONSET-ANCHOR (initial-condition shift); IC identifiability limit documented
**Changed/Found:** `ols_tf_pipeline.m` §10T/§10T2 — replaced the single-sample clamp (`clamp0`, deleted) with an **onset anchor**: fit a line to a `tf_anchSec=0.25 s` pre-onset segment and evaluate it AT the onset frame, i.e. a *trend-corrected pre-onset mean*. Because the estimator is linear it collapses to a fixed weight vector: `a0 = wA'*x(anchCols)`, `sum(wA)=1` — so it applies as cheap arithmetic everywhere (incl. vectorized over `Rg`). Beats both predecessors: the single onset sample injects its own noise into the whole trace; a plain pre-onset mean is centred ~anchSec/2 *before* onset, so drift leaves `r(0)≠0` → the step discontinuity that biases `proto_fitTF` (which builds `y=[zeros(nPreZ,1); r]`, assuming zero before the impulse). Applied to `rRef`, the §10T prototype fits (order lock), the §10T 0V null, §10T2's `Rg`, every §10T2 null draw, and both inspector traces. **Also new:** §10T2 prints the template's **VIF against the DC/ramp nuisance** (+warns if >5, se(g) inflated by √VIF) so the nuisance's cost in sensitivity is visible rather than assumed. Cache key → `algo=fixedtmpl_tstat_v8_anchor` (+`anchSec`). Verified the tricky shapes: `squeeze` on `[1×Wb×1]` drops the trailing singleton and returns a ROW, hence the explicit `(:)` at each site.
**Why:** User: "if we cannot clamp then let's remove all clamps, but when fitting/predicting we need to shift the activity to account for initial condition — the activity at zero defines how the stim response will look." Correct, and it splits cleanly by tool: §10T2's GLM *carries* the shift in-model (DC regressor), so it needs no preprocessing; `tfest` carries no offset/IC term, so on the TF path the shift **must** be applied before the fit — that is what the anchor is (not a clamp, an estimate of the ongoing level at stim start).
**Found (identifiability — this is a HARD limit, recorded so it is not re-litigated):** the IC cannot be modelled beyond DC+ramp. A system's **zero-input response** from state x(0) is `Σ c_k exp(p_k t)` and its **impulse response** is `Σ r_k exp(p_k t)` — the SAME poles, hence the SAME subspace. So over a post-onset-only window an arbitrary initial condition is **mathematically indistinguishable from a scaled stim response**: adding the full modal IC basis to `D` makes the gain `g` UNIDENTIFIABLE (rank-deficient). What *is* separable is a constant + slow drift, because `1` and `t` correspond to poles at `s=0` while sysRef's poles are strictly stable (Re p<0) ⇒ `[1,ramp]` is not in the template's span. **DC+ramp is therefore the MAXIMAL initial-condition model that still lets the gain be measured** — not a compromise. It also matches what actually survives trial-averaging (a slow residual), not a free modal decay. Only a delay, or including pre-stim samples, could break the degeneracy further.
**Next:** User runs it (key auto-recomputes). Watch (1) the printed **template VIF** — if >5 the DC/ramp is mimicking `h_ref` and eating sensitivity (try `tf_useRamp=false` or a shorter `tf_win`); this is a candidate cause if under-detection persists. (2) The **REF line** — `order`/`delay`/`trough`/`rebRatio` should shift vs the mean-baselined version and `VAF` rise; `delay` in particular was likely absorbing the step discontinuity. Note the anchor does NOT move `t` (DC in `Dt` ⇒ offset-invariant); it moves the map via the TEMPLATE and the order lock, and makes `dipD`/the display read against the same zero.

### 2026-07-15 — FOUND: rRef mean-baselined but its null clamped → clamp-at-stim-start made universal (§10T/§10T2)
**Changed/Found:** `ols_tf_pipeline.m` — user reported "slight discrepancies" and asked that activity at stim start be clamped to zero everywhere, template generation included. **Found a real convention split:** `rRef` (the reference trace that GENERATES the template) was mean-baselined over `baseCols` → `rRef(1) ≠ 0`, while §10T's 0V-null already clamped (`rn = rn - rn(1)`) and `href` was clamped — so **the reference and the null it is scored against were calibrated under different conventions**, and the §10T prototype fits (which LOCK the canonical order) used a third. This is not cosmetic: `proto_fitTF` builds `y = [zeros(nPreZ,1); r]`, i.e. it **assumes the trace is zero before the impulse**; a mean-baselined `r` with `r(1) ≠ 0` injects a **step discontinuity** at that junction which the fit can only absorb by distorting the poles/zeros/delay → a biased template. **Fix:** new local fn `clamp0(x)` (subtract row 1 = the window's first sample = the impulse instant; verified `rel = (-preN:postN)` ⇒ `rel(tf_win(1)) = rel(preN+1) = 0`, so the anchor IS the onset frame). Applied to: `rRef`, the §10T prototype fits (order lock), the §10T fixed-order 0V null, `href`, §10T2's `Rg` (`Rg - Rg(:,1,:)`), every §10T2 null draw, the inspector's window trace, and the inspector's wide ±2 s trace (`exc - exc(find(tW>=0,1))`, previously a −0.25→0 s mean → the visible fit/trace mismatch). Convention documented in a block above §10T's reference fit + in `clamp0`'s docstring. Cache key → `algo=fixedtmpl_tstat_v7_clamp0`. `baseCols` now legacy (kept, only carried in DBT).
**Why:** User: "we should fit everything such that activity at stim start is always zero, even while generating template or fitting models". Correct and load-bearing — it is the convention `proto_fitTF` already implicitly assumes, it matches `href` (a causal strictly-proper impulse response MUST start at 0, so data and template were otherwise not commensurate), and it costs no signal because the anchor is the impulse sample where the true response is still ~0 (whatever sits there is ongoing activity = exactly what should be removed).
**Next:** Note for interpretation — because DC is a nuisance regressor in `D`, the §10T2 **t-stat is mathematically INVARIANT to any constant offset**: the clamp does NOT move the criterion directly (proof: adding `c·1` to the data leaves the `href` coefficient and the residual unchanged, since `1` is in `D`'s column space). It bites via the **template shape** (`rRef` → `sysRef` → `href`) and via the order lock. So expect the affected map to shift because the TEMPLATE changed, not because the baselining of the pixels changed. Watch the printed REF line: `order`/`delay`/`trough`/`rebRatio` should move (hopefully `delay` drops, since the step discontinuity was previously being absorbed there) and `VAF` should rise.

### 2026-07-15 — FOUND: §10T2 gain+R² double-gate UNDER-detects → replaced with fixed-template GLM t-test (DC/ramp nuisance)
**Changed/Found:** `ols_tf_pipeline.m` §10T2 — user reported the v5 fixed-template detector flags FEWER affected pixels than it should. **Two faults found in the `GAINtf>gGate & R2tf>r2Gate` criterion.** (1) **R² is shape fidelity ⇒ amplitude-BLIND.** The 0V null segments are trial-averages (smooth, autocorrelated), so pure noise traces the template shape well *at tiny amplitude* → the null R² distribution runs high → `r2Gate` lands high → real-but-modest pixels (the affected periphery) are rejected while the strong core survives. This is the SAME root cause as the earlier cosine OVER-classification (a shape statistic ignoring amplitude), sign-flipped: floor instead of ceiling. (2) **The R² formula was internally inconsistent** — `sse` used a non-mean-centred model (`Rg−g·href`) against a mean-centred `sst` (`Rg−mean(Rg,2)`). `href` is forced to start at 0 so `g·href` *cannot* represent a DC offset; any onset offset (ongoing activity / slow drift over the ~700 ms window) is charged entirely to `sse` while `sst` removes it → R² collapses/goes negative → rejection. Also (3) ANDing two marginally-calibrated `tf_alpha` gates makes real pixels clear the bar twice. **Fix (v6):** design `D = [href, 1, ramp]` — template with fixed dynamics + DC + linear drift as NUISANCE (knob `tf_useRamp`); DC absorbs the onset offset (= "response relative to activity at stim start", estimated over the whole window instead of by subtracting the noisy single r(0) sample), ramp detrends within-window. **Criterion is now ONE test:** `affected ⇔ t = g/se(g) > tGate(amp)`, `tGate` = `tf_alpha` tail of the per-amp 0V null through the IDENTICAL regression. New local fn `tmpl_stats` (vectorized GLM over all nG×nA columns; returns gain, t, partial-R²). `partialR2` = variance the template explains beyond nuisance — demoted to a diagnostic/paint feature, no longer a gate. Cache key `algo=fixedtmpl_tstat_v6` (+`ramp`). DBT/`tfmap_detail` now show the full GLM fit AND the template component with a t-vs-gate verdict; `paint_learn` features → `{tstat,gain,partR2,dip}`. Grep-verified no dangling `gGate`/`r2Gate`/`hh`/`R2tmpl` refs.
**Why:** User: "we are underestimating, fewer affected pixels detected than should be" + "better to filter the dynamics and take response relative to activity at stim start so that dF at stim start is zero" — the offset intuition was exactly fault (2). t is amplitude-vs-own-noise = the Neyman–Pearson test for a KNOWN template, so it restores sensitivity to modest-but-real pixels while wrong shape still fails it (big residual → small t). Being scale-free it also makes pooling the null across pixels legitimate — raw gain pooled over heteroscedastic pixels did not.
**Next:** User runs it (cache auto-recomputes on the new key). Check the printed per-amp affected counts vs §10 — expect MORE than v5, graded by amp. `tf_alpha` is the single sensitivity knob (raise → more pixels). If the periphery is still thin, the next suspect is the shared pooled null (t is scale-free so pooling should be sound — verify by comparing a per-pixel null on a few pixels). Then §17d [STIMBLIND-SELECT].

### 2026-07-15 — 638 nm calibration slope changed 3.5× between the two sine sessions
**Changed/Found:** Calibration inspection — s1 (2026-07-01, calib 06-30): slope 0.480 mW/V, 5 V → 2.21 mW, `maxPct` 30. s2 (2026-07-14, calib 07-09): slope 1.659 mW/V, 5 V → 8.01 mW, `maxPct` 100.
