Both files' stored linear fit matches their measured curve well (R² 0.998 / 0.999) but extrapolates to negative power at 0 V, so `laser_v2mw` interpolates the measured `cmdVals→mWmed` curve instead of using `calibSlope`/`calibIntercept`.

### 2026-07-15 — CORRECTED 4-mode result: feedback + preview both help, additively (AL_0048 07-01/6)

**Why:** Mechanism is now readable and control-theoretically coherent: **preview cancels the plant lag** (previewT_steps=5 = 143 ms @35 Hz ≈ the measured 156 ms OL lag — the lookahead is tuned to the delay), while **feedback rejects disturbance** (variance 25.1→15.3 with little change in fundamental gain). ### 2026-07-15 — ROOT CAUSE: findStims mode 1 horizon fallback → onsets ~36 s early; ALL tracking results invalid
**Changed/Found:** The phase scatter is an ALIGNMENT BUG, not physiology. For the FFA build, `input_params` col 2 (`kk`) marks the trial **END** (the rig appends the row after the trial runs): the 87 logged `traj_on` epochs are each exactly ~140 samples (4 s @35 Hz) and `kk − epoch_start = 138` for every trial. `findStims` mode 1 computes `stimStarts = t(kk − horizon)` using `d.params.horizon`, but this session's `params` has **no `horizon` field**, so it silently falls back to `horizon = 40*35 = 1400` → onsets land **1262 samples ≈ 36 s BEFORE** the true trial start. With an ITI cycle of ~12 s plus random 0–0.5 s jitter, a 36 s (~3-trial) offset randomises the phase → the near-uniform phase distribution. PROOF: re-aligning onsets to the `traj_on` epoch starts raises the input's (average-amplitude / per-trial-amplitude) ratio from **0.14 → 0.89**, i.e. trials are properly phase-locked.
**Why:** Chasing why the trial-averaged input was flat while individual inputs were large sinusoids. The old windows `[−1,+4]s` around the bogus onset were sampling mostly ITI, not the stimulus — which is why traj_on≈0.01 and reference_raw≈0 inside them.
**Next:** INVALIDATES every tracking number so far (MSE per mode, gain 0.05, "plant bandwidth wall", "Kp too low", and the phase-smearing interpretation in the entry below — the smearing was real but its CAUSE was this bug). Fix: derive onsets from `traj_on` epochs and score against the logged `reference_raw.csv` (the true 0–6 raw trajectory, on the same `kk` clock as `states.csv`) instead of reconstructing a sine. Then re-run all 4-mode results. Also applies to AL_0048 2026-07-14/1 when it uploads.

### 2026-07-15 — MAJOR: sine phase is NOT onset-locked → trial-averaged tracking is a phase-smearing artifact
**Changed/Found:** ad-hoc diagnostic on AL_0048 2026-07-01/6 OL trials, comparing the recorded `lightCommand638` input against the dF/F response. For BOTH signals the per-trial 1 Hz amplitude greatly exceeds the amplitude of the trial-AVERAGE: input 0.526 V per-trial vs 0.072 V of the average (ratio 0.14); response 1.463% per-trial vs 0.163% of the average (ratio 0.11). Cause: the commanded sine's phase is not locked to `d.stimStarts` — per-trial input phase is ~uniform (circular std 1.88 rad), and the command value at t=onset is 0.535 ± 0.850 V (a phase-reset sine would start at baseline every trial). Only partially explained by a pure free-running clock (resultant 0.28).
**Why:** User asked to see the OL trial-averaged response alongside the trial-averaged input; the input average being flat while individual inputs are large sinusoids is the giveaway — an identical artifact in input and response cannot be physiology.
**Next:** SUPERSEDES the "gain=0.05 / plant bandwidth wall / Kp" reading — that came from the confounded average. True per-trial gain ≈ 1.46/3 ≈ 0.49. `sine_ff_modes.m` MSE-vs-onset-locked-sine and the 4-up trial-average panels are CONFOUNDED and must be rebuilt to score each trial against the actual recorded 638 command (or the intended sine at that trial's true phase). Also settle whether phase free-runs or `stimStarts` is jittered vs true command onset.

### 2026-07-15 — ols_tf_pipeline.m: replace biphasic-characteristics detector with FIXED-TEMPLATE matched filter (gain-only)
**Changed/Found:** `ols_tf_pipeline.m` — replaced the §10T2 per-pixel free-TF biphasic-characteristics detector with a **fixed-template matched filter**. The reference TF `sysRef` (§10T) now FIXES the dynamics: template `href = proto_sim(sysRef, …)` (its impulse response; poles/zeros fixed, zero tolerance). Each pixel×amp gets ONE free parameter — a gain `g = href'r/(href'href)` projecting its 0V-referenced evoked (`Rg = mResp(:,tf_win,:) − mean(mResp(:,baseCols,:))`) onto `href`; template fidelity `R2tf = 1 − sse/sst`; raw `dipD = min(Rg)`. All vectorized — **no per-pixel `tfest`** (fast). Classification leans on the 0V no-stim baseline: a per-amp null (2000 draws of nT-averaged random 0V trials, matched averaging) sets `gGate`/`r2Gate` at the `tf_alpha=0.01` tail; `affected_tf(:,ai) = GAINtf>gGate & R2tf>r2Gate`. New knob `tf_alpha`; removed `tf_rebMin/tf_rebMax/tf_troughMinMs/tf_screenK` (and their per-cell fit loop). Cache key `algo=fixedtmpl_matched_v5` (keyed on winEnd/refAmp/alpha/href → auto-recompute). §10T3 DBT rebuilt (`href/GAINtf/R2tf/gGate/r2Gate/dipD` replace the TF-characteristic maps); `tfmap_detail` now overlays `g·h_ref` with a gain/R²-vs-gate verdict; `paint_learn` LDA features → `{gain,R2tmpl,dip}`. Warns if `chRef.reboundRatio<0.02` (reference near-monophasic → weak template). §10T prototype-table diagnostic (uses `proto_fitTF_fix`, `tf_np/nz/nd`, `tf_vafFloor/tf_matchTol`) left intact. Grep-verified no dangling refs to removed maps/knobs; `AFFECT_TF` struct only constructed, not consumed downstream (§17b reads `affected`).
**Why:** User: the free per-pixel TF fit has too many DoF — it discards the reference pole/zero VALUES (keeps only the order), so noise fits a spurious biphasic and over-classifies; "10T framing does not carry over to pixel selection." Fix (user chose option 1, fully-fixed template): fix the dynamics entirely, let ONLY the gain flex (dose-dependent), and rest affectedness on departure from the 0V baseline null. Unifies §10 (matched filter) with §10T (its template IS the ipsi TF's impulse response), so §10T dynamics now genuinely drive selection.
**Next:** User runs it (cache recomputes). Watch: (1) if `chRef.reboundRatio<0.02` warns, §10T locked a monophasic order — template is then just a dip and the "rebound" discrimination is weak; (2) per-amp affected counts printed vs §10 matched map — sanity-check they're not near-zero (gate too tight → lower confidence via `tf_alpha`) or near-all (gate too loose). Then paint ~10 affected + 10 clean, LEARN & APPLY, confirm gain/R² separate the classes. Once trusted, build §17d [STIMBLIND-SELECT].

### 2026-07-14 — ols_tf_pipeline.m: replace cosine detection with biphasic-TF characteristics + paint-and-learn UI
**Changed/Found:** `ols_tf_pipeline.m` — reworked the affected-pixel detector (§10T/§10T2/§10T3) after the cosine approach over-classified. **Removed the cosine mechanism entirely** (template `hd`, `COSall`, null-cal `cosGate`/`depthGate`, knobs `tf_cosMin`/`tf_alpha`/`tf_cosMinDepth`) — cosine on the dip-only window can't see the rebound, so any monophasic dip/bleed matched it. **New detector A (biphasic characteristics):** fit each pixel×amp over its OWN per-amp biphasic window (`winA` = onset→settle from the §10 `inhCols`/`rebCols` landmarks — no hardcoded 0.35 s; reverses P1's dip-only crop), extract the fitted TF's characteristics via `proto_chars` (dip, troughMs, reboundRatio, reboundMs, settleMs, slowTauMs, VAF) into per-cell feature maps, and classify affected iff the fit EXPRESSES the reference biphasic nature: `VAF>vg` AND a dip (`ch.dip<0`, trough in the dip window) AND a smaller/later rebound (`reboundRatio in [tf_rebMin,tf_rebMax]`, `reboundMs>troughMs`, within the rebound window). Screen loosened to `tf_screenK=0.5` (bounds tfest count only). New local fns: `tfmap_button`/`tfmap_hit`/`paint_redraw`/`paint_clear`/`paint_learn`; cache key `algo=biphasic_chars_v4` (auto-recomputes). Grep-verified zero dangling cosine/old-map refs.
**Why:** User: the cosine template (dip-only window) flagged pixels whose TFs don't express the larger-dip/smaller-slower-rebound reference; wants the classification to rest on whether the fitted TF has dip+rebound nature, on properly-computed per-amp windows, with a paint-to-calibrate option.
**Next:** User runs it (cache recomputes) — watch two things: (1) if the map is empty, the locked TF order may be monophasic (needs ≥2 poles to rebound; the print shows the order); (2) runtime with the loose screen (raise `tf_screenK` if too many fits). ### 2026-07-16 — FOUND: paper "MSE" panels actually compute RMS (metric-label mismatch) — ⚠ PARTLY WRONG, see the RMSE correction entry above (E/G are ‖e‖₂, not RMS)

### 2026-07-14 — network_topology_sylvester.ipynb: van Waarde et al. Sylvester topology-ID — implemented, validated, assessed
**Changed/Found:** New notebook `bilateral/grid/network_topology_sylvester.ipynb` (12 cells) implementing the generalized-Sylvester topology-identification method of van Waarde, Tesi & Camlibel 2020 ("Topology Identification of Heterogeneous Networks", Automatica; PDF `bilateral/grid/network dynamic paper.pdf`). Model `x⁺=(A+BQC)x+BRu`; recover coupling `Q` from network Markov parameters by solving `K=Σ_i L_i Q M_i` (Kronecker LS, optional ridge). Core fns `homog_network`, `markov_params`, `reconstruct_Q`. **Our impulse tensor H IS the required Markov-parameter input** (`M_ℓ[i,j]=H[j,i,ℓ]`). **Validated on synthetic** (known Q + 2nd-order oscillator nodes, r=2n−1): exact recovery, error **7e-16**. **Real data:** method needs KNOWN node dynamics (assumed a homogeneous AR(2) node from the pooled self-response) + `r≥2n−1=207` lags but we have **36** → underdetermined; unregularized `‖Q‖≈4500` (blows up), ridge tames it and at strong ridge the recovered coupling is **local** (corr(|Q|,distance) −0.44) + hemisphere-respecting → corroborates the graph-wave. Installed `pypdf` in `.venv` to extract the PDF text.
**Why:** User dropped the paper in `bilateral/grid` and asked to implement its algorithm as a separate notebook if it fits our work. It's a genuine methodological match (Markov-parameter input) but the opposite data regime (needs known nodes + long persistently-exciting data), so built as a validated reference + honest cross-check, not a replacement for the graph-wave.
**Next:** if a persistently-exciting multi-site drive experiment is run (vs single-site impulses), revisit exact closed-form Sylvester reconstruction with a committed node model.

### 2026-07-14 — network_id.py: DISTANCE constraint (length-weighted L1) — discourage long-range edges
**Changed/Found:** `bilateral/grid/network_id.py` — `fit_directed_wave()` gained `dist_pen`/`dist_pow`: each candidate edge carries penalty `pen_ij = l1 + dist_pen·(d_ij/d_scale)^dist_pow` (d_scale = median candidate-edge length), applied to non-backbone edges, so **long edges pay more and are pruned first** — soft locality on the dense candidate set (long connections discouraged, not forbidden; the fit keeps a long edge only if it earns its weight). MST backbone (short) is exempt so connectivity holds. **Dense sweep (knn=None):** dist_pen 0→0.001→0.003 drops weight-avg edge length 3.54→3.38→2.73 mm and edges>1%max 283→249→179; **dist_pen=0.001 gives the BEST in-window R² (+0.24 vs +0.12 unpenalized)** — the locality prior improves generalization (same story as sparsity). Too-strong (≥0.006) over-penalizes and R² falls. Rebuilt `network_id_explore.ipynb` (26 cells): `show_directed()` now takes `dist_pen`/`dist_pow` and reports `wmean-len`; new §3c "distance-constrained (dense + length penalty)" cell; models-math + knobs updated. Fig `net_directed_distance_penalty.png` (dense w/ vs w/o penalty: weighted edge-length distribution shifts to short edges).
**Why:** User asked to enforce a distance constraint discouraging long-distance connections. Implemented as a soft length-weighted penalty (vs the existing hard k-NN cutoff), which composes with sparsity + connectivity and, at mild strength, both localizes the graph and improves prediction.
**Next:** pick the final connectivity-figure config (candidate `knn=None, dist_pen≈0.001–0.002, connect=True`); consider distance-decay kernel (exp) if a hard length scale is wanted.

### 2026-07-14 — network_id.py: CONNECTIVITY guarantee (MST backbone) + dense & sparse directed variants
**Changed/Found:** `bilateral/grid/network_id.py` — `fit_directed_wave()` extended: `knn=None` ⇒ **fully-connected** (all ordered pairs as candidate edges) vs `knn=<int>` ⇒ **sparse** local; `connect=True` adds a **minimum-spanning-tree backbone** (both directions) floored via `w = softplus(θ) + floor·bb_mask`, and the L1 penalty applies to NON-backbone edges only — so the learned graph is guaranteed **weakly connected** (one component) no matter how hard L1 prunes. Verified with `scipy.sparse.csgraph.connected_components`. **Dense** (knn=None, l1=0): 2652 edges, density 0.98, components=1, free-run R² +0.12 / self +0.14. **Sparse** (knn=6, l1=3e-3): 279 edges, density 0.10, components=1, free-run **+0.19** / self +0.15 — the sparse local graph **predicts BETTER than dense** and its W is near-diagonal (local) + respects the midline; the dense fit wastes capacity on spurious long-range edges (a few dominant sink columns). Classic sparsity/regularization win. Rebuilt `network_id_explore.ipynb` (24 cells): §3 is now "directed weighted graph with connectivity guarantee (dense & sparse)" via a `show_directed(knn, l1, connect, …)` helper that fits, prints the component count, and draws the full directed graph + W matrix; §3a dense, §3b sparse; ladder shows both. Fig `net_directed_dense_vs_sparse.png`.
**Why:** User noted connectivity wasn't enforced ("the graph must be connected") and asked for a fully-connected and a sparse version.
**Next:** sweep L1 for the sparse backbone density; pick dense-vs-sparse for the paper connectivity figure (sparse is more interpretable AND generalizes better here).

### 2026-07-14 — network_id.py: DRIVEN 2nd-order DIRECTED weighted graph + notebook model-math & full-graph viz
**Changed/Found:** `bilateral/grid/network_id.py` — added `fit_directed_wave()`: 2nd-order `ẍ = −(L_dir+diag leak)x − Γẋ + Bu` with L_dir = diag(rowsum W)−W and **DIRECTED** non-neg weights (W[i,j]=edge j→i, W_ij≠W_ji) on a k-NN local candidate set, optional `l1*ΣW` sparsity penalty, sim-error fit (node-space params, reduced POD simulation), warm-started from the directed eq-error 2nd-order fit; directed 2nd-order isn't stable-by-construction so the reduced companion poles are projected to Re≤0 for the free-run only (returned W is the raw graph). **Result: free-run R² 0.28, self-site 0.06, 410 directed edges, density 0.15, asymmetry ‖W−Wᵀ‖/‖W‖ 1.41 (strongly directed).** Learned graph is LOCAL (near-diagonal banded W when hemisphere-ordered) and largely respects the midline (excit-left source edges stay left, inhib-right stay right; few cross-midline at the display threshold). Rebuilt `network_id_explore.ipynb` (22 cells): added a **"The models (math)"** markdown section with the equations/constraints for all four models, plus a directed-graph section with the **full learned directed-graph visualization** (grid arrows width∝weight, source-hemisphere colored + full directed W matrix heatmap). Fig `net_directed_graph_full.png`.
**Why:** User wants a directed & weighted graph (not forced-symmetric) with sparsity/locality constraints, the full learned graph shown, and the model math written into the notebook. Directed adds flow-direction interpretability at ≈ the same prediction accuracy as the undirected wave (free-run 0.28 both; self 0.06 vs 0.11).
**Next:** optionally raise `L1` to prune to a sparse directed backbone; sweep `KNN_D`/`R_POD_D`; decide directed (flow direction) vs undirected (slightly higher self R²) as the paper's connectivity figure.

### 2026-07-14 — ols_tf_pipeline.m §10T/§10T2: TF affected-detection hardened (cosine-everywhere recall + robustness)
**Changed/Found:** `ols_tf_pipeline.m` — five airtightness fixes to the TF affected-detector, headlined by lifting the recall ceiling. **(1) Cosine-everywhere:** the dip-shape cosine to the ipsi reference is now computed for ALL nG×nA cells (vectorized `COSall`, no tfest) and gates `affected_tf` directly — previously the cosine rescue ran only over screened candidates, so shallow/noisy genuinely-affected pixels the `tf_screenK=2.0` screen dropped could never be flagged (the cause of "some affected regions not selected"). A soft depth guard `tf_cosMinDepth=0.75` (dip must be > 0.75·pre-onset SD) prevents pure-noise cosine hits; the expensive tfest still runs only on screened candidates and its VAF/match gate now ADDS cells on top of the cosine base (`affected_tf = cosine ∪ TF-gate`). The screen (`tf_screenK`) now only bounds tfest COUNT, not recall. **(2) Robust baseline:** ref/prototype/per-pixel fits baseline to a pre-onset mean (`baseCols`, ~0.1 s) instead of the single onset sample `ri(1)`; cosine is demean-invariant so unaffected. **(3) Calibrated null:** the 0V-null VAF floor (`vaf95`) is now fit at the SAME fixed order as the per-pixel fits (was a coarse 2p/0z/2d sweep) and moved AFTER the order lock; draws bumped 15→50. **(4)** `dipWinT` synced to `tf_win` (was a duplicated hardcoded 0.35 s). **(5)** cache key gained `cosMinDepth`/`refAmp`/`algo` tag (auto-invalidates old caches). Click-inspector `tfmap_detail` baseline switched to `baseCols` to match. Grep-verified no stale `tCos`/`hdL`/`nRescue`.
**Why:** User: "iterate over the affected pixel selection routine to make it airtight." Audit found the tfest screen was a hard recall ceiling that also gated the P3 cosine rescue; single-sample baseline, an uncalibrated null, and a desynced screen window were secondary robustness holes. User chose cosine-everywhere + apply-all-now.
**Next:** User runs it (cache auto-recomputes) and reviews the new §10T3 map with the ±2 s inspector; tune `tf_cosMin`/`tf_cosMinDepth` if over/under-selecting. Then implement §17d [STIMBLIND-SELECT] honest selector.

### 2026-07-13 — network_id.py: DRIVEN 2nd-order graph-WAVE (node-space Laplacian cone, sim-error) — the velocity model pays off
**Changed/Found:** `bilateral/grid/network_id.py` — added `fit_driven_wave()` and `_wave_figures()` (net_driven_wave.png), plus a unified MODEL-LADDER print. Model: `ẍ = −(L+diag leak)x − Γẋ + Bu`, L = D−W with undirected non-neg weights on a **k-NN (k=8) local candidate edge set** (proper node-space graph-Laplacian, symmetric PSD, interpretable/sparse), leak≥0 grounds the zero mode, Γ=diag(g)≥0 damping, B=diag sign-free. Fit by **simulation error**: params live in NODE space but the trajectory is simulated in an r=18 POD subspace (Lr=PᵀLP) so each L-BFGS eval is cheap; warm-started from the equation-error 2nd-order fit (correct operator scale); non-negativity via softplus. **Result: free-run R² 0.28, self-site R² +0.11, 104/104 stable, 56 oscillatory modes (~7–8 Hz), 452 learned edges (density 0.17, mostly local).** MODEL LADDER: 1st-order driven Laplacian free −0.29 / self −1.04 (monotone, cannot rebound); **2nd-order driven graph-wave free +0.28 / self +0.11 (oscillates, captures the rebound)**; delay-DMD ceiling free +0.64 (not a node Laplacian). The node-space local-candidate wave also **beat the earlier dense POD-space structured fit (0.12)** — the locality prior + node parametrization helped.
**Why:** User specified the graph-Laplacian as a hard constraint with diagonal sign-free B, and step-1 proved first-order structurally fails on the rebound. This is step 3: a proper node-space Laplacian, made 2nd-order so complex modes can oscillate, recovers the biphasic dip→rebound (self R² −1.04 → +0.11) while staying a stable, interpretable, sparse graph — the velocity model was earned AND delivers a big jump, at a genuine but quantified gap below the non-interpretable delay-DMD ceiling.
**Next:** Decide framing: (a) report the graph-wave as the interpretable mechanistic model + delay-DMD as the predictive ceiling (parsimony↔accuracy pair), or (b) push the wave higher (raise POD r, relax k-NN, per-node damping) to narrow the 0.28→0.64 gap. Also validate multi-site prediction by linear superposition once multi-site stim data exists.

### 2026-07-13 — network_id.py: DRIVEN 1st-order symmetric-PSD Laplacian + rebound diagnostic — first-order EARNS the velocity model
**Changed/Found:** `bilateral/grid/network_id.py` — added `fit_driven_laplacian()` (x_dot = -(L+diag γ)x + B u; L = D−W undirected non-neg weights = symmetric PSD, γ≥0 leak grounds the zero mode, B = diag sign-free) fit by convex bounded-variable equation-error LS (`scipy.optimize.lsq_linear`, sparse design over pooled post-pulse snapshots, undirected weight shared between rows i,j), + `_driven_figures()` (net_driven_laplacian.png). B recovered as the signed self-kick per site. **Results:** free-run R² −0.29, **self-site R² −1.04** (poor), stable (all real eig, max λ=−0.20). **Opsin-sign recovery: 77% of diagonal-B signs match hemisphere** (left/excit x<0 → +; 22 +, 30 −; mismatches near-midline/weak sites) — the dual-opsin layout falls out of the data with a sign-free B, no polarity imposed. **Rebound index +0.16**: pooled same-site response dips to a trough ~0.25 s then rebounds to a local max ~0.48 s; the symmetric-PSD first-order model (`[e^{At}]_ss = Σ v_ms² e^{λt}`, λ≤0, coeffs ≥0 → strictly monotone) structurally cannot produce it.
**Why:** User specified the graph-Laplacian as a hard constraint + a grid→grid sign-free B (opsin polarity should be a *result*, not imposed), and asked to prove first-order fails before adopting the velocity/2nd-order model. This is that proof: the first-order symmetric Laplacian is stable+interpretable+recovers opsin signs but cannot fit the same-site dynamics because the biphasic rebound is mathematically unrepresentable at first order.
**Next:** Build step 3 — DRIVEN 2nd-order graph-wave (done, see entry above).

**Changed/Found:** `bilateral/grid/network_id.py` — added `fit_driven_laplacian()` (x_dot = -(L+diag γ)x + B u; L = D−W undirected non-neg weights = symmetric PSD, γ≥0 leak grounds the zero mode, B = diag sign-free) fit by convex bounded-variable equation-error LS (`scipy.optimize.lsq_linear`, sparse design over pooled post-pulse snapshots, undirected weight shared between rows i,j), + `_driven_figures()` (net_driven_laplacian.png). B recovered as the signed self-kick per site. **Results:** free-run R² −0.29, **self-site R² −1.04** (poor), stable (all real eig, max λ=−0.20). **Opsin-sign recovery: 77% of diagonal-B signs match hemisphere** (left/excit x<0 → +; 22 +, 30 −; mismatches are near-midline/weak sites) — the dual-opsin layout falls out of the data with a sign-free B, no polarity imposed. **Rebound index +0.16**: pooled same-site response dips to a trough ~0.25 s then rebounds to a local max ~0.48 s; the symmetric-PSD first-order model (`[e^{At}]_ss = Σ v_ms² e^{λt}`, λ≤0, coeffs ≥0 → strictly monotone) structurally cannot produce it.
**Why:** User specified the graph-Laplacian as a hard constraint + a grid→grid sign-free B (opsin polarity should be a *result*, not imposed), and asked to prove first-order fails before adopting the velocity/2nd-order model. This is that proof: the first-order symmetric Laplacian is stable+interpretable+recovers opsin signs but cannot fit the same-site dynamics because the biphasic rebound is mathematically unrepresentable at first order.
**Next:** Build step 3 — DRIVEN 2nd-order graph-wave `ẍ = −Lx − Γẋ + Bu` (L Laplacian-cone-constrained via Egilmez/Kumar projection, diagonal sign-free B), fit by simulation error, presented against this first-order baseline + the delay-DMD predictive ceiling in one table.

### 2026-07-13 — ols_pixel_predictor_wip §10T/§10T2: fixed-ORDER design — lock #poles/#zeros in §10T, learn TF per pixel in §10T2
**Changed/Found:** `ols_pixel_predictor_wip.m` — reworked per user: §10T now **sweeps only the sampled points** (reference + deep affected prototype pixels), reports each one's AIC-selected order, and **LOCKS a canonical order** `tf_np/tf_nz/tf_nd` = mode(#poles), mode(#zeros) over affected+ref, delay = min (user: "we don't need delay or very minimal"). §10T2 then fits **every screened pixel×amp at that FIXED order** — one `tfest` call per cell, no per-pixel sweep — via new helper `proto_fitTF_fix`. Refactor: `proto_fitTF` now returns the selected order (4th out); shared `proto_vafch` computes VAF+chars for both fitters. Result: every contra pixel is the SAME model class (uniform dynamics), ~12× fewer fits/cell than the old sweep, and detection (VAF>null + trough match) unchanged. `AFFECT_TF.order` replaces `.sweep`. `affect_mode='tf'`+`tf_run='full'` still feeds `affected_tf` to §17/§17b.
**Why:** Per-pixel order sweeping was slow, crash-prone (iterative optimizer ×12/cell), and non-uniform (each pixel its own model). §10T showed the affected pixels share one order + ~1-sample delay, so fixing the order from the sampled points is both faster and enforces the "same ipsi dynamics across contra" claim by construction.
**Next:** On restarted+idle MATLAB, user runs `load_experiments`+WIP; check §10T's ">> CANONICAL order locked" line + §10T2 subset confusion vs §10 (now ~1 tfest/cell). If clean, `tf_run='full'`. Note: with delay fixed, the delay half of matchD is vacuous → discrimination now rests on VAF + trough (verify clean pixels still rejected).

### 2026-07-13 — ols_pixel_predictor_wip §10T: SECOND crash diagnosed from dump = onCleanup destructor → structuredoutput access violation; removed onCleanup + parfor
**Changed/Found:** Second MATLAB crash on §10T, this time on a user-run (not my injection) — so a real trigger. Crash dump (`matlab_crash_dump.10496-1`) = **Access violation in `structuredoutput.dll::signalVisualOutputEvent`, fired from `mcos::impl::terminateFcn` (an object DESTRUCTOR)** — no tfest frames. Root cause: the `onCleanup(@() warning(ws))` inside `proto_fitTF` (called in a tight loop) — its destructor restores warning state and routes through the MCP output-event layer that faulted. **Fixes in `ols_pixel_predictor_wip.m`:** (1) removed `onCleanup` from `proto_fitTF`; (2) suppress tfest warnings ONCE at the top of §10T (`tfWarnPrev=warning('off','all')`) + single restore at end of §10T2; (3) switched §10T null loop, §10T prototype-pixel loop, and §10T2 default (`tf_useParfor=false`) to SERIAL — parfor routes worker output through the same crashing structuredoutput layer; (4) trimmed for serial cost: null 30→15 draws w/ coarse 2p/0z/2d floor sweep, §10T2 subset 120→60 cells.
**Why:** The onCleanup destructor + parfor worker-output both hit MATLAB R2025b's structured-output event path under the MCP session — a host/runtime bug, not a logic bug, but avoidable by not using those constructs. Prior entry's "re-entrancy" theory was only half the story; the dump shows a destructor-driven output event is the real fault.
**Next:** On a freshly RESTARTED MATLAB (idle), user runs `load_experiments` + WIP; expect §10T ~3–4 min + §10T2 subset ~6–8 min serial. If it runs clean, scale §10T2 to `tf_run='full'`. Corresponding correctness (delay+trough separation) already validated pre-crash. Do NOT call MATLAB while a run is in progress.

### 2026-07-13 — ols_pixel_predictor_wip §10T/§10T2: tms-order bug fix, parfor speedups, crash + helper hardening
**Changed/Found:** `ols_pixel_predictor_wip.m` — (1) **Bug:** §10T referenced `tms` before it is first defined (§10b, line ~677) → fresh top-to-bottom run errored at `tms_w=tms(tf_win)`; fixed by defining `tms=rel/Fs*1000` locally in §10T. (2) **Speed:** §10T 0V-null was the bottleneck (60 serial full-sweep tfest fits, ~30 min) — now `parfor` over 30 draws using the narrowed 2p/1z/3d sweep (floor-only); prototype-pixel fits also moved to `parfor`. (3) **Robustness:** wrapped `proto_sim`/`proto_chars` calls in `proto_fitTF` (and the `impulse`/`pole` block in `proto_chars`) in try/catch so a single pathological fit rejects cleanly (vaf=-100) instead of aborting the whole parfor. (4) **Crash:** MATLAB crashed during a §10T run — root cause was almost certainly my static-check/eval MCP calls being injected into the busy session via the tfest optimizer's `drawnow` (re-entrancy), NOT a code bug. Lesson: never issue MATLAB MCP calls while a run may be in progress.
**Why:** User flagged §10T/§10T2 as slow and MATLAB then crashed mid-run. Needed the cell fast + crash-proof before any further validation runs.
**Next:** On a confirmed-idle MATLAB: one clean `load_experiments` + WIP pass to verify §10T is now fast (~1 min null) and §10T2 `tf_run='subset'` separates vs §10; then decide on the `tf_run='full'` map. Do NOT call MATLAB while it is busy.

### 2026-07-13 — Simulation-error structured graph-Laplacian fit: works + stable but RIGID (R²0.12) — parsimony-vs-accuracy tradeoff
**Changed/Found:** Implemented the proper structured marriage (`fit_structured_simerror` in `bilateral/grid/network_id.py`): 2nd-order graph model ẍ=−Λx−Γẋ with Λ=BB^T symmetric-PSD (generalized graph-Laplacian) + Γ⪰0 damping — STABLE by construction — fit by SIMULATION (output) error (minimize free-run trajectory MSE, not the acceleration equation), on an r=18 POD-reduced state, warm-started from the reduced equation-error fit, L-BFGS. Node operator L=PΛP^T. **Result: converges, STABLE, free-run R² 0.12 (r=18; r=28 → −0.26, harder nonconvex fit + ringing) — far below the delay-DMD's 0.638.** Recovered modal freqs 5–13 Hz with fast damping τ≈0.02–0.07 s (oscillations die before the 0.3–0.5 s rebounds); recovered L is dense/not-clean (rank-18 PΛP^T). **Conclusion: a stable interpretable graph-Laplacian dynamical model is achievable, but the symmetric-PSD 2nd-order structure is too RIGID (one freq/damping per spatial mode) to predict these responses — genuine parsimony↔accuracy tradeoff.**
**Why:** User greenlit the simulation-error structured fit (the principled marriage). It correctly fixes the equation-error failure (−24→+0.12, stable) but reveals the structure itself, not the fitting, is the limit.
**Next (final synthesis / recommendation):** DON'T force prediction+interpretation into one rigid model. Use the **stabilized delay-DMD (0.64) for prediction**, and read connectivity from the **directed 1st-order Laplacian** (sparse, local, interpretable) OR by projecting the delay-DMD modes to node space. Highest-value remaining build = the **DRIVEN form** (input matrix B, opsin sign per hemisphere) on the delay-DMD, so it predicts responses to NEW stim patterns (needed for control). Figures: net_struct_laplacian.png, net_struct_freerun.png.

### 2026-07-13 — 2nd-order graph-Laplacian (structured marriage) CANNOT be fit by equation error — needs simulation-error
**Changed/Found:** Tried to make the 2nd-order graph model ẍ=−Lx−Γẋ both interpretable and predictive (the "structured marriage"). Fitting it by EQUATION error (row-wise NNLS on data-estimated accelerations) FAILS both ways: (a) directed L → free-run R² −0.49 and often unstable; (b) Savitzky-Golay-smoothed derivatives → WORSE (−1.47, unstable); (c) symmetric PSD L (guaranteed-stable damped oscillator) → STABLE but free-run R² **−24.5** (satisfies the local acceleration equation but simulates at the wrong frequencies). Classic equation-error vs simulation-error gap, severe for oscillatory 2nd-order systems: a good one-step/acceleration fit does not imply a good simulated trajectory. Experiments reverted (they degrade the file); the working models are unchanged (delay-DMD 0.638/0.448 stable is the predictive model; directed 1st-order Laplacian is the interpretable-but-non-predictive one).
**Why:** User wants the proper Laplacian system-ID (structure + prediction). This pins down that the structured model must be fit by SIMULATION (output) error, not equation error.
**Next:** proper structured marriage = minimize the free-run trajectory error over the structured (L≽0 graph-Laplacian, Γ⪰0) parametrization directly (simulate via expm / ODE, backprop or numerical/analytic gradient, stability guaranteed by the symmetric-PSD-L + damping structure) — a nonconvex output-error optimization, likely on a POD-reduced state to keep it cheap. Alternative: extract an interpretable graph from the WORKING delay-DMD modes (project its dynamics back to node space) rather than re-fitting structure. Add the driven form (input B) either way.

### 2026-07-13 — Stabilized + tuned delay-DMD is a solidly predictive stable network model (free-run R² ~0.64)
**Changed/Found:** Added eigenvalue-projection stabilization (reduced-propagator eigenvalues clipped to |λ|≤1) + a (delay-depth × rank) sweep to the delay-DMD in `bilateral/grid/network_id.py` (`fit_delay_dmd(stabilize=True)`, `sweep_delay`). **Result: best = d=4, rank=80 → free-run R² 0.638, extrapolation 0.448, STABLE (80 states)** — ~2× the first-order DMD baseline (0.315/0.068) and now non-divergent. Sweep plateaus at free-run ~0.60–0.68 / extrapolation ~0.39–0.45 across d∈{3,4,6,8}×rank∈{60..130}; rank matters more than delay depth. Visually the tuned delay-DMD tracks the initial dip + first rebound and decays sensibly (bounded). So a LATENT-STATE linear network model predicts the grid dynamics reasonably and stably; the ~0.64 ceiling reflects residual trial-avg noise / neighbour ripple / mild nonlinearity, not model-class failure.
**Why:** User greenlit pushing the delay-DMD to a solidly predictive model first. Stabilization fixes the divergent extrapolation; the sweep finds the bias/variance sweet spot (extrapolation R² as the selection metric guards against high-rank overfitting of the 52 trajectories).
**Next:** the structured marriage — impose graph-Laplacian block-structure on the delay/latent operator (interpretable directed connectivity + latent prediction), e.g. a block-companion with Laplacian coupling blocks or a structured-DMD constraint. Also: fix the 2nd-order graph model with Savitzky-Golay derivatives / direct companion state-space fit; add the DRIVEN form (input B, opsin sign per hemisphere) for generalization to new stim patterns.

### 2026-07-13 — Latent-state network models (2nd-order graph + delay-embedding) RECOVER the biphasic dynamics
**Changed/Found:** Added two latent-state fits to `bilateral/grid/network_id.py`: (1) **2nd-order graph** ẍ=−Lx−Γẋ (damped wave on the Laplacian; latent state = velocity; W≥0,γ≥0 row-wise NNLS on data accelerations), (2) **delay-embedded DMD** (Hankel-DMD ≈ ERA; D_EMBED=8 lags, rank-40 truncated propagator; latent states = temporal history). **Free-run R² (extrapolation): DMD baseline 0.315 (0.068); 2nd-order −0.489 (−1.27, stable/104 states); delay-DMD 0.385 (0.186, best, 40 states).** Qualitatively BOTH latent models reproduce the dip-then-rebound biphasic shape that ALL first-order fits missed (e.g. stim (−0.5,−2): actual −1.7%→+0.2%, both models track it) — confirms latent states are the missing ingredient. Delay-DMD is the well-behaved predictor (tracks dip+first rebound, bounded). 2nd-order oscillates but is mis-calibrated (over-oscillatory, wrong phase) because regressing on double-finite-difference ACCELERATIONS is noise-dominated.
**Why:** User asked to try delay embedding too (with the 2nd-order graph model). Both add the latent capacity a first-order observed-state model lacks; delay embedding (Hankel/ERA) is the more effective and stable-in-window route on this data.
**Next:** delay-DMD ceiling (~0.4) is dragged by later-time oscillation mismatch + marginal instability — stabilize (project |λ|≤1 / optDMD), sweep D_EMBED/rank. Fix the 2nd-order model with SMOOTHED derivatives (Savitzky-Golay) or fit the companion state-space directly (avoid explicit ẍ) + damping/leak regularization. Then MARRY the two: impose graph-Laplacian block-structure on the delay/2nd-order operator for interpretability+prediction. Add the DRIVEN form (input B, opsin sign) so it predicts responses to NEW stim patterns. Figures: net_latent_freerun.png, net_latent_spectra.png.

### 2026-07-13 — Directed graph-Laplacian network fit (stable-by-construction) — interpretable but prediction still first-order-capped
**Changed/Found:** Added `fit_directed_laplacian` to `bilateral/grid/network_id.py` (user chose the directed-Laplacian route). Model: ẋ_i = Σ_{j≠i} W_ij(x_j−x_i) − γ_i x_i with W_ij≥0, γ_i≥0, so A = W − diag(rowsum W) − diag(γ) is Metzler with non-positive row sums → **Hurwitz-stable by construction** (no eigenvalue constraint needed). Fit SEPARATES BY ROW into 52 non-negative least-squares (scipy.nnls) — no cvxpy. Opsin sign is meant to live in the input B, not the (non-negative diffusive) edges. **Result: STABLE (52/52 decaying modes), physical timescales (slowest τ 3.15/1.35/1.07/0.58 s vs the unconstrained fit's spurious 28 s), sparse directed graph (9% edge density, mostly LOCAL/adjacent edges, W asymmetry 1.41). BUT free-run R² = −0.30 (extrapolation −0.43) — no better than the DMD baseline (0.32); leak map is degenerate (many γ≈0 → decay dumped into coupling).** Confirms the ceiling is the MODEL CLASS (first-order observed-state), not the fit/constraint: a non-negative Laplacian is essentially over-damped diffusion+leak, real spectrum, so it can't reproduce the oscillatory rebounds / secondary peaks (which need complex modes / latent states).
**Why:** User wants a proper Laplacian system-ID. The non-negative leaky-Laplacian parametrization is the principled convex, stable, interpretable first-order version; delivered + honestly evaluated. Its poor prediction is diagnostic, not a bug.
**Next (recommend):** 2nd-order graph model ẍ = −L x − Γ ẋ (+ B u) — a damped wave / networked oscillator on the SAME graph-Laplacian L, which produces the oscillatory rebounds while staying Laplacian-structured (latent state = velocity, interpretable). Also: add a small leak prior (ridge on γ) to fix the degenerate leak map, and fit the DRIVEN form with input B (opsin sign per hemisphere) instead of autonomous-only. Figures: net_laplacian_W.png, net_laplacian_grid.png.

### 2026-07-13 — Network system-ID feasibility on the grid: naive first-order observed-state model is INADEQUATE
**Changed/Found:** New `bilateral/grid/network_id.py` — fits a linear dynamical NETWORK model to the 52-node grid impulse-response tensor H (the controlled MIMO impulse experiment = Markov parameters). Three fits on the post-pulse autonomous window t∈[0.08,0.6]s (ends before the 0.716 s ITI): (1) DMD unconstrained discrete propagator M (VAR/"GDAR" baseline), (2) unconstrained continuous A=Ẋ X⁺ (directed), (3) SYMMETRIC-constrained A (Laplacian-flavored, closed-form via Sylvester PA+AP=Q). **Result: 1-step-ahead R² 0.97–0.99 (trivial at 14 ms) but FREE-RUN (multi-step) R² only DMD 0.32 / A_un 0.16 / A_sym −0.28, extrapolation ≤0; all marginally UNSTABLE (max|λ|≈1.006–1.02 → free-run diverges).** Free-run traces capture only the smooth decay envelope and MISS the structured propagation (secondary bumps at 0.3–0.55 s, delayed target rises, oscillatory rebounds). Coupling |A_ij| is NOT strongly distance-local (binned median ~flat to 5 mm) — widefield blur + real long-range coupling. A_un is 1.41× more ANTI-symmetric than symmetric, and the symmetric constraint HURT free-run → coupling is genuinely DIRECTED (symmetric Laplacian too strong).
**Why:** User asked whether a dynamical network model can be fit to the grid data (Laplacian system-ID goal, VAR/GDAR baseline). A first-order model on the OBSERVED nodes can only decay/redistribute — biphasic/traveling impulse responses need LATENT states (order > #nodes) + explicit propagation structure, so a direct A=Ẋ X⁺ is underpowered. This is the expected motivation for a proper state-space realization.
**Next:** (decision pending w/ user) proper route = ERA/Hankel realization from the Markov parameters (Hankel-SVD picks model order, gives a STABLE reduced (A,B,C)), THEN impose a DIRECTED weighted graph-Laplacian structure (leak on diagonal, regularized/sparse, stability-constrained) — likely wants cvxpy (not in .venv) or a scipy projected-gradient solver. Baseline VAR on the continuous roi_ts (driven, with photostim input u) still to add. network_png/ figures: eigenspectrum, A-matrix, coupling-distance, free-run examples, modes.

### 2026-07-13 — ols_pixel_predictor_wip §10T: TF per-pixel impulse fit (framing A) prototype + first finding
**Changed/Found:** `ols_pixel_predictor_wip.m` — new `§10T [AFFECT-TF-PROTO]` cell (after §10) + 4 local helpers (`proto_fitTF`/`proto_sim`/`proto_chars`/`tern_col`). Fits a low-order continuous TF (`tfest` sweep 3p/2z/4d, AIC-selected, unit-impulse iddata per `tf_fit.m` convention) to each contra pixel's own evoked response (`mResp`), extracts tunable characteristics (onset delay, trough, 5%-settle, rebound ratio, slow τ), and scores a dynamic-match distance to the ipsi-site reference TF. Prototype run on strong amp (4.9 V) over 4 deepest §10-affected + 4 clean pixels. **Finding:** ref ipsi TF fits at VAF 94.5% (delay 29 ms, trough 86 ms, settle 714 ms, rebR 0.26). All 4 affected pixels recover the reference's **delay=29 ms and trough=86 ms to the sample**; clean pixels scatter (delay 0/86/114, trough 143/343/1000). **VAF does NOT discriminate** (clean g153 hits 82% > every affected pixel's 71–74%; a smooth clean bump fits a TF fine). The discriminator is the dynamic MATCH — specifically delay+trough — not fit quality.
**Why:** User approved framing A (fit a TF to each contra pixel's response; claim "dynamically affected by ipsi" when its dynamics match the ipsi reference) as a candidate replacement for §10's matched filter. Prototype-first to lock the parameterisation + detection statistic before any 442px×9amp rollout.
**Next:** (1) Fix the 0V-null VAF crash ("Expected X0 to be finite" → `vaf95=-Inf`, gate useless) — guard non-finite sims/aic in `proto_fitTF`. (2) Redefine detection to lead with **delay+trough match** (report settle/rebound, don't gate); drop settle from the hard match (affected settle 286–400 ms legitimately ≠ ref 714 ms and adds noise). (3) Retune threshold (~0.7 on delay+trough-only match cleanly separates aff≈0.0 from clean≥1.0; clean g18 is the borderline case, delay 0/trough 143). (4) Add a VAF floor (~40–50%) only to certify a meaningful fit. Then decide with user whether to build the full §10-TF sibling with `affect_mode` toggle.

### 2026-07-13 — ols_pixel_predictor_wip NATIVE investigation: nblind=4 hits 100% dip capture + ~0.95 pred R²; "nonStimR2" was a lying metric
**Changed/Found:** Investigated (AL_0033, live workspace, no file edit yet) why the NATIVE stim-blind doesn't hit 100% dip capture at lower amps while keeping ≥0.9 prediction R² pre/post. **Two root causes.** (1) `native_nblind=2` is too few blinding windows — it only zeroes 2 sub-window means, so within-window dip structure leaks into the prediction at low/mid amps (the 52% capture at 3.2 V, 85% at 1.6 V). Raising to **nblind=4** zeroes 4 → dip fully blinded. (2) The reported "non-stim R²" (0.32–0.56) is a MEASUREMENT ARTIFACT: it's computed on the trial-AVERAGED evoked, which is ≈0 signal + noise OUTSIDE the stim window, so its R² is meaningless. Measured PROPERLY — per-frame on the real per-trial pre/post-stim activity — the blinded predictor's prediction R² is **~0.95**, not 0.32. **Result: original NATIVE (keep all unaffected px per amp) × nblind=4 gives, per amp, dip capture 96–100% at every amp AND true pre/post-stim prediction R²: amps ≤3.2 V → R²_pre 0.946–0.983 / R²_post 0.97–1.0 (BOTH targets met for all lower amps); amps 3.7/4.3/4.9 V → capture 100% but R²_pre 0.88–0.90 (only because heavy stim-affection leaves 57–138 clean px there).** Swept ref-set×nblind: the full/native set + nblind=4 STRICTLY DOMINATES every small fixed set (4.3 V/57 px, 4.9 V/127, 3.7 V/138) on both capture and pred R². The `native_nblind` auto-pick chose 2 only because it maximized the lying nonStimR2.
**Why:** User: lock on NATIVE, make "native v2 with 4.3 V", investigate the capture-vs-pred-R² tension. The investigation shows the 4.3 V fixed-config detour was unnecessary — the leak was a too-low-nblind symptom + a misleading metric, not a set-size problem. So "native v2" = original NATIVE machinery with nblind=4 and the proper per-frame pre/post R² metric replacing nonStimR2.
**Next:** codify (pending user OK) — set/allow native_nblind=4 (or repoint the auto-pick to maximize capture s.t. per-frame pred R² floor, NOT nonStimR2), and replace the trial-avg nonStimR2 readout with the per-frame pre/post-stim prediction R². Then re-run + validate (blocked CV / held-out) at nblind=4; replicate on AL_0041.

### 2026-07-13 — ols_pixel_predictor_wip §17b2 NAIVE: reference-amp sweep — hardcoded 3.7 V is strictly dominated
**Changed/Found:** `impulse-analysis/ols_pixel_predictor_wip.m` §17b2 [STIMBLIND-NAIVE] — replaced the hardcoded `min(abs(amps-3.7))` reference with (a) a `naive_refAmp` knob (numeric = force; `'auto'` = pick max dip+rebound capture s.t. non-stim R² ≥ `naive_refNsFloor`=0.35), (b) a reference-amp SWEEP that deploys the fixed config from every usable ref (`Sc{a}` ≥ `naive_refMinPx`=20) across all amps and tabulates median dip/reb capture + non-stim R² + #px, saved to `REFSWEEP` / `STIMBLIND_NAIVE.refSweep`. Default kept at 3.7 V so the headline result is unchanged. **Sweep result AL_0033: ref 0.5–3.2 V → full grid (442/354 px), dip 90% / reb 85% / non-stim R² 0.563 (≡ NATIVE); ref 3.7 V (138 px) dip 91% / reb 89% / R² 0.380; ref 4.3 V (57 px) dip 94% / reb 90% / R² 0.411; ref 4.9 V (127 px) dip 92% / reb 89% / R² 0.442.** ⇒ the hardcoded 3.7 V is STRICTLY DOMINATED by 4.3 V (better on all three metrics); `'auto'` picks 4.3 V. The choice is a clean two-way: max capture → 4.3 V (or auto), or max fit at ~equal capture → NATIVE/full grid (R² 0.563).
**Why:** User: iterate on the naive stim-blind. The reference set drove the whole capture↔non-stim-R² tradeoff but was an arbitrary hardcode; instrumenting it made the tradeoff auditable and revealed 3.7 V was a poor operating point (lowest non-stim R² of the whole sweep for a marginal capture gain). Caveat: 4.3 V uses only 57 ref px (smallest set) → per-amp stability (M1 CV / held-out capture) should be checked before adopting.
**Next:** user to choose the operating point — flip `naive_refAmp` to `'auto'` (→4.3 V) or a fixed value, then re-run NAIVE-VAL to confirm 57-px stability; consider whether NAIVE still earns its place vs NATIVE given ref≤3.2 V makes them identical.

**Changed/Found:** ran `impulse-analysis/ols_pixel_predictor_wip.m` top-to-bottom (selExp=3, AL_0033 0129 e1) to baseline the three stim-blind decompositions before iterating. Residual dip/rebound capture + fit quality: **GREEDY** (§17) median 97% dip in residual, spont R² 0.996→0.871, non-dip R² 0.789. **NATIVE** (§17b, keep-all-unaffected + KKT blind, auto `native_nblind`=2) dip 90% / reb 85%, spont R² 0.996→0.979, non-stim R² 0.563. **NAIVE** (§17b2, fixed 3.7 V/138 px set) dip 91% / reb 89%, spont R² 0.956→0.947, **non-stim R² 0.380** (negative −0.47 at 4.9 V); NAIVE-VAL held-out (even trials) dip 78% / rebound 38%. SETTLETIME: trough ~143 ms, median recovery 314 ms, rebound peak ~570 ms, full-settle erratic 571–1000 ms.
**Why:** User: iterate on the NAIVE stim-blind + do target #3 (SETTLETIME rebound), then run. Needed the current numbers to see what to iterate. NAIVE's weakness is clear: non-stim R² 0.38 (worst of the three) because its fixed reference set = the 3.7 V-unaffected pixels, but 304/442 px are already stim-affected at 3.7 V, leaving only 138 spatially-biased far-from-site px that are poor spontaneous predictors (spont R² 0.956 vs 0.996 for the full grid).
**Next:** (a) target #3 — make SETTLETIME derive+report a session-level rebound-settle landmark (~770 ms); (b) NAIVE iteration direction is a real capture↔non-stim-R² tradeoff (bigger ref set → better spont R² but reintroduces stim leak, e.g. NATIVE@3.2 V with 354 px captured only 52% of the dip) — pending user steer.

### 2026-07-07 — Grid TF fit overfitting: fixed via split-half CV + order cap (model-class finding)
**Changed/Found:** `tf_fit.py` — order was overfitting (BIC picked 4-5). Root cause 1: BIC used n_obs=~70 (70 Hz samples) as if independent, but dF/F is autocorrelated so it under-penalized. Fix: `split_half_means()` + `fit_lti_cv()` select order by 2-fold split-half TRIAL cross-validation (fit half A, score held-out half B), now the default in `fit_all(selection="cv")`; cache stores `cvr2`. CV collapsed weak/noise pairs to order 1 and revealed only ~21% of all pairs (66% of STRONG) carry a response that generalizes to held-out trials (median CV-R2 strong +0.16, weak −1.49) — the rest was noise BIC was fitting. Root cause 2: a residual order-5 spike in STRONG pairs was NOT noise (CV keeps it) and NOT neighbour contamination (tightening the window made it WORSE) — it is a MODEL-CLASS artifact: a sum of DECAYING real exponentials can't cleanly make a rise-then-decay, so it stacks poles to approximate the sharp rise (worsened by the lag-term removal). Fix: capped `ORDERS=(1,2,3)` (perturbation IR is ~2nd order) and `FIT_TMAX=0.6` s (clean pre-neighbour window). Strong pairs still pile at the order cap (signature of the model-class mismatch).
**Why:** User: the TF fit is overfitting, what should be done.
**Next:** Strong pairs pile at whatever order cap is set → the decaying-exp basis is the real limitation. Cleaner fix if wanted: model class with an explicit rise (biexponential rise+decay, or complex/underdamped poles) — would fit the transient with 1-2 interpretable params and remove the pile-at-ceiling. Prototype: grid_png/tf_prototype.png.

### 2026-07-07 — ols_pixel_predictor_wip: STATEDEP reframed as VARIABILITY (§17c2) — Rel-δ effect, held-out permutation p
**Changed/Found:** `impulse-analysis/ols_pixel_predictor_wip.m` — new §17c2 [STATEDEP-VAR]. The signed linear fit (§17c) is ~flat, but the single-trial Local dip cloud FANS OUT with state → the effect is on VARIABILITY (dispersion), not the mean. Metric = |within-amp-centered Local dip|; statistic = partial Spearman(|dev|, state | pre-error). Added: raw per-trial dip `LD` accumulated for split re-centering; a deterministic held-out split (even-within-amp trials = TEST; amp-means for centering from TRAIN); a permutation p (N=2000, shuffle state labels on the test split, p=(1+#{|null|≥|real|})/(N+1)); figSV1 (dispersion vs state quantile bins + boot CI — the finding, NO p) and figSV2 (held-out permutation null + real ρ + p — shows HOW p is derived); `STATEDEP_VAR` struct; `ternstr` helper. **Result AL_0033 0129 e1: Rel-δ (power-indep) held-out ρ=+0.113, perm p=0.025 — local-response VARIABILITY genuinely rises with relative delta. Motion null (ρ=+0.02, p=0.64). Pre-var/abs-δ significant (p=0.01/0.03) but flagged power-confounds.**
**Why:** User: a linear fit is the wrong tool for the claim; the plots show the residual shows MORE VARIABILITY when pre-var/delta markers are high — a dispersion/heteroscedasticity finding. Also wanted a held-out p and to understand how p is computed → permutation null on a held-out split, shown as two plots (one with the value, one without).
**Next:** promote the Rel-δ variability result to FINDINGS.md once replicated across sessions; FDR across states + sessions; consider a formal scale test (Breusch-Pagan / quantile regression) as a parametric cross-check.

### 2026-07-07 — ols_pixel_predictor_wip: add §17b2 STIMBLIND-NAIVE (fixed 3.7 V pixel config) — cuts high-amp stim leak
**Changed/Found:** `impulse-analysis/ols_pixel_predictor_wip.m` — new §17b2 [STIMBLIND-NAIVE], a THIRD stim-blind model. Reference amp = 3.7 V (cleanest NATIVE decomp, user obs Fig 11); its NATIVE unaffected-pixel set `S_naive` (138 px) is reused as the FIXED predictor config at every amp (intersect-guarded above ref so no bled px is reintroduced). Same KKT dip+rebound blinding + `native_nblind`. Adds figs NV1 (dose), NV2 (traces+model), NV3 (per-amp pixel maps), NAIVE-VAL fig (blocked CV + held-out capture), and `STIMBLIND_NAIVE`/`NAIVE_VAL` structs. **Result AL_0033 0129 e1: median DIP 91% / REBOUND 89% in residual (vs NATIVE 90%/85%); non-stim R² 0.380 (vs 0.563). Biggest win at 3.2 V: dip capture 52%→72%, rebound 70%→83% — the leak the user flagged at mid/high amps. CV optimism 0.018, held-out dip 78%.** Greedy §17 + NATIVE §17b untouched.
**Why:** User: "too much stim predicted by contra at high amps (3.7/4.3/4.9)" + "create stimblind-naive using 3.7's pixel config for lower amps, run same metrics+validation". Root cause: at low/mid amps NATIVE keeps ALL unaffected px (up to 442) — enough predictor freedom to reconstruct part of the stim itself (stim leaks into Global; residual under-captures). Fixing the smaller, cleaner 3.7 V set constrains that freedom → less leak, one uniform basis. Tradeoff: fewer predictors → lower non-stim R².
**Next:** decide per-session which model is canonical (NATIVE = better non-stim tracking; NAIVE = better/uniform stim capture); consider PCA-of-evoked blinding subspace as an alternative leak fix that keeps more px. Not yet run cross-session.

### 2026-07-07 — ols_pixel_predictor_wip: coupling-window (§10b) predicted-evoked clamped to 0V
**Changed/Found:** `impulse-analysis/ols_pixel_predictor_wip.m` §10b [COUPLING-WINDOW] — the predicted-evoked trace `P_obs` and its bootstrap null `bd` are now referenced to the grand 0 V predicted trace `P_0V = b_ols_cw'·mean(Z0,3)` (subtracted from both), not just each amp's own pre-onset baseline. **Result: session coupling window 629→600 ms; minor per-amp shifts (1.1 V 143→171, 4.3 V 629→571).** 
**Why:** User: trial-averages don't necessarily have zero mean (a few high-variability trials leave a common baseline drift), so a self-baseline reference biases the coupling-end test. Clamping every amp AND the null to the 0 V condition makes the test measure departure from 0 V, not from a possibly-corrupted self-baseline.
**Next:** none — cosmetic/robustness; coupling window still drives the same §14/§17 downstream windows.

### 2026-07-07 — ols_pixel_predictor_wip: STATEDEP (§17c) DV back to SIGNED deviation
**Changed/Found:** `impulse-analysis/ols_pixel_predictor_wip.m` §17c — DV reverted from |dev| to SIGNED within-amp z-deviation `(dip-ampMean)/std` (carries direction: <0 deeper, >0 shallower). Updated print header, scatter y-label/yline (centered 0), sgtitle. **Result AL_0033 0129 e1: signed partials near-zero (Motion −0.01, Rel-δ −0.01) vs the abs metric's Rel-δ +0.15 — high-δ trials are more VARIABLE (abs caught it) but not systematically deeper/shallower (signed washes out).**
**Why:** User: "state dep don't use abs, so deviation carries direction of error." (Reverses the 2026-07-06 abs-dev change, "for now".)
**Next:** keep both framings in mind — abs = unpredictability magnitude, signed = directional bias; report whichever the state question needs per panel.

### 2026-07-07 — ols_pixel_predictor_wip: STATEDEP (§17c) add pre-stim-error TRUST control
**Changed/Found:** `impulse-analysis/ols_pixel_predictor_wip.m` §17c — added a trust control: `trust_r = Spearman(|DV|, pre-stim error)` diagnostic; a "trust-only" partial-corr column recomputed on the well-predicted half (`PRE ≤ median`); scatter points now COLORED by pre-stim error (parula, colorbar "low=trustworthy"); title shows both full and trust-only ρ. New struct fields `rho_partial_trust/p_partial_trust/trust_r/pre_error/trust_ok`. **Result AL_0033 0129 e1: trust_r=+0.156 (noisier baseline fits do inflate the deviation); trust-only partials remain near-zero for admissible states, so the null signed result isn't a fit-quality artifact.**
**Why:** User: "add a control where pre-stim prediction error defines how much we can trust the deviation metric." A trial whose prediction already fails before onset has an untrustworthy dip deviation; restricting to well-predicted trials tests whether any state effect is genuine vs baseline mis-fit.
**Next:** once multi-session, could weight the pooled partial by inverse pre-error instead of a median split.

### 2026-07-07 — ols_pixel_predictor_wip: tune native_nblind (sweep + auto-pick) to recover non-stim R²
**Changed/Found:** `impulse-analysis/ols_pixel_predictor_wip.m` §17b — refactored NATIVE into cache-once + sweep + final-compute. New `native_project` local fn returns (bN,yg,rL,r2ns,dipCap,rebCap,r2sb,nCon); a cache loop computes the expensive per-amp SVD reconstruction / `decomposition(Gz)` ONCE, then a sweep over `nblind_grid=[2 3 4 5 6 8]` scores each candidate (median dip cap, rebound cap, non-stim R², spont R²b) and auto-picks the nblind maximizing non-stim R² subject to `cap_dip_min=90` & `cap_reb_min=80`. **Result AL_0033 0129 e1: picked native_nblind=2 → median non-stim R² 0.32→0.563 (nearly doubled) while dip cap 90% / rebound cap 85%; spont R²b 0.979.** Sweep table: nb2=0.563, nb3/4/5≈0.32, nb6=0.09, nb8=0.18. Greedy §17 untouched.
**Why:** Prior 4-window blinding cost too much non-stim R² (0.32 median, negatives at 2.7/4.9 V — flagged in the 07-07 biphasic entry's Next). Coarser 2-window (dip+rebound) blinding constrains fewer directions, so Global tracks Actual better outside the stim window while the residual still carries ~90%/85% of the biphasic effect — the intended tradeoff. Cache-once avoids re-running the 17 s SVD read per sweep candidate.
**Next:** at nb=2 one amp (4.90 V) still has negative non-stim R² (−0.62, small kept-px set n=127); acceptable at the median but worth a per-amp adaptive nblind if it recurs across sessions.

### 2026-07-07 — ols_pixel_predictor_wip: add §17bV NATIVE-VALIDATION (overfitting + performance metrics)
**Changed/Found:** `impulse-analysis/ols_pixel_predictor_wip.m` — new §17bV [NATIVE-VALIDATION] section (gated `RUN_NATIVE_VAL`, NATIVE only; greedy untouched). Four checks + figure + `NATIVE_VAL` struct: **M1** blocked 5-fold CV of the spont fit (contiguous folds), **M2** circular-shift permutation null of spont R², **M3** NATIVE held-out dip/rebound capture (odd trials→blinding subspace, even→test), **M4** weight-map bootstrap stability. **Result AL_0033 0129 e1: M1 held-out R²=0.992±0.008, train 0.996, optimism 0.004 (no overfit); M2 real 0.996 vs null 0.078±0.005, p=0.005; M3 held-out dip 78%, rebound 11%, held-out predDip 0.24; M4 weight r=0.999.** → spont predictor is significant, stable, not overfitting; dip decomposition generalizes to held-out trials, rebound noisier.
**Why:** User asked for a statistical validation of the models (overfitting + performance metrics). Contiguous-block CV chosen because widefield frames are temporally autocorrelated (random-frame CV leaks); permutation gives an honest p vs the parametric R²; held-out trial split tests that the dip-blinding generalizes, not just fits the trials that defined the constraint.
**Next:** cross-session / leave-one-session-out validation (allSelExp) for generalization across mice; FDR the STATEDEP permutation p once multi-session.

### 2026-07-07 — ols_pixel_predictor_wip: NATIVE stim-blind now captures DIP+REBOUND (biphasic) in the residual
**Changed/Found:** `impulse-analysis/ols_pixel_predictor_wip.m` §17b — extended NATIVE's dip-blinding from a single equality constraint to a **K-column subspace projection** (`native_nblind=4` equal sub-windows spanning onset→settle, each column = sub-window mean evoked direction; KKT `b=b0-G⁻¹D(D'G⁻¹D)⁺D'b0`). Residual (Local) now carries BOTH the dip and the slow positive rebound (previously the rebound leaked into Global). Added a **shared parametric biphasic model** (fast+slow gamma, peaks 143/571 ms, same shape all amps; per-amp gains `kDip`/`kReb`) overlaid on the traces. figNB1 now 2 panels (dip + rebound dose curves); struct fields renamed (ActualDip/Reb, LocalDip/Reb, r2nonStim, kDip/kReb). **Result: median DIP 100% + REBOUND 99% in residual; rebound dose-response Actual≈Local, Global≈0; spont R² cost 0.996→0.970.**
**Why:** User (Fig-8 obs): as amplitude rises the ipsi shows the dip then a rebound jumping activity out to ~700 ms; wanted the residual to capture the FULL biphasic effect, not just the dip, and suggested a model. Greedy left unchanged per user.
**Next:** NATIVE non-stim R² dropped (0.32 median, negative at 2.7/4.9 V) — 4 constraints + smaller post-settle window is the cost of full-window blinding; if it matters, reduce native_nblind or use rank-k PCs of the evoked instead of equal sub-windows.

### 2026-07-07 — ols_pixel_predictor_wip: seed RNG + raise bootstrap draws (fix stim-affected detection inconsistency)
**Changed/Found:** `impulse-analysis/ols_pixel_predictor_wip.m` — added `rng(7,'twister')` near the top; raised `bleed_nRand` 200→1000 (§10 affect) and `bc_nRand` 300→600 (§14 bleedchar). The affect gates (z/corr/peak) are deterministic; only the per-pixel 0V bootstrap NULL had sampling noise, so with no seed the null mean/std wobbled run-to-run and pixels near z=2 flipped in/out — the source of the detection inconsistency. Seed → bit-for-bit reproducible; more draws → borderline pixels stop flipping.
**Why:** User: "we still have inconsistency in stim affected pixel detection." Diagnosed the stochastic source as the unseeded bootstrap null (the matched-filter score itself is deterministic). Seed + more draws is the minimal robust fix; the strong near-site core (z≫2) was always stable, only the boundary moved.
**Next:** optional — colour the affect/bleed map by flag-probability (run the null a few times, keep ≥80% agreement) so borderline pixels read as borderline rather than binary.

**Changed/Found:** `impulse-analysis/ols_pixel_predictor_wip.m` §17c — (1) the per-trial loop now stores full per-trial traces (`trAll`/`ygAll`/`rlAll`) + a pooled (amp,trial) index (`AMPi`/`TRi`). (2) New figure `figTP` [TRIALPRED], placed BEFORE the state-dep analysis: 2 rows × nA amps — top = 5 example trials' actual ipsi (grey) + stim-blind prediction (red), thin, with amp-avg actual (thick blk) + amp-avg pred (thick red); bottom = the 5 per-trial residuals (thin blue) + amp-avg residual (thick blue). (3) The state scatter (`figST2`) is now CLICKABLE: `statedep_click` (new local fn) maps the nearest point → its (amp,trial) → a 2-panel inspector (this trial's actual/pred ipsi vs amp-avg; residual vs amp-avg residual with the dip window marked). ### 2026-07-06 — ols_pixel_predictor_wip: §10 AFFECT gains a peak-time gate (evoked peak must be <=700ms)
**Changed/Found:** `impulse-analysis/ols_pixel_predictor_wip.m` §10 — added a third gate to the stim-affected call: a pixel is affected only if its LARGEST evoked deflection (argmax|amp-0V| over the full post-onset window) lands within `peak_max_ms=700` ms. Detection is now `zImp>2 & corrImp>0.40 & peakMs<=700`. Stored `peakMsA` for audit; header text updated. **Effect AL_0033 0129 e1: low-amp false positives eliminated — 1.10 V 6%→0%, 1.60 V 10%→0%; high-amp core trimmed of late stragglers (3.70 72%→63%, 4.30 91%→86%, 4.90 81%→74%).** Map now 0/442 affected at 0.5-2.7 V.
**Why:** User: non-stim deviations were being mischaracterized as bled because a pixel could slope like the dip inside the 286 ms matched-filter window while its true dominant feature was a late (hemodynamic/behavioral) peak. The genuine stim inhibition troughs at ~143 ms (§15, amplitude-invariant), so requiring the evoked peak <=700 ms rejects late wander. User confirmed this only affected the AFFECTED (bled) side, not unaffected-pixel identification.
**Next:** none — cleaner `affected` propagated to §17/§17b/§17c (greedy/native now start from full 442 clean px at 1.1-2.7 V); verify the 700 ms cutoff still admits all genuine high-amp core px on other sessions.

### 2026-07-06 — ols_pixel_predictor_wip: STATEDEP DV → ABSOLUTE deviation |dip−ampMean| (was signed z)
**Changed/Found:** `impulse-analysis/ols_pixel_predictor_wip.m` §17c — changed the state-dep DV from signed within-amp z of the Local dip to `|dip − amp-mean|` normalized to the amp's mean |dev| (1 = average-deviation trial; matches CP-RES `devL1`). Updated NOTE/labels (ref line at 1, "unpredictability magnitude"). **New result AL_0033 0129 e1: Motion ρ=−0.04 (n.s.); Rel-δ ρ=+0.10 (p<0.05, power-indep); Pre-var +0.18 / Pre-δ +0.16 flagged confound.** The admissible Rel-δ effect (invisible under the signed metric, +0.01) emerges: the single-trial Local dip departs MORE from the amp template in synchronized (high rel-δ) states → stim response less predictable there, Motion null.
**Why:** User: "state dep plots do abs dev instead of dev." Signed deviation cancels bigger-vs-smaller departures, washing out the predictability effect; the absolute (L1) deviation is direction-agnostic and is exactly why CP-RES uses it as the primary DV. Consistent with CP-PREDQ (rel-δ genuine +0.29, motion null).
**Next:** Confirm the Rel-δ effect replicates across sessions (allSelExp) before treating it as more than n=1; it is small (+0.10) and only marginally past p<0.05.

### 2026-07-06 — ols_pixel_predictor_wip: add §17c STATEDEP (trial-by-trial state-dependence of the Local dip)
**Changed/Found:** `impulse-analysis/ols_pixel_predictor_wip.m` — added §17c [STATEDEP] after §17b. For every trial it builds the greedy stim-blind (§17 `bKeepA`) prediction from THAT trial's own contra pixels, subtracts from the trial's actual ipsi → single-trial Local residual; DV = its 0-300 ms dip, z-scored WITHIN amp (removes the dose-response mean). Decisive test `partialcorr(localDip_z, state | dev_pre)` (Spearman), dev_pre = pre-onset residual energy. States use CP-RES/cp_residual_core defs verbatim: Motion = Σ|z| of session motion trace over [-2,+0.5]s; Pre-var = var(y) [-1,+0.5]s; Pre-δ = 1-4 Hz power same window; plus Rel-δ = δ/broadband (power-independent). Two figs (dose curve + single-trial SD bars; 4-panel state scatters) + STATEDEP struct. **Result AL_0033 0129 e1: Motion ρ=−0.01, Rel-δ ρ=+0.01 (both null, n.s.); Pre-var +0.04 / Pre-δ +0.02 flagged POWER-CONFOUND.** → single-trial Local inhibition dip is essentially STATE-INVARIANT this session.
**Why:** Journal 2026-06-16 scoping: map per-trial impulse prediction error to brain state (motion, pre-stim var/delta) — does the stim response itself depend on state, or only baseline prediction quality? Honors the established caveat (RESEARCH 2026-07-01/02) that raw var + abs-δ are signal-power confounds; only Motion + Rel-δ are admissible, so those are the only rows interpreted.
**Next:** Greedy degenerated to keep=2 px at 4.90 V (nonDipR²=−0.36) → noisy per-trial Global at top amp; if STATEDEP needs a cleaner predictor, swap `bKeepA`→`bUseN` (NATIVE weights). Also run across sessions (allSelExp) to see if state-invariance holds beyond n=1.

### 2026-07-06 — ols_pixel_predictor_wip: BLEEDCHAR map → amplitude-weighted severity (not raw amp count)
**Changed/Found:** `impulse-analysis/ols_pixel_predictor_wip.m` §14 — the BLEEDCHAR clickable map (fig 4, right panel) previously colored each grid pixel by `nBledAmp` (# amps bled at, parula 0..nA). Replaced with `sevBleed = bledA*amps(:)` = Σ of the voltages of the amps each pixel is bled at; encoded by BOTH marker size (14..58) and a hot colormap. A pixel bled only at 4.9 V now outranks one bled at 0.5+1.1+1.6 V; unbled px sit small/dark. Same `bledA` bleed call, only the color weight changed. `sevBleed` added to BC + BLEEDCHAR structs.
**Why:** User wants the map to point out which pixels are affected by MORE *and* HIGHER amps, not just the raw number of amps — a plain count treats a low-amp-only pixel the same as a high-amp one. The amplitude-weighted sum surfaces the near-site, radially-graded, high-power bleed core (visible as the white/yellow medial edge next to the site).
**Next:** none — the ALLSESS BLEEDCHAR map (§ headless, ~line 1512) still uses `# amps bled`; repoint it to `sevBleed` if the cross-session panel needs to match.

### 2026-07-06 — ols_pixel_predictor_wip: add §17b STIMBLIND-NATIVE (all unaffected px, dip zeroed by constraint)
**Changed/Found:** `impulse-analysis/ols_pixel_predictor_wip.m` — added §17b [STIMBLIND-NATIVE] between the §17 STIMBLIND struct and §18 [ALLSESS]. Unlike the greedy (which prunes down to a sparse set), NATIVE keeps ALL §10-unaffected px (`active=~affected`) and enforces dip-blindness by projecting the unconstrained spont OLS off the dip direction via KKT/Lagrange: `bN = b0 - Gd*((d'*b0)/denom)` with `b0=G\c`, `d=evDip(S)`, `Gd=G\d`, `denom=d'*Gd`. Reports nUse, spont R² all→constrained, non-dip R², predDip(~0), Local dip, %capt; 3 figs (figNB1 dose, figNB2 per-amp, figNB3 used-px maps) + STIMBLIND_NATIVE struct. Ran clean: **100% dip capture at every amp** (predDip exactly ±0.0000), uses 442 px at low amps → 30/97 at 4.3/4.9V; non-dip R² beats greedy where it matters (0.974 vs 0.894 @0.5V, 0.871 vs 0.773 @3.2V).
**Why:** User: wants an accuracy-first alternate to the sparse greedy — "take all available pixels ... sparsity is not important but valued." At low amps the greedy was leaving many clean unaffected px (non-black in the affect map) unused; keeping them all raises out-of-window (non-dip) accuracy while the equality constraint still guarantees the predictor is exactly blind to the dip.
**Next:** 1.10V shows non-dip R²=−1.485 (constraint over-bends the fit on that one amp; unconstrained ceiling there is 0.983, greedy got 0.592). Isolated to one amp — revisit if it recurs on other sessions; consider a soft (ridge-penalized) dip constraint instead of hard equality if it does.

### 2026-07-06 — ols_pixel_predictor_wip: §10 affect detector flags INHIBITION ONLY (signed)
**Changed/Found:** `impulse-analysis/ols_pixel_predictor_wip.m` §10 — changed the affect gate from `abs(zImp)>impulse_z & abs(corrImp)>corr_min` to SIGNED `zImp>impulse_z & corrImp>corr_min` (and `stimDev=zImp`, not `abs`). Now only a DIP in the template direction is flagged; an up-going/positive deflection anti-correlates with the dip template and is rejected. AL_0033 1.1 V: affected are now ~31 near-site medial px (was over-detecting far px with a positive peak at window-end).
**Why:** User: the affect detector became too aggressive — at 1.1 V many far-off px were flagged with the evoked peak on the POSITIVE side, when the true evoked peak is the inhibition (dip). Using `abs()` let positive bumps through; the sign fix restricts detection to genuine inhibition.
**Next:** none — verify on AL_0041 when its per-amp affect is next reviewed.

### 2026-07-06 — ols_pixel_predictor_wip: drop §17 ipsi-mirror prior; add-back now dip-safety-ordered; PIXVIEW constant y-lims
**Changed/Found:** `impulse-analysis/ols_pixel_predictor_wip.m` — (1) removed the ipsi-mirror geometry + mirror-weighted removal + mirror marker from §17; the greedy is plain "drop the biggest dip-driver". (2) The forward add-back now re-adds clean px ordered by `abs(evDip)` ASCENDING (most dip-safe first) instead of by mirror distance / univariate predictivity — this fixed a 4.9 V degeneracy (was keep=2, nonDipR²=−0.30; now keep=33, nonDipR²=0.65). (3) PIXVIEW (§16) now uses CONSTANT y-limits (full mResp min/max) so traces are comparable across clicks and deep dips do not clip. AL_0033: median 97% dip in residual, spont R² 0.987→0.871, median NON-dip R² 0.818.
**Why:** User: the mirror wording was a mistake — no explicit homotopic prior is needed because the sparse spont OLS already concentrates weight on the contra region most predictive of the ipsi site. Ordering add-back by univariate predictivity tried dip-driving px first and starved the high-amp fit; dip-safety ordering lets many dip-neutral px back in and lifts the non-dip prediction. PIXVIEW needed fixed axes for visual consistency.

### 2026-07-06 — ols_pixel_predictor_wip: remove §18 BESTPRED (no best-pred on stim)
**Changed/Found:** `impulse-analysis/ols_pixel_predictor_wip.m` — deleted §18 [BESTPRED] entirely (the per-amp trial-CV ridge that fit contra→ipsi directly on stim trials, capturing 77–95% of the dip) and the `BESTPRED` output struct; renumbered §19→§18; fixed the header dip-window note (removed BESTPRED reference).
**Why:** User: do NOT want any best/ceiling predictor deployed on the stim response — only the stim-BLIND predictor (§17) belongs in this script. BESTPRED's job (predict the dip as well as possible) is the opposite of the paper's claim (isolate the ipsi-unique local effect), so keeping it invited the wrong read.

### 2026-07-06 — ols_pixel_predictor_wip: §17 greedy gets an ipsi-mirror prior + add-back + non-dip R²
**Changed/Found:** `impulse-analysis/ols_pixel_predictor_wip.m` §17 — made the greedy stim-blind selection "natural": (1) compute the ipsi-MIRROR point (reflect the ipsi site across the hemispheric midline = mean of the two mask col-centroids); (2) in the greedy dip-removal, weight the removal score by mirror distance (`score = contrib .* dMirN.^mirror_pow`, mirror_pow=1.5) so FAR-from-mirror dip-drivers are dropped first and near-mirror clean px are protected; (3) after the dip is killed, a forward ADD-BACK pass re-adds the nearest-mirror clean px that don't reintroduce the dip; (4) report a new NON-dip-window R² (Global vs Actual fit OUTSIDE the stim dip) + median kept-px mirror distance; mark the mirror (magenta ×) on the per-amp pixel maps. AL_0033: median 98% dip in residual (unchanged), spont R² 0.993→0.870, **NON-dip R² 0.811**, kept sets now hug the medial contra edge nearest the mirror.
**Why:** User: choose sparsity so nearness to the ipsi mirror (the natural homotopic bilateral predictor) is prioritized while bled px stay excluded, and improve prediction OUTSIDE the stim window. The mirror sits just across the midline (site is near-midline), so near-mirror = homotopic-adjacent; the affected/dip px there are still dropped, and the add-back lifts overall (non-dip) fit without breaking dip-blindness.
**Next:** Consider a small FIR/lagged-mirror or common-mode regressor to push NON-dip R² higher at the weak amps (1.1 V 0.65, 4.9 V 0.61); tune mirror_pow if the medial arc is too thin.

### 2026-07-06 — ols_pixel_predictor_wip: §16 FARPIX positive population reclassified as likely non-specific
**Changed/Found:** `impulse-analysis/ols_pixel_predictor_wip.m` §16 [FARPIX] — relabeled the positive-sign population from "far INSTANTANEOUS optical bleed" to "far, likely NON-SPECIFIC (drift/selection)"; added peak-magnitude reporting and softened all figure titles/legends. Data (AL_0033): POSITIVE = 33 px, dist med 298, onset 0 ms, peak 343 ms, |peak| 1.04%; NEGATIVE (dip) = 56 px, dist 159, onset 86 ms, peak 143 ms, |peak| 1.75%. (Tried a per-pixel 0V-null overlap check but it compared trial-avg peaks against single-trial spread → uninformative 100%/100%; removed it.)
**Why:** User asked how the positive "bleed" is classified and how true it is. It is split purely by SIGN of the early peak among px passing max-over-9-amps |z|>2 (a selection step that inflates false positives). The positive population is far, ~60% of the dip amplitude, and slow/late (peak 343 ms) — the opposite of true sensor bleed (same-frame ~0 ms, power-scaling). So it is not a confirmed second biological effect; only the near-site delayed NEGATIVE dip is treated as real coupling (matches the §14 header note).
**Next:** If a bleed control is ever needed, test power-scaling + same-frame kinetics explicitly; otherwise treat positive px as non-effect (already excluded from §17 which targets the dip directly).

### 2026-07-06 — ols_pixel_predictor_wip: §10 matched-filter window/threshold fix (dip-only template)
**Changed/Found:** `impulse-analysis/ols_pixel_predictor_wip.m` §10 — reworked the matched-filter template/window after the first version under-detected (0% affected at 0.5–3.2 V on AL_0033 vs BLEEDCHAR 57–62 / FARPIX 40 near-dip px). Iterated three variants: (a) strong-amp template + fixed window → still 0% at low amp; (b) per-amp template + per-amp `>20%-of-peak` window → UNSTABLE (noisy weak-amp template's window sprawled to ~1000 ms → spurious 65% at 0.5 V); (c) FINAL = one canonical template built from the strong-half amps AVERAGED, restricted to the **inhibition (dip) window only** (onset→clean-template recovery, 286 ms here), applied at every amp. Lowered `impulse_z` 3.0→2.0, added shape gate `|corr|>0.40`. Result: 0.5 V 0% · 1.1 V 24 px · 1.6 V 19 · 3.2 V 73 · 3.7 V 322 · 4.3 V 403 · 4.9 V 369 (med |corr| 0.79–0.95); the 1.1 V hits are exactly FARPIX's near-site neural-dip cluster.
**Why:** The rebound is amplitude-DEPENDENT (height 0.40 at 1.1 V → 1.19 at 4.9 V per §15), so a dip+rebound template decorrelates from low/mid-amp dip pixels (dip present, little rebound) → they failed the corr gate → detector read 0% despite a clear dip. The DIP is the amplitude-invariant neural-impulse signature (trough ~143 ms at every amp), so a dip-only template detects the impulse across amps and stays monotonic-trending + near-site. Mid-amp zeros at 2.1/2.7 V mirror BLEEDCHAR's own zeros there (a session-noise property, not a detector bug). Greedy (§17, task 3) is unaffected: median 98% dip captured in residual.
**Next:** Confirm on an AL_0041 session (0-amp catch fallback path) that the dip-only template + recovery window behave; consider whether the 2.1/2.7 V zeros warrant a per-amp z-null tweighting. Then fold the WIP repairs back into the canonical `ols_pixel_predictor.m`.

### 2026-07-06 — ols_pixel_predictor_wip: §10 affect metric → matched filter to the impulse shape
**Changed/Found:** `impulse-analysis/ols_pixel_predictor_wip.m` §10 — replaced the directional window-mean-vs-0V-null affect test with a MATCHED FILTER to the stim impulse shape. Template = primary-pixel evoked at the strongest amp, demeaned + unit-norm over [onset→settle]; per pixel/amp score s = <demeaned pixel evoked, template>; significance z vs a per-pixel size-matched 0V bootstrap of the same projection; affected if |z|>impulse_z (3.0). Also reports cosine-to-template (shape match). Removed now-unused `z_thr`.
**Why:** User: the window-mean-deviation metric was flawed — it flagged non-stim slow wander (false positives) and missed biphasic impulses whose signed mean cancels (false negatives). Demeaning the template kills DC/slow-drift projection (removes false positives) and using the whole dip+rebound waveform coherently catches the real impulses (removes false negatives) — i.e. it tests whether the pixel showed an impulse-LIKE response, not just any deviation. Aligns with the user's view that the positive/non-impulse population is a non-effect.
**Next:** Live-run and click pixels in §16b to confirm the matched filter flags the dip-shaped (neural co-suppression) pixels and ignores wander; tune impulse_z if over/under-inclusive. Then re-check §17 greedy start-set (~affected).
