Left panel = contra grid on the brain; click a pixel → right panel updates IN PLACE (cla+redraw, same window) with that pixel's per-amp trial-averaged evoked response (mResp, re 0 V, one line per amp) over the 0 V null envelope (95% band from a size-matched 0 V bootstrap of that pixel) + vertical marks at median recovery (end-inhibition) and settle (end-rebound). New local fns `pixview_click`/`pixview_show`.
**Why:** User wants to inspect, before fitting, whether contra pixels actually carry the stim dip/rebound and where the critical windows sit — a feasibility check for the greedy stim-blind (a pixel staying inside the null band is a stim-blind predictor candidate; one leaving it inside the windows is stim-coupled).
**Next:** Live-run to click through pixels; confirms whether a usable stim-blind set is plausible before trusting §17's %captured. Static analyzer clean.

### 2026-07-06 — ols_pixel_predictor_wip: rebuilt tasks 2 & 3 (directional affect map + greedy stim-blind)
**Changed:** `impulse-analysis/ols_pixel_predictor_wip.m` (WIP repair copy). TASK 1 (§8 interactive session-picker best-predictor) confirmed already correct (elastic-net "blob" weights, edgeMargin drops corner/boundary px, green+ at data-derived crater, primary pixel LOCKED via §3 cp_stim_site cache) — no change. TASK 2 (§10) reworked from abs-peak-over-[0,1s] to a **directional** test: signed evoked window means (re 0 V) tested vs a size-matched 0 V bootstrap null over **data-driven** INHIBITION [onset→recovery] and REBOUND [recovery→settle] windows (landmarks per amp from the primary-pixel impulse, no hardcoded window); pixel affected if |z|>1.64 in either phase; outputs `affected` [nG×nA] + per-amp %inh/%reb/%affected. TASK 3 (§17) replaced with **greedy pixel selection**: start from stim-UNAFFECTED px, greedily remove the pixel contributing most to the predicted per-amp dip, refit spont OLS, repeat until predicted dip ≤5% of start; report Actual/Global(≈0)/Local(residual), %dip-captured, and spont R² full→blind (cost of blinding). §19 batch gated OFF (its engine still uses the old drop-bled method).
**Why:** User directive + diagnosis that the prior per-amp drop-bled stim-blind did the OPPOSITE of intent (network-correlated clean px still predict the dip). Greedy targets the predicted dip DIRECTLY so, by construction, Global≈0 and residual carries ~100% of the dip; the spont-R² full→blind readout shows whether that's achievable at low cost (the real empirical question). Directional + data-driven windows per task-2 spec (capture full inhibition then the rebound, ~770 ms).
**Next:** Live-run (needs load_experiments) to read %dip-captured (target ~100%) and spont-R² cost per amp, and confirm greedy converges (may be slow at nGrid=500 — lower if needed). Then: treat FARPIX positive pop as non-effect; repoint §19 to greedy; user's "second" instruction was cut off — ask.

### 2026-07-06 — Sweep temp/Editor path guard across remaining cp_/impulse scripts
**Changed/Found:** `lds_ipsi.m`, `cp_state_batch.m`, `cp_doseresponse.m` — applied the same `startsWith(path,tempdir)` + `which()` fallback used in `contra_prediction.m`/`cp_residual_core.m`. `lds_ipsi` resolved `dataDir` straight from unguarded `mfilename` (real cache bug); `cp_state_batch`/`cp_doseresponse` used `bp=mfilename/..` to drive `paperExport`, which would have written PNGs into a temp `paper/images/`. Audited the rest: `contra_prediction.m` §1 and `cp_residual_core.m` already had the guard; `cp_agl/cp_bleed_control/cp_cont_state/cp_spont_predq/cp_hemo_ablation` don't self-resolve paths (inherit `dataDir` from caller); `run_all.m` + `package_for_collab.m` use `mfilename` only for `addpath`/`run()` and are run whole (F5), so temp-staging isn't a failure mode there — left unchanged.
**Why:** Same root cause as the prior entry — section-by-section runs stage a temp copy under `%TEMP%\Editor_*`, so any script deriving data/paper paths from bare `mfilename` silently reads/writes the wrong dir.
**Next:** None outstanding; static analysis clean on all three (only pre-existing semicolon infos).

### 2026-07-06 — Harden data-path resolution against MATLAB temp/Editor copies
**Changed/Found:** `ols_pixel_predictor.m` §1 + `load_experiments.m` — running a *section* (Ctrl+Enter) or evaluating a selection makes MATLAB execute a temp copy under `%TEMP%\Editor_*`, so `mfilename('fullpath')` points there; combined with a temp `pwd`, path resolution fell through to `impulseDir = here` and produced bogus loads like `...\Editor_tsxbn\data\cp_roi2_AL_0033_0129_e1.mat`. Fix: (1) in ols_pixel_predictor, discard `here` when it contains `tempdir` or `Editor_`, add an absolute `knownDir` anchor as the last candidate, and prefer `knownDir` in the empty-fallback branch. (2) in load_experiments, the line-22 recompute now prefers `which('load_experiments')` (finds the on-path file, not the temp copy) and only falls back to `mfilename` when it is not a temp path — previously it clobbered the good `which()`-based `impulseDir` from line 10. (3) changed the line-192 `save(fullfile('data',...))` relative path to `fullfile(impulseDir,'data',...)` so stim_vars never writes into a temp `data/`.
**Why:** Section-by-section runs intermittently loaded/saved caches from a throwaway temp dir instead of the project `data/`, silently using stale or missing files.
**Next:** If other `cp_*.m` scripts show the same symptom, apply the same `tempdir`/`Editor_` guard; verified here via a simulated worst case (pwd=temp, here=temp Editor path) → resolved to the real data dir and found the ROI file.

### 2026-07-06 — Orientation-normalize ALL brain-map overlays → ols_pixel_predictor is session-safe
**Changed/Found:** `ols_pixel_predictor.m` — introduced ONE shared display transform `Torient = pick_orient(contra_mask,ipsi_mask,px_prim,py_prim,nY_cp,nX_cp)` computed once before §8, with precomputed display coords `dspGr/dspGc` (grid), `dspSr/dspSc` (site) and oriented image `dspImg`. Routed every brain-map overlay through it, replacing the hardcoded `mimg_cp.'` + `scatter(grR,grC)`: §8 (kernel viewer), §10 (per-amp bleed maps + `bleed_click` + `bleed_detail` spatial map), §13 (STIMBLIND combo + CLEANMAX combo incl. radial circle), §14 (BLEEDCHAR z-matrix map + `bleedchar_click`), §16 (FARPIX map). Click handlers now match `CurrentPoint` against display coords (`gdC/gdR`) instead of original `grR/grC`. Also normalized the headless §19 path: `local_stimblind_session` now computes a per-session `Tor` and stores `BC.gdR/gdC/sdR/sdC` + oriented `BC.gimg`; `local_bleedchar_session` draws with those and stores them in its click struct. Verified: full run clean; AL_0033 main-path maps unchanged (pick_orient == the old transpose there, user-confirmed "perfect"); AL_0041 normalized to vertical-midline / ipsi-right (proven earlier + §19 loop ran error-free with the new fields).
**Why:** User wants to run the script on ANY session (selExp / selExp_override) and get upright maps. Previously only AL_0033 rendered correctly; AL_0041's ~90°-rotated camera frame made every static overlay transposed. One shared transform keeps all overlays consistent and correct per session.
**Next:** Only the §16 FARPIX population-trace subplot reuses the variable name `mn` (mouse name) as a trace mean — pre-existing shadowing, harmless (mn not used after §16), leave unless it bites. Consider a quick selExp_override=1 end-to-end run if you want to eyeball AL_0041's main-path bleed maps directly.

**Changed/Found:** `ols_pixel_predictor.m` §8 + `ols_click` — the interactive kernel viewer HARDCODED `image(mimg_cp.')` with `scatter(grR,grC)`. That transpose equals `pick_orient`'s choice for AL_0033 (`rot90(fliplr)`) so the overlay was correct there (verified: grid dots fill the contra mask exactly), but for AL_0041 — whose camera frame is stored ~90° rotated — the hardcoded transpose gives a HORIZONTAL midline (contra top / ipsi bottom), i.e. the "transposed" view the user reported. Fixed by using the same orientation machinery as the §20 viewer: `T=pick_orient(contra_mask,ipsi_mask,px_prim,py_prim,nY_cp,nX_cp)`, display `T.imgOp(mimg_cp)`, place grid/site via `orient_fwd`, and invert clicks via `orient_inv` in `ols_click`. Refit stays in ORIGINAL coords (orientation-independent). Proof render (OLD vs NEW, both sessions): AL_0033 unchanged (ipsi right, vertical midline); AL_0041 OLD horizontal-midline → NEW vertical-midline ipsi-right, matching AL_0033.

Normalize them the same way if/when AL_0041 static maps are needed.

### 2026-07-05 — §20 session-viewer slowness = 17 s SVD disk read per switch; fixed by memoizing
**Changed/Found:** `ols_pixel_predictor.m` §20 — profiled the interactive session viewer after a "taking too long" report. The per-click lasso refit is fast (44 ms) and the orientation math is negligible; the cost is `local_load_session`, which re-reads the full 500-component SVD from the server on EVERY session switch: `loadUVt` = 13.4 s + `loadData` = 3.3 s ≈ **17 s per switch** (pure disk I/O). Fix: added a `containers.Map` cache in `local_session_viewer`/`viewer_load` keyed by `sel` — first visit to a session pays the ~17 s read, every switch BACK is instant (reuses cached D/px/py/T). Verified the memoize pattern (isKey miss→hit, struct round-trip, handle persistence across the guidata struct copy).
**Why:** User reported the viewer/pixel-selector was slow. Needed to confirm whether it was the refit (compute) or the loader (I/O) before optimizing — it is entirely the SVD disk read, which repeats needlessly when toggling between the same few sessions.
**Next:** First-visit 17 s is inherent to the locked 500-SV load (not reduced). Memory note: cache holds each session's Uflat[313600×500]+V per entry (~0.7 GB each) → ~2 GB for the 3 viewer sessions; cap the cache if selList ever grows large. Optionally reduce nSV for the viewer only (display fidelity) if first-visit latency still bothers.

**Changed/Found:** `ols_pixel_predictor.m` §19 loader (grid section) — `grR`/`grC` were computed from `gridIdx` BEFORE the edge-margin `keep` filter but never re-indexed by `keep`, while `gridIdx`/`nG` were. Harmless until now because the stim-blind path indexes only via `gridIdx`; surfaced as `Index exceeds 442` the moment the new `local_bleedchar_session` used `BC.grR/grC` (length = pre-filter count) to scatter `nG`-length data. ### 2026-07-05 — Floor stim-blind null span to the dip window → AL_0041 e1 fixed (100% Local)
**Changed/Found:** `ols_pixel_predictor.m` §17 and §19 — the coupling-subspace null now spans `null_win_s = max(couple_win_s, dip_win_s)` instead of `couple_win_s` alone (added `null_win_s`, threaded through prints and the AGL/S structs). Root cause of AL_0041 e1's earlier 41–216% Local overshoot: its measured coupling window (~143–171 ms, genuinely short because its stim tops out at 2.70 V — coupling span is amplitude-graded and AL_0033 only reaches 629 ms via its 3.7–4.9 V amps) was SHORTER than the 300 ms dip window over which Global/Local are reported, so the (couple..dip) dip tail sat outside the null subspace and leaked back into Global sign-flipped (Global went positive while Actual was negative). Flooring the null to ≥300 ms nulls the full reported dip. Combined run now: AL_0033 e1 100% Local (couple 600, null 600), AL_0041 e1 **100% Local all 4 amps** (couple 143, null 300, 44 dirs, cost 0.022→0.041), AL_0041 e2 100% Local (couple 714, null 714). Confirmed AL_0041 e1 has 55 real 0 V catch trials (baseline was never the issue); e2 has none (pseudo-catch) yet is clean.
**Why:** User asked to analyze/redefine the AL_0041 coupling window. Decision (AskUserQuestion): "Floor null to dip window" — the null must cover at least the window over which the effect is quantified; makes §17/§19 consistent with the §13/§14 bleed labeling floor. User confirmed AL_0041 and AL_0033 voltages are physically comparable (same opsin/laser), so the amplitude-limited explanation holds across mice.
**Next:** Measured `couple_win_s` still varies ±1 bin run-to-run (bootstrap, 200 draws in §19) — the floor makes the result robust regardless, but consider bumping §19 bootstrap draws to 300 (match §10b) for a stable printed coupling number. Next: give AL_0041 e1/e2 their own §13/§14 bleed pass.

### 2026-07-05 — §17/§19 stim-blind now nulls the FULL coupling subspace (point-5 satisfied)
**Changed/Found:** `ols_pixel_predictor.m` §17 [AGL-STIMBLIND] and §19 `local_stimblind_session` — replaced the single per-amp dip-mean null direction with a full coupling-subspace null. Build `Apatt` = contra evoked z-patterns at every bin over the session coupling window (from §10b, predicted-evoked test) across all amps, `Ae=orth(Apatt)`, and project it out in closed form `b_con = b_ols - GiA*(pinv(Ae'*GiA)*(Ae'*b_ols))`. On AL_0033 e1: 198 directions nulled, spont held-out R² 0.9962→0.799 (cost 0.197), Global(contra) evoked ≈0 and Local(residual)=100% of the stim dip at every one of 9 amps; `max |Global evoked|` over the 629 ms window = 0.0000. §19 combined run: AL_0033 e1 100% Local (9/9), AL_0041 e2 100% Local (6/6), AL_0041 e1 only partial (couple window just 171 ms → 24 null-dirs → Global not fully zeroed, 41–216% Local).
**Why:** User pipeline step 5 — reject any stim-blind model that does not maximize the residual's capacity to carry the stim effect. The old dip-mean null zeroed only the unipolar 0–200 ms dip, leaving the biphasic rebound in Global; nulling the whole measured coupling trajectory forces the entire stim effect into the Local residual.
**Next:** AL_0041 e1's short (171 ms) coupling window is a data property of that session, not a bug — flag to Nick that its Global is not fully nulled; consider whether a per-session minimum-window floor is warranted.

### 2026-07-05 — §13/§14 bleed labeling switched to ABSOLUTE energy over the coupling window
**Changed/Found:** `ols_pixel_predictor.m` §13 [CLEANMAX-RADIAL] and §14 [BLEEDCHAR] — the bleed-classification statistic changed from signed mean evoked over the 0–200 ms dip window to `mean_t |amp−0V|` (absolute energy) integrated over the full session coupling window (`max(couple_win_s,dip_win_s)`, 629 ms this session), with a matching 0V-bootstrap null and a one-sided z test (`z>bc_z_thr`). §13's `evDip`/`actualDip` (the signed dip we cancel) are left unchanged. Effect on AL_0033 e1: §13 flags 49/442 bled (was 210) and radial-cancels them at negligible cost (R² 0.996→0.991, predicted dip −0.913→−0.916); §14 detail/colorbar/labels updated to "coupling energy".
**Why:** The ipsi→contra coupling is biphasic (dip then rebound); a signed dip-mean cancels the two lobes and under-/mis-counts bleed at high amps — the exact failure mode the user said "hurt us in our previous analysis." Identifying ALL coupling (via the §10b predicted-evoked window) BEFORE labeling bleed is user pipeline step 4.
**Next:** Confirm the tighter 49-px bleed set holds up on AL_0041 e1/e2 (different coupling windows) when those sessions get their own §13/§14 pass.

### 2026-07-05 — Session 1/2 "transpose" is camera orientation, NOT an indexing bug
**Changed/Found:** Diagnostic (overlaid detected site vs params.pixel + contra/ipsi masks per session): the apparent transposition of AL_0041 e1/e2 vs AL_0033 is a DISPLAY-ORIENTATION difference, not a correctness bug. AL_0033's camera frame has a vertical midline; both AL_0041 sessions are stored ~90° rotated (horizontal midline). Three checks confirm no row/col swap: (1) detected site sits adjacent to params.pixel in every session, (2) contra & ipsi masks always straddle that session's own midline with the site inside ipsi, (3) held-out R²≈0.99 in all three (impossible if grid/target were transposed relative to each other). params.pixel is a fixed default [380 320] for all three (not session-specific), so only the data-derived site is meaningful. The script's hardcoded `mimg.'` display convention matches AL_0033 but renders AL_0041 rotated — purely cosmetic; click-selection still works (guards on ipsi(row,col)).

### 2026-07-05 — ols_pixel_predictor §10b [COUPLING-WINDOW]: data-driven coupling extent before bleed labeling
**Changed/Found:** `impulse-analysis/ols_pixel_predictor.m` — added §10b (runs after §10, before the §11+ bleed/stim-blind steps) that measures the full temporal extent of ipsi↔contra coupling via the stim-locked structure in the FULL contra→ipsi OLS PREDICTION of the ipsi site (the exact signal §17 nulls), vs a size-matched 0V-window predicted-evoked null (95th pctile). Metric is far more sensitive than a raw contra-population average (which sat below the 0V null and gave noise). AL_0033 result: coupling extent scales monotonically with amplitude — 0.50V→0 ms (no effect), 1.1–2.7V→143–200 ms (dip only), 3.2–4.9V→514–571 ms (dip PLUS rebound). Session coupling window = 571 ms, well beyond dip_win_s=300 ms → confirms the fixed window cuts high-amp coupling short (the user-flagged problem). peak|pred| scales 0.66→3.9 with amp. Outputs `COUPLING` struct + per-amp figure. Also FOUND + fixed two false starts: (1) first metric (RMS over all 442 contra px) diluted coupling below null — replaced with predicted-evoked; (2) initially overwrote dip_win_s with couple_win_s — WRONG: the coupling trace is BIPHASIC (dip then rebound), so a signed-mean over the wide window cancels and broke CLEANFIT residuals + BESTPRED dipCap (went to −178%) + inflated §17 cost drop. Reverted; couple_win_s is kept SEPARATE from the unipolar dip_win_s.
**Why:** User: find the perfect stim-effect window capturing ALL ipsi↔contra coupling BEFORE the bleed-affected-pixel analysis (cutting coupling short is what corrupted the earlier bleed/neural split).
**Next:** Wire couple_win_s into the bleed-affected labeling as an ABSOLUTE-energy window (no biphasic cancellation) and upgrade §17 to a per-bin stim-blind null across the coupling window (nulls dip+rebound, maximizing residual stim-capture = user point 5). Both change bleed/stim-blind results, so confirm with user first.

### 2026-07-04 — DECISION (locked): STIM-BLIND (§17) is the PRIMARY predictor; BEST-PRED (§18) is SECONDARY
**Changed/Found:** `impulse-analysis/ols_pixel_predictor.m` — marked §17 [AGL-STIMBLIND] as ***PRIMARY*** (header updated) and codified BEST-PRED as §18 [BESTPRED] labelled ***SECONDARY***. Two contra→ipsi predictors now coexist with fixed roles: (§17 PRIMARY) spont-trained, stim-dip direction projected out → Global carries only ongoing global state, Local (residual) = FULL stim-evoked inhibition; (§18 SECONDARY) per-amp trial-CV ridge fit on stim trials → Global captures 77–95% of the dip (ceiling), Local = small ipsi-unique remainder. Static-clean.
**Why:** User decision. **The stim-blind decomposition reflects the TRUE TASK of the analysis — isolating the LOCAL-ONLY stim effect.** Making the contra predictor blind to the stim forces the residual to contain the full local inhibition (the thing we want to characterize/state-test), rather than letting genuine bilateral co-suppression absorb it. BEST-PRED answers a different question ("how much CAN contra explain?") and is kept only as a ceiling / robustness check on bilateral sharing.
**Next:** Re-run the residual STATE analysis on the PRIMARY (stim-blind) residual (the earlier motion-null result used the BEST-PRED residual) — that is the residual the paper claims about. Reconcile the two decompositions' labelling with contra_prediction.m's RRR Actual/Global/Local. Replicate on AL_0041.

### 2026-07-04 — RESID-STATE: local residual is MOTION-NULL (state-robust); variance "effect" is the signal-power confound (independent replication)
**Changed/Found:** `impulse-analysis/ols_pixel_predictor.m` (inline) — per-trial local residual (Actual − per-amp trial-CV ridge contra prediction) vs pre-stim brain state, z-within-amp, n=748 trials. RESULTS: **motion NULL** — signed dip ρ=−0.020 p=0.59, L1-dev ρ=+0.008 p=0.83, partial (|pre-var) ρ=−0.024, template-gain ρ=+0.024 → the local stim effect is state-ROBUST to motion. **Pre-variance: signed dip NULL (ρ=−0.033 p=0.37) but L1-dev ρ=+0.203 p=2.4e-8** — i.e. the variance "state-dependence" appears ONLY in the unsigned L1 metric = the SIGNAL-POWER CONFOUND, reproducing the retracted PreVar+0.213 finding (RESEARCH 2026-07-01/02) near-identically. Relative-δ weak (signed −0.073 p=0.046; L1 +0.115 p=0.002).
**Why:** User chose brain-state dependence as the residual analysis; connects to the paper's state-robustness claim and the var/δ retraction.
**Next:** Independent confirmation on a DIFFERENT predictor (direct-pixel per-amp CV ridge vs RRR `cp_residual_core`) → strengthens the motion-null headline. Same caveat: use SIGNED/directional DV, not L1-dev, to avoid the power confound. Motion untested = N/A here (have mv_z). Replicate on AL_0041. Consider codifying BESTPRED+RESID-STATE as §18/§19.

### 2026-07-04 — BESTPRED: per-amp trial-CV ridge contra→ipsi stim prediction (sets up residual analysis)
**Changed/Found:** `impulse-analysis/ols_pixel_predictor.m` (inline, live) — for each amplitude, fit the best contra→ipsi predictor of the peri-stim ipsi trace directly on that amp's stim trials, 5-fold CV **across trials** (ridge, per-amp λ from {1,10,100,1000}, evoked = per-trial pre-onset baselined). RESULT (AL_0033 e1): CV R² (single-trial) = **0.97–0.98 at every amp** — contra tracks the ipsi stim response tightly and trial-generalizably; best predictor captures **77–95% of the 0–300 ms dip** (Global). Local residual (Actual−Global) = 5–23%, absolute magnitude **grows with amplitude** (−0.04 @1.1V → −0.22 @4.9V, ρ=+0.75 p=0.026). This is the genuine shared-out decomposition (Local = what contra CANNOT predict), distinct from §17 stim-blind (Local=100% by construction).

**Next:** Residual (per-amp trial-avg Actual−bestPred) is the local-effect trace to analyze next — kinetics / spatial / state-dependence. Offer to codify BESTPRED as §18. λ hit the grid floor (=1 everywhere); could extend grid lower but CV R² already saturated ~0.98. Replicate on AL_0041.

### 2026-07-04 — DECISION + rework: §17 is now the STIM-BLIND Actual/Global/Local decomposition (residual = full stim effect)
**Changed/Found:** `impulse-analysis/ols_pixel_predictor.m` §17 renamed [CONSTRAINED]→[AGL-STIMBLIND] and reframed. User locked the decomposition goal: **contra prediction (Global) carries only the ongoing global brain state and predicts ZERO stim dip; the residual (Local) carries the FULL stim effect.** Implemented via the closed-form equality constraint (project the per-amp stim-dip direction out of the spont OLS weights). RESULT: Global dip = 0.000 at all 9 amps, Local = 100% of Actual, spont R² 0.996→0.949. Figure = Actual/Global/Local dip-vs-amp + 1.1 V & 4.9 V trace panels (Global flat through the trough, Local≡Actual). Output struct renamed `CONSTRAINED`→`AGL`. Static-clean.
**Why:** User insisted (3×) that "bleed-free" must mean residual=all stim effect, contra pred=none. Key clarification established first: this is NOT a bleed property — even bleed-free / stim-invariant-only, contra predicts 77–101% of the dip via REAL transcallosal co-suppression (§16), so predicting-no-dip must be IMPOSED. The stim-blind constraint does exactly that; it is a spontaneous-vs-stim-evoked split (everything stim-locked → Local), defensible as global-state nuisance regression.
**Next:** This makes Local=full dip by construction — the scientific content is now in the GLOBAL predictor (does contra spont coupling really carry no stim-locked component beyond the projected direction?) and in the spont R² cost (0.047). Reconcile with contra_prediction.m's RRR-based Actual/Global/Local (which does the shared-out split, giving a SMALL local). Two different decompositions coexist — label clearly in the paper. Replicate on AL_0041.

### 2026-07-04 — LOW-AMP is bleed-free (confirms Nick): contra predicts 101% of the 1.1 V ipsi dip, residual ≈ 0
**Changed/Found:** `impulse-analysis/ols_pixel_predictor.m` (inline, live workspace) — tested Nick's claim that bleed is power-dependent. RESULT: bleed ≈ 0 at 0.5 V (−0.160) and 1.1 V (−0.009, only 44% of far px positive), then switches ON at ≥1.6 V (+0.31→+0.62, 97–100% positive). So **0.5–1.1 V is the bleed-free regime — confirms Nick**. Deploying the spont-trained full-grid OLS predictor on stim: at bleed-free 1.1 V it predicts **101% of the ipsi 0–300 ms dip with a FLAT residual** (actual −0.908, pred −0.914); fraction-of-dip-predicted is if anything HIGHER at bleed-free amps (97%) than bleed-present (94%) — so bleed does NOT inflate the prediction. ⇒ at low amp the ipsi inhibition is essentially FULLY explained by genuine bilateral contra coupling (Global); no bleed, and (with the full grid) no local-only residual.

(3) Offer to codify as §18 [LOWAMP]. (4) Replicate on AL_0041.

### 2026-07-04 — CORRECTION: flagged contra px are TWO populations, not "all bleed" (dip-energy |z| is not bleed-specific)
**Changed/Found:** `impulse-analysis/ols_pixel_predictor.m` §16 [FARPIX] rewritten (robust: strong px |z|>2 at each px's best amp, early 0–400 ms window, split by sign). RESULT on AL_0033 e1: 57 strong contra px split ~evenly — **28 NEGATIVE dips** (median dist 152 px = NEAR the callosal mirror, onset 29 ms = 1-frame delay, trough ~100 ms → neural transcallosal CO-SUPPRESSION) vs **29 POSITIVE** (median dist 291 px = FAR, onset 0 ms, peak 29 ms = instantaneous → bleed-like fast artifact). Distance separation ranksum p=9.6e-11. This CORRECTS the 2026-07-03 FARPIX entry ("all 70 responsive positive = bleed"), which was an artifact of a narrow amp-pool + 200 ms window that only selected far/positive px.
**Why:** User asked to characterize far-pixel onset/settling; the naive pooled selection was underpowered (3 px at 300 ms window) and the earlier all-positive claim did not survive a robust selection.
**Next:** IMPORTANT — the dip-energy |z| bleed metric (used by BLEED/CLEANMAX/BLEEDCHAR) is NOT bleed-specific: it flags near-site NEURAL dips together with far bleed, so |z|/radial removal discards genuine coupling. A bleed-specific test needs SIGN(+) × LATENCY(instantaneous, 0-frame) × amp-SLOPE, not |dip energy|. Note the far-positive's spatial profile (far>near) is atypical for laser scatter — "bleed-like" is a hypothesis, not proven optical bleed. Consider adding a sign×latency bleed classifier.

### 2026-07-03 — CONSTRAINED predictor: spont R²=0.94 WITH zero stim dip is achievable (but a choice, not evidence)
**Changed/Found:** `impulse-analysis/ols_pixel_predictor.m` (inline analysis, ran end-to-end) — built equality-constrained least squares: max spontaneous fit s.t. deployed 0–200 ms predicted dip = 0 at every amplitude (project OLS weights onto null space of the per-amp deployed dip patterns, `b_con = b_ols - GinvA·pinv(A'GinvA)·A'b_ols`). RESULT: predicted dip forced to exactly 0.000 at all 9 amps, spont held-out R² 0.9962→0.9387 (**cost only 0.057**). Far cheaper than STIMBLIND greedy (which needed to drop 423/442 px, R²→0.744) because it removes only 9 directions, not pixels. KEY caveat: unconstrained OLS already predicts ~101% of the dip (−1.162 vs actual −1.152 @1.1V) and bleed-free CLEANMAX predicts 91% — so the dip is GENUINE contra→ipsi coupling, and zeroing it just re-assigns real network signal to the residual by fiat. The stim-dip subspace is low-variance (5.7% of spont R²) but highly predictable.
**Why:** User asked for strategies to train on spont and predict stim via a contra-pixel combination that does NOT predict the ipsi stim dip at all — and to run it and report if achievable.
**Next:** Report delivered. The defensible paper predictor is BLEED-FREE (removes ~9% optical bleed, keeps genuine coupling → residual=local effect), NOT dip-zeroed (discards genuine coupling). Offer to codify constrained-LS as a script section (17) if the dip-zeroed decomposition is wanted as a bound.

### 2026-07-03 — SETTLETIME RUN: 200 ms UNDER-includes; "200–300 ms = rebound" note is WRONG
**Changed/Found:** `impulse-analysis/ols_pixel_predictor.m` §15 — ran on AL_0033 e1. Primary-pixel trial-avg impulse landmarks: trough 114–143 ms (CONFIRMS locked ~114 ms), but median RECOVERY (return to baseline = end of inhibition) = **314 ms**, and rebound PEAK sits at ~500–600 ms. So 0–200 ms cuts the inhibition off mid-recovery (under-includes by ~114 ms), and there is NO rebound at 200–300 ms (that region is still inhibition). Recovery is amp-graded and inverted: high amp recovers faster (229 ms @4.9 V) than low (457 ms @1.1 V).

### 2026-07-03 — SETTLETIME (S15): data-driven inhibition window vs eyeballed 200 ms
**Changed/Found:** `impulse-analysis/ols_pixel_predictor.m` — added section (15) `[SETTLETIME]`. Instead of assuming the 0–200 ms inhibition/dip window, it measures it: per amplitude it takes the trial-averaged impulse at the PRIMARY (ipsi) pixel (0V-catch subtracted, pre-onset baselined) and extracts landmarks — trough (peak inhibition, time+depth), RECOVERY (first return to baseline within a ±max(2·baselineSD, 5%·|trough|) band = honest end-of-inhibition), REBOUND peak (positive overshoot after recovery), and full SETTLE (±10%·|trough| band). Prints a per-amp table + median recovery time and flags whether 200 ms OVER-includes (reaches into the rebound), UNDER-includes, or matches. Figure: left = overlaid per-amp impulses with trough(▽)/rebound(△) markers, 200 ms (red) and median-recovery (green) verticals; right = landmark time vs amplitude (trough/recovery/rebound/settle) against the 200 ms line. Outputs `SETTLE` struct. Static-checked clean (only 4 pre-existing warnings).
**Why:** User: 200 ms is an eyeball for when the impulse settles, and there's a post-inhibition rebound (signal diving up) — the window edge should be quantified from the per-amp trial-averaged settling/recovery time so the inhibition-energy window doesn't bleed into the rebound lobe.
**Next:** Run in MATLAB after section (10); read median recovery vs 200 ms. If recovery < 185 ms consistently, tighten the dip window to the recovery time everywhere it's used (BLEED/CLEANFIT/STIMBLIND/CLEANMAX/BLEEDCHAR all hardcode `round(0.2*Fs)`), and reconsider the locked "0–200 ms, rebound 200–300 ms" note in impulse-analysis/CLAUDE.md.

### 2026-07-03 — First 4-mode sine-tracking result (AL_0048 2026-07-01/6, right/inhib)

### 2026-07-02 — ols_pixel_predictor.m: [CLEANMAX] (S13) → sensitive + RADIAL bleed cancellation
**Changed/Found:** `impulse-analysis/ols_pixel_predictor.m` reworked section (13) from a flat threshold sweep into [CLEANMAX-RADIAL]. (1) MORE SENSITIVE bleed test: integrated 0–200 ms evoked ENERGY (signed dip) z-scored against a 0V bootstrap null (SD-based `zBleed`, recomputed from `blk0` for the 1.1 V trial count), flagged if `|z| > bleed_z_thr` (default 1.28 ≈ 90%; LOWER = more sensitive) — replaces the hard 95th-pctile peak test. (2) RADIAL cancellation: bled pixels ordered by distance from the primary/laser pixel and cancelled nearest-first (expanding radius), refitting OLS on leftovers each step; knee = smallest radius that drops the predicted dip ≤10% of full. New figures: Fig 0 radial bleed profile (|z| vs distance + threshold + cancel radius), Fig 1 radial sweep (R² & predicted dip vs cancel radius), Fig 2 bleed-free predictor at knee, Fig 3 combo map with the cancel-radius ring drawn around the site. `CLEANMAX` struct now carries `zBleed/bled/dist/bledOrd/swpRad/radSel`.
**Why:** user: "increase bleed sensitivity, moreover the bleed is conventionally radially outward from the laser point, so sweep for cancelling bled pixels should be in a pattern away from primary." Energy+z is more sensitive than peak-vs-95th; radial ordering matches the physical falloff of the stim artifact from the laser point.
**Next:** user runs in MATLAB — read Fig 0 to confirm bleed |z| decays with distance (radial), and Fig 1 to see whether cancelling the near radial core removes the predicted dip cheaply (bleed) or only with R² cost (network). ### 2026-07-02 — ols_pixel_predictor.m: [CLEANMAX] (S13) discard 1.1 V bled pixels, sweep bleed-free accuracy
**Changed/Found:** `impulse-analysis/ols_pixel_predictor.m` new section (13) [CLEANMAX] — reverse of [STIMBLIND]. At `clean_amp=1.1` V, discards only the stim-affected (bled) pixels and fits OLS on the leftover clean pixels (Gram solve, reuses section-12 `Gz/cz` if present). SWEEPS the bleed threshold `tf` (Inf→0.3 on the 0V-baseline ratio `stimDev./nullThr`), recording held-out spont R² + predicted 1.1 V dip + #discarded at each. Full grid (tf=Inf) = R² ceiling; picks the "knee" = max discard with R² ≥ ceiling−0.01. Three figures: (1) sweep tradeoff (R² & predicted dip vs #discarded, knee + ceiling marked), (2) bleed-free predictor on 1.1 V (actual/pred/residual) at the knee, (3) kept (weight-colored) vs discarded (black) combo map. Console prints the full sweep table. Output struct `CLEANMAX`.
**Why:** user asked to "reverse" the diagnostic — find all bled pixels at 1.1 V, discard them, fit OLS on leftovers to maximize prediction accuracy; user chose to SWEEP the bleed strictness rather than fix one threshold. Honest framing: the full grid is the R² ceiling (bleed px can only help spont prediction), so the sweep quantifies how much (if any) accuracy is sacrificed for bleed-freeness. R² barely dropping while discarding all detectable bleed ⇒ bled px redundant ⇒ the 1.1 V predicted dip is genuine network coupling.
**Next:** user runs in MATLAB and reads the sweep — check the R² sacrifice at the knee (near 0 = bleed removable for free) and how much of the actual dip the bleed-free predictor still captures (network vs bleed). Optionally generalize the knee pixel set across amps.

### 2026-07-02 — ols_pixel_predictor.m: [STIMBLIND] (S12) greedy pixel-strip diagnostic @ 1.1 V
**Changed/Found:** `impulse-analysis/ols_pixel_predictor.m` new section (12) [STIMBLIND] — at `blind_amp=1.1` V, starts from the full contra grid and greedily removes the pixel contributing most to the PREDICTED peri-stim dip (`contrib = b .* evDip`, sum = predicted 0–200 ms dip), refitting the spont OLS via a precomputed Gram (`Gz=Ztr'Ztr`, tiny ridge) each step. Records predicted 1.1 V dip + held-out spont R² vs #pixels removed; "stim-blind" = predicted dip ≤5% of initial. Three figures: (1) tradeoff curve (predicted dip & spont R² vs #removed, stim-blind point marked), (2) stim-blind predictor on 1.1 V trials (actual / full pred dashed / stim-blind pred solid / residual), (3) surviving pixel COMBO map (removed=black, kept=weight-colored, site marked). Output struct `STIMBLIND`.
**Why:** user asked to "use diagnostic on 1.1 V amp to figure out what combo of pixels can be used for prediction so that no stim effect is seen in the prediction." Distinguishes network co-suppression (dip only removable by destroying spont R² ⇒ genuine, distributed) from bleed (a few pixels carry the whole predicted dip at little R² cost ⇒ stim-driven).
**Next:** user runs in MATLAB and reads the tradeoff curve — if R² collapses alongside the predicted dip, the contra dip is distributed network coupling (not removable bleed); if the dip zeros out cheaply, inspect the removed pixels (STIMBLIND.removedOrder) as the stim-driven set. Optionally repeat across amps to see if the same combo generalizes.

### 2026-07-02 — ols_pixel_predictor.m: [CLEANFIT] (S11) bleed-free predictor deployed on stim
**Changed/Found:** `impulse-analysis/ols_pixel_predictor.m` new section (11) [CLEANFIT] — drops every grid pixel flagged at ANY amplitude (`clean = ~any(affected,2)`), refits the contemporaneous contra→ipsi OLS on spontaneous data with only the clean pixels (reuses section-7 `Ztr/Zte/mu_p/sd_p`, same 2/3–1/3 split), reports spont held-out R² full-grid vs clean-grid, then DEPLOYS the clean predictor on peri-stim windows. Per-amp figure: actual ipsi trial-avg (black) / clean prediction = Global (red) / residual = actual−pred = Local (blue), all pre-onset baselined; console prints per-amp 0–200 ms dip for actual/pred/residual. Output struct `CLEANFIT`.
**Why:** user asked for "a predictor that switches out the blacked pixels and makes prediction on stim, show responses." Standalone analogue of contra_prediction `[CP-CLEAN]/[CP-CLEANRES]`: if dropping bleed pixels barely changes spont R² and the peri-stim residual dip survives, the contra coupling is network, not stim bleed.
**Next:** user runs in MATLAB — check (a) R²_full ≈ R²_clean (bleed px not carrying the prediction) and (b) residual dip persists per amp (Local effect not a bleed artifact); compare residual-dip magnitudes to the RRR `[CP-CLEANRES]` numbers (~73% of actual).

Added helper `local_pixtrials` (single-pixel pre-baselined peri-onset traces). `DB` now carries `Xpct`, per-amp `onFcell`, `onF0`, `preN`, brain image `gimg`, and `site`.

### 2026-07-02 — ols_pixel_predictor.m: [BLEED] baseline switched to 0 V catch trials + per-amp power diagnostics
**Changed/Found:** `impulse-analysis/ols_pixel_predictor.m` section (10) [BLEED] — replaced the random-spont matched null with the **amp-0 (0 V catch) trials** as baseline. Now evoked = peak |trialavg(amp a) − trialavg(0 V)| over [0,1s] (subtracts the command artifact common to all amps); null = 95th pctile of bootstrapped 0 V subsets (size = nTrials of amp a, minus the full 0 V mean, 200 draws). 0 V trials capped at `maxBaseTrl=500` (gap-fill amp-0 can be many). Added a per-amp diagnostic table printing `nTrl | medEvoked | medNull95 | %aff`. New local helpers `local_onsets`/`local_periavg`; `mResp` now stores the 0V-subtracted excess; inspector relabeled ("evoked − 0V baseline", shows n(amp) vs n(0V)); `DB` carries `m0`-derived `nT0`. Static check: only pre-existing cosmetic warnings; ends balanced.
**Why:** user: "there is a 0v data as well use that as baseline, help debug as 0.5 is having effect but higher amps not why." The 0 V catch trials are the proper no-stim baseline (remove shutter/galvo command artifacts a random-spont null misses). The counterintuitive "0.5 V flags, higher amps don't" is diagnosed by the table: the matched null threshold RISES as trial count FALLS (a size-n average is noisier), so a high amp with few trials needs a larger real effect to clear its own (higher) null — a statistical-power effect, not an amplitude reversal.
**Next:** user runs in MATLAB and reads the printed table — confirm whether high amps have fewer trials (→ higher medNull95, fewer %aff) i.e. power-limited, vs medEvoked actually not growing with amp (→ genuine). If power-limited, consider a trial-count-independent statistic (e.g. per-trial two-sample amp-vs-0V test, or fixed-n subsampling across amps).

### 2026-07-02 — ols_pixel_predictor.m: elastic net (ridge alone gives no sparsity)
**Changed/Found:** `impulse-analysis/ols_pixel_predictor.m` — clarified/fixed the sparsity confusion: ridge (L2) never zeros weights, so fit_mode='ridge' is dense at ANY value. Upgraded the lasso solver to ELASTIC NET: `cd_lasso` now takes `lam1` (L1) + `lam2` (L2), denom `dg+lam2`. In lasso mode the `ridge` knob = L2 as a fraction, scaled `lam2 = ridge*median(Gdiag)` (≈ridge·nTr) so it's interpretable in ~0..1. Set defaults fit_mode='lasso', l1_frac=0.1, ridge=0.2. Sparsity from l1_frac; ridge groups/stabilizes correlated neighbor px; debias unchanged.
**Why:** user tried ridge=0 and 1 expecting sparsity — L2 can't do that. Elastic net gives sparse + stable.
**Next:** tune l1_frac (sparsity) and ridge (grouping) to taste.

### 2026-07-02 — ols_pixel_predictor.m: stronger edge erosion (edgeMargin 5→12) for corner px
**Changed/Found:** `impulse-analysis/ols_pixel_predictor.m` — raised `edgeMargin` 5→12 so grid nodes within 12 px of the mask edge are dropped; corner/edge pixels are no longer in the grid so can't be weighted. Noted the deeper cause: at `l1_frac`=0.01 (near-OLS/dense) OLS assigns LARGE weights to low-SNR edge pixels; raising l1_frac zeros them first while debias keeps R² — the statistical alternative to the spatial guard.
**Why:** user still saw corner pixels weighted after edgeMargin=5; wants them gone.
**Next:** edgeMargin=12 also thins near internal mask holes → fewer grid px; lower if over-thinned. Or raise l1_frac to ~0.1-0.2 (debias preserves R²).

### 2026-07-02 — ols_pixel_predictor.m: running-mean detrend for best/worst windows
**Changed/Found:** `impulse-analysis/ols_pixel_predictor.m` — replaced the per-window constant DC correction with a running-mean (sliding) detrend: `p_corr = p + movmean(a-p, W)`, W = `detrendSec`(1.0s)/stride samples. Applied to both R² scoring and the plotted pred in the best/worst panel. Removes slow drift WITHIN a window (per-window constant only removed the window mean). Legend → "pred (run-mean corr)".
**Why:** user confirmed ("do") the running-mean upgrade offered last turn — worst windows still showed within-window drift under the constant correction.
**Next:** tune detrendSec (shorter=more drift removed but eats slow signal; longer→approaches per-window constant).

### 2026-07-02 — ols_pixel_predictor.m: erode edge/corner grid px; mean-correct best/worst

(2) best/worst windows now DC-re-aligned per window (`p = p - mean(p) + mean(a)`) for both R² scoring and the plotted pred — same mean-correction as CP-PREDQ; removes the mean-shift on worst windows (predictor has no per-window intercept). ### 2026-07-02 — ols_pixel_predictor.m: fix empty best/worst; add Lasso debias + tradeoff knob
**Changed/Found:** `impulse-analysis/ols_pixel_predictor.m` — (1) best/worst panel was EMPTY: when spont frames > maxFrm they're subsampled (linspace) so frame indices aren't consecutive; the old `diff==1` gap guard skipped every window. ### 2026-07-02 — [CP-PREDQ] on Zhiwen data: relative-δ predictability effect FLIPS SIGN (does not replicate)
**Changed/Found:** New `impulse-analysis/cp_zhiwen_predq.m` ports our [CP-PREDQ] (spontaneous contra→ipsi single-pixel predictability vs brain state) to Zhiwen's `AB_0004_20210330_1`. Model: predictor = LEFT sensory redoSVD modes (K=50), target = a representative RIGHT sensory pixel (median train-R²; selected on train, evaluated held-out). 400 random 3-s windows from the held-out test block. Target pixel test R²=0.918 (median window 0.905) — real dynamic range. Spearman ρ(local R², state): **Variance +0.74** (power-CONFOUND), **abs δ-power +0.34** (power-CONFOUND), **Relative-δ −0.25** (power-INDEP). Robustness over 150 sensory pixels: Variance median +0.77 (100% positive), abs-δ +0.28 (79% positive), **Relative-δ median −0.33 (99% NEGATIVE)**. Fig+mat → `impulse-analysis/data/cp_zhiwen_predq.{png,mat}`.
**Why:** Test whether our AL_0033 [CP-PREDQ] result (Variance +0.79 / abs-δ +0.67 = power-confounds; Relative-δ +0.29 = the one power-INDEPENDENT "genuine" effect) replicates on independent, cleaner, hemo-corrected data. Two methodological traps hit and fixed first: (1) predicting the hemisphere spatial-MEAN gives R²≈0.999 (global mode, no dynamic range) — must predict a single pixel; (2) predicting a K-dim target from K modes is near-degenerate (R²≈1) — target must live in the FULL 200-dim signal while predictor stays at K=50 (the rank asymmetry that makes prediction lossy, as in CP-HEMI).
**Next:** The variance/abs-δ CONFOUNDS replicate (expected — var is R²'s denominator). But the one power-INDEPENDENT effect (relative-δ +0.29 on AL_0033, "more predictable in synchronized states") **robustly INVERTS to −0.25/−0.33 on Zhiwen** ⇒ it is NOT a generalizable brain-state law — prep/task-specific or spurious. Combined with the earlier var/δ retraction, the predictability-vs-state story has NO power-independent component that survives cross-dataset replication. CAVEATS: single Zhiwen session; NO motion trace on his data (the cleanest AL_0033 claim = motion-null is untested here); passive-spontaneous vs our task/stim prep; his higher R² ceiling. Consider a 2nd Zhiwen session + partial-corr(relδ | var). Revisit any paper claim resting on relative-δ predictability.

### 2026-07-02 — CP-LOCAL: readable figure + data-driven explainer
**Changed/Found:** `impulse-analysis/contra_prediction.m` [CP-LOCAL] — replaced `paperFig(18,14)`+`FontSize 5` (unreadable paper-panel sizing) with a 1180×900 px screen figure, 11–16 pt fonts, color-coded Actual/Global/Local column headers, plain 200-dpi PNG export. Per-panel ρ/p titles REMOVED (user request) — stats now live only in the console table + explainer. ### 2026-07-02 — redoSVD.m root cause of the frame-976 step: PER-BATCH mean subtraction (not mode 1 / not truncation) — fixed to global demean
**Changed/Found:** `utils/redoSVD.m` — the Fs (Vnew) loop subtracted a PER-BATCH mean (`data-mean(data,2)`, imgbatchSize=975), while the Ua basis was built from a GLOBALLY demeaned `mov`. This injects a piecewise-constant offset into `Vnew` that jumps discontinuously at every batch boundary (frame 1+k·975). Definitive tests in the live workspace: (a) raw all-modes readout `zz_yr` is SMOOTH at 976 (Δ=−0.14) but BOTH redoSVD all-modes (`zz_yfa`, Δ=−4.78) and K=50 (`zz_yk`, Δ=−4.55) step → it is a redoSVD bug, NOT truncation (all-modes would match raw if lossless-in-offset). (b) 35/35 detector jumps land EXACTLY on `mod(f−1,975)==0`. (c) Reproduced the step additively: rawstep(−0.140) + batch-mean-offset-jump(−4.665) = −4.805 ≈ observed. (d) Mode 1 is NOT degenerate (sv1/sv2=3.7) — not an eigenvector rotation. **Broader impact:** rms(deployed `zz_yk` − raw readout `zz_yr`) = 1.37 %dF/F, |·|>1 on 41% of frames; 127/198 batch boundaries had |step|>1 — pervasive, not 46 isolated frames. **Fix:** compute `globMean = U*mean(V,2)` once, subtract it in every batch. After fix: step at 976 = −0.139 (=raw), rms(fixed−raw)=0.0062 (220× better), boundaries |step|>1 drop 127→45 (the 45 are genuine signal, present in raw too). Verified end-to-end by re-running the edited redoSVD on the contra mask.
**Why:** the [CP-PREDQ] guard (entry below) treated this as a rare <0.03% mode-1/truncation glitch and band-aided it downstream. Re-investigation with hard numbers shows it is a systematic per-batch DC artifact contaminating the predictor everywhere; the honest fix is at the source.
**Next:** RECOMPUTE the cached SVDs — delete `*_svdraw*.mat` (contra `_svdraw.mat` AND ipsi `_svdraw_ml_ipsi.mat`) and re-run contra_prediction S05/[CP-HEMI] so both `V_c_full` and `V_ipsi_full` are rebuilt clean. NOTE R² effect: kernel R² (vs REAL y_full — the deployed contra→ipsi readout) IMPROVES 0.847→0.885; pooled R² DROPS 0.937→0.874 because the old pooled score was inflated by a shared batch-artifact (corr(contra-step, ipsi-step)=0.979) that predicted itself — y_full itself is clean at boundaries (step 0.42≈overall). The [CP-PREDQ] discontinuity guard can be relaxed/removed once caches are rebuilt. Also: redoSVD is NOT frame-lossless regardless (Ua built from 39-frame-averaged mov, rank/averaging loss ~100 units both old and new) — that is by design, separate from this bug. User to review edit before commit (not committed).

### 2026-07-02 — [CP-PREDQ] prediction-discontinuity guard: rare redoSVD/truncation step artifact in the readout (not source data)
**Changed/Found:** the [CP-PREDQ] click-inspector surfaced a "jump" in a low-R² window (win 19, frame 976): a −4.55 %dF/F single-frame STEP in the contra prediction while the ipsi target was smooth (Δ=0.16). Diagnosed: (1) NOT a timestamp gap (t_full uniform 0.0285 s, 0 gaps); (2) NOT motion (z≈−0.2, flat); (3) NOT in the source — the raw contra-mean signal (mean contra pixel from V_cp) is smooth at 976 (|d|=0.94, z=0.1). The step lives in the redoSVD-reorthogonalized `V_c_full` **mode 1** (contributes −2.0 of −4.55) and leaks only into the K=50-truncated readout (compensated by higher modes in the full reconstruction). Scope: a per-frame matched detector (`|d yhat|>3 %dF/F & |d y|<1`) finds 46/193206 frames → 6/534 spontaneous windows; these are exactly the spurious very-negative-R² tail (win 19 R²=−4.39). **Fix:** added a prediction-discontinuity guard to `cp_spont_predq.m` (opts `disc_thr`=3, `targ_smooth`=1) that drops any window straddling such a frame. After guard: 530 windows, worst R² −4.39→−3.05 (the remaining −3.05 is a GENUINE low-SNR flat window: var 0.45 vs median 3.85, no step). Aggregate ρ shifts ≤0.005 (Motion −0.01, Variance +0.80, abs-δ +0.69, rel-δ +0.29 — conclusions unchanged).
**Why:** user spotted the jump in the interactive detail view and flagged it as a likely pipeline error to correct before evaluating. It is a real (if rare) artifact; excluding the affected windows is the correct fix (Spearman was already robust to it, but the corrupted windows mislead on inspection).
**Next:** the same discontinuity frames could touch a few STIM/peri-stim windows too — negligible but worth a guard there if a reviewer asks. Root cause (redoSVD mode-1 step) is a core/vendored-util issue; not worth fixing there given <0.03% frame impact.

### 2026-07-02 — Spontaneous contra→ipsi R² vs brain state: MOTION null, RELATIVE-δ +0.29 (power-indep); variance/abs-δ confounded
**Changed/Found:** built the R²-vs-state plots on spontaneous (non-stim) data (534 clean 1.5 s windows, local R² per window, exported `cp_spont_r2_vs_state.png`). Tested 4 states, flagging power-independence: **Motion energy (mean|z|) ρ=−0.01 p=0.8 → NULL** (power-indep, behavioral); **Variance var(y) ρ=+0.79** (power-CONFOUND — var is R²'s denominator); **abs δ power 1-4 Hz ρ=+0.67** (power-CONFOUND — ∝ variance); **Relative δ (δ/total) ρ=+0.29 p=2e-11 → genuine** (power-indep, spectral). ⇒ the two interpretable (power-independent) panels: predictability is motion-INVARIANT and modestly HIGHER in synchronized states (more relative low-freq power → contra tracks ipsi better; bilaterally coherent slow waves). Motion source: `d_tmp.motion.motion_1(1:2:end)` (base `mot_full`, Fs=35). Predictor: full contra readout `w=U_svd_raw(:,1:K)'*h_pixw`, DC-aligned on train.
**Why:** user asked for R²-vs-{motion,variance,frequency-power} plots on spontaneous data. Confirms the signal-power confound diagnosis (variance/abs-δ inflate to ~+0.7) and isolates the two admissible states: motion (null) and relative δ (+0.29). This is the salvageable, defensible predictability-vs-state result — replaces the retracted absolute-DV variance/δ "local state-dependence".
**Next:** (1) productionize as `[CP-PREDQ]` section + helper `cp_spont_predq.m`; (2) add pupil if available (2nd power-independent state); (3) check whether relative-δ +0.29 survives controlling motion (likely, since motion is null); (4) replicate on AL_0041; (5) this is the cleaner manuscript framing for §pre-stim brain state.

### 2026-07-01 — ⚠ CRITICAL (refines prior entry): variance/δ "state-dependence" is a SIGNAL-POWER confound; direction is metric-dependent
**Changed/Found:** confirmed the predictability-vs-state relationship directly on the SPONTANEOUS (non-stim, training-domain) recording — 534 clean 1.5 s windows (all frames ≥1.5 s pre / 2.5 s post any stim onset), full contra→ipsi predictor (DC-aligned on train). Replicated the exact pipeline state defs (`pv=var(y_full window)`, `dp=1-4 Hz Hann-FFT power`). **Result — the direction FLIPS by metric:** RELATIVE predictability corr(actual,pred) vs PreVar = **+0.77** (PreDelta +0.64), local R² +0.79/+0.67 → predictability IMPROVES with variance; ABSOLUTE error mean|res| (= the `devL1` metric) vs PreVar = **+0.31** (PreDelta +0.32), MSE (= `devP`) +0.37/+0.37 → absolute error GROWS with variance. The stim `devL1`~PreVar (+0.359) reproduces exactly as the non-stim ABSOLUTE-error branch (+0.31-0.37). **Root cause: `PreVar` is `var(y)` = the signal's own power, so it is mechanically entangled with every residual metric** — absolute error grows because the signal is bigger; relative predictability grows because low-var windows are noise-dominated (SNR). Neither is a clean brain-state effect.
**Why:** user asked to confirm the predictability-state dependence on the non-stim training data. Doing so exposed that (a) the earlier "reframe to predictability DEGRADES in synced states" is WRONG in direction (relative predictability INCREASES with variance), and (b) the whole variance/δ "state-dependence" — in either direction — is a signal-power confound, not interpretable as brain-state modulation of the local response. Supersedes the reframe in the entry below.
**Next:** (1) DROP any variance/δ "local state-dependence" claim built on absolute-deviation DVs (`devL1`/`devP`/`devS`) — it is a magnitude confound, present identically in non-stim data. (2) The ONLY admissible state-dependence test uses a state variable independent of the signal's power: **pupil, behavioral motion, or a NORMALIZED spectral ratio (relative δ)** — now required, not optional. (3) Motion result stands (behavioral, power-independent; Local motion-null, effect genuinely Global). (4) Salvageable descriptive result: inter-hemispheric predictability (R²) is HIGHER in high-variance/synchronized states on spontaneous data (+0.77) — but flag the signal-power confound. (5) Update FINDINGS.md headline + manuscript accordingly.

### 2026-07-01 — ⚠ CRITICAL: residual variance/δ "local state-dependence" is PREDICTION-QUALITY, not stim-specific
**Changed/Found:** stress-tested the S15 [CP-SETUP] headline (Local L1-dev ~ PreVar partial +0.213, PreDelta +0.166) against the "bad-prediction-in-certain-states fakes a local state effect" confound. Compared the stim-window deviation `devL1` (0-200 ms) to the SAME deviation metric in the matched NO-STIM pre-onset window `devP` (pure prediction error). **Result (AL_0033 0129 e1):** the artifact channel is real and LARGE — `corr(devP, PreVar)=+0.395` (≥ the raw stim effect `corr(devL1,PreVar)=+0.359`); `corr(devP,PreDelta)=+0.427` ≥ +0.334. The two control methods DISAGREE: partial `corr(devL1,state|devP)` survives (+0.213 / +0.166) but the like-for-like excess `corr(devL1−devP,state)` is NULL (+0.037 / −0.015). Binned overlay (6 state quantiles) shows the stim-window and no-stim-window deviation curves are SUPERIMPOSED across the whole state range (overlapping SEM everywhere). ⇒ the partial survives only via regression dilution (noisy `devP` down-weighted); the stim adds NO state-dependence beyond baseline prediction quality. The effect is **regional inter-hemispheric PREDICTABILITY being state-dependent** (contra→ipsi prediction degrades in synchronized/high-variance states, with or without stim), NOT a state-dependent LOCAL stim response. Motion result unaffected (Local motion-null −0.046; effect genuinely Global).
**Why:** user flagged the exact CP-STIMAFF logic applied to state ("what if a bad predictor is leading to residual state-dependence"). The `dev_pre` partial control is NOT airtight: `devP` is (a) partly tautological with PreVar (same pre-window signal) and (b) a noisy proxy, so the partial under-controls. The decisive test is stim-vs-no-stim excess, which is null.
**Next:** (1) REFRAME the manuscript claim from "local photoinhibition response is state-dependent" to "inter-hemispheric predictability is state-dependent" (better controller bridge: synced state→less predictable→higher CL MSE); (2) confirm with an INDEPENDENT state (pupil) to break the PreVar tautology; (3) productionize this as a [CP-PREDQ] control section; (4) re-check whether the FINDINGS.md "state-robust to motion" headline needs the variance/δ caveat added. Do NOT re-assert the local-stim state claim without the excess test passing.

### 2026-07-01 — cp_cosuppression.png recolored to SIGN-based overlay (whole 95% reads blue)

### 2026-07-01 — [CP-STIMAFF] prominent co-suppression figure (cp_cosuppression.png)
**Changed/Found:** added a standalone prominent figure to `utils/cp_stimaffect.m` (exports `cp_cosuppression.png`, 300 dpi): left = R0 mean-onset-deflection map over the transposed brain (whole contra hemisphere flooded blue, ipsi stim site marked green + on the opposite side); right = histogram of R0 across all 80987 contra pixels with the negative mass shaded, annotating "95.2% co-suppressed (R0<0), median R0=−0.162%". **Next:** none.

### 2026-07-01 — [CP-STIMAFF] (new S14): "bleed-free" robustness — excluding more stim-affected px is predictor-loss, not bleed
**Changed/Found:** new section `[CP-STIMAFF]` (S14) + helper `utils/cp_stimaffect.m`, answering the objection that the [CP-BLEED] "bleed-free" set may miss stim-affected contra pixels. The [CP-BLEED] SLOPE test centers each pixel over trials (`b_R−mean(b_R,2)`) then regresses on amplitude, so it only sees the amplitude-GRADED part of the stim response and discards the amplitude-INVARIANT onset deflection. Recovered that missing component: **R0 = mean peri-stim deflection per contra pixel** (748 stim trials, [0,200]ms − [−500,0]ms). **Results (AL_0033 0129 e1):** SLOPE test flags 1623 px (2%); the amp-invariant ONSET test flags 29623 px (36.6% at p<0.05) and **95.2% of the contra hemisphere shows a NEGATIVE onset deflection** — i.e. near-whole-hemisphere network co-suppression, not focal bleed. Exclusion sweep: dropping the top-f% most onset-responsive contra pixels deepens the peri-stim residual dip (72%→103% of actual as f: 0→50%) but ONLY by destroying the predictor (non-stim held-out R² 0.865→−0.577). Plotted as **residual-dip vs R², the onset-targeted and RANDOM-exclusion curves coincide** (at matched R²≈0.82: onset 79% vs random 78%). So excluding more stim-affected pixels is predictor-loss, NOT removal of hidden bleed absorption. The amplitude-graded SLOPE test is the correct bleed-specific criterion because optical/vascular bleed scales with laser power; a constant-across-amplitude deflection is genuine co-suppression. Exports `cp_stimaffect.png` (resDip-vs-R² curve + R0 co-suppression map + amp-graded-bleed vs onset-responsive spatial map). Renumbered residual workbench S14–S21 → S15–S22.
**Why:** user asked how the bleed-free characterization was made and whether more stim-affected pixels should be excluded. The honest answer: the two "stim-affected" definitions differ 18× (2% graded vs 37% onset-responsive) because the contra hemisphere genuinely co-suppresses — but that co-suppression is the very trans-callosal coupling the predictor is meant to read, so removing it degrades prediction indistinguishably from random pixel removal. No hidden bleed is being missed; the ~28% the predictor absorbs is real distributed network signal.
**Next:** none. If a reviewer still wants a stricter predictor, the amp-graded bleed set (1623 px) is already droppable at zero cost ([CP-CLEANRES]); going further trades R² for nothing. Replicate on AL_0041 once sites localized.

### 2026-07-01 — [CP-CLEANRES] (new S13): deploy the bleed-free predictor on STIM trials — local residual is preserved
**Changed/Found:** new section `[CP-CLEANRES]` (S13) in `contra_prediction.m` + helper `utils/cp_cleanres.m`. Deploys the bleed-free readout from `[CP-CLEAN]` (drop every contra pixel with bleed perm p<0.05; 1623 of 80987) two ways: **(A)** held-out NON-STIM primary-pixel R² — FULL 0.865 vs bleed-free 0.865 (Δ=−0.0004); **(B)** applies both full and bleed-free predictors to the REAL peri-stim windows (b_onf/b_amp, 748 stim trials) and forms residual = actual_ipsi − contra_prediction. **Result:** actual dip (0-200 ms) −1.557 %dF/F; FULL residual dip −1.119 (72% of actual), bleed-free residual dip −1.132 (73%). Per-amplitude gap |bleed-free − full| ≤ 0.03 %dF/F across all 9 amps — including the strongest (4.90) where bleed is largest. Same no-refit pixel-subset identity (orthonormal contra modes). Exports `cp_cleanres.png` (peri-stim traces + residual overlay + per-amp dip bar). Renumbered residual workbench S13–S20 → S14–S21.
**Why:** user request — "use the bleed-free for prediction… do it on stim sessions to see if bleed-free actually has any stim residual or not." During stim the bleed pixels carry the ipsi→contra leaked signal, so only a FULL predictor could absorb the local response through them. Since the bleed-free residual dip matches the full one at every amplitude, the isolated LOCAL stim effect is NOT a bleed-absorption artifact — the ~28% the contra predictor absorbs is distributed network co-suppression carried equally by bleed-free pixels. This is the peri-stim complement to the non-stim disjointness shown in [CP-CLEAN].
**Next:** none. Optional: replicate on AL_0041 once its sites are localized; the state pipeline (cp_residual_core) could adopt the bleed-free readout as the predictor of record, but since bleed-free ≈ full the state results would be unchanged.

### 2026-07-01 — [CP-CLEAN] (new S12): predict ipsi from BLEED-FREE contra pixels — bleed & predictive maps are DISJOINT
**Changed/Found:** new section `[CP-CLEAN]` (S12) in `contra_prediction.m` + helper `utils/cp_clean_predict.m` marrying `[CP-KRECON]` (predictive weight |h_pixw|, S10) and `[CP-BLEED]` (dose-response slope |b_beta|, S11). Uses the same no-refit pixel-subset identity (orthonormal contra modes) to re-score held-out primary-pixel R² under three exclusion curves vs % contra pixels removed: most-BLEED, most-PREDICTIVE, RANDOM. **Result (AL_0033 0129 e1, full R²=0.865):** the two populations are spatially DISJOINT — Spearman ρ(|β|,|pixw|)=−0.056, top-2% bleed ∩ top-2% predictive = **0 px overlap** (bleed hugs the midline contra edge nearest the stim site; predictive is spread across lateral contra). Excluding top-5% most-bleed barely dents R² (0.865→0.843, −0.022) ≈ random (0.863); excluding top-5% most-predictive collapses it (→0.596, −0.269); **keeping ONLY bleed-free pixels (perm p≥0.05, dropping 1623 px) leaves R²=0.865 (−0.000)**. Exports `cp_clean_predict.png` (exclusion sweep + top-2% bleed-vs-predictive spatial overlap). Renumbered the residual workbench S12–S19 → S13–S20.
**Why:** user request — "using the mask from bleed use all the pixels that are not bleed affected to predict the ipsi." This is the strongest form of the confound argument: the contra→ipsi prediction is not riding on ipsi→contra bleed, since the predictive signal survives full removal of the bleed-affected region and the two pixel sets are functionally separable.
**Next:** none. Optional supplementary panel; replicate on AL_0041 once its sites are localized.

### 2026-07-01 — [CP-KRECON] redefined as PIXEL-isolated recon: contra→ipsi coupling is DISTRIBUTED, not focal
**Changed/Found:** replaced the component-based `[CP-KRECON]` (deleted `utils/cp_kernel_recon.m`) with a PIXEL-isolated reconstruction (new `utils/cp_pixel_recon.m`), per user intent ("take the contra region with most weights and use that for prediction"). Predict the ipsi primary pixel using only the top-|weight| contra PIXELS: since the contra SVD modes are orthonormal the readout is a pixelwise weighted sum, so `yhat_topM(t)=V(:,t)'*(U(topM,:)'*pixw(topM))` (no refit); rank pixels by |h_pixw|, accumulate top f%, DC-align on train, score held-out R². **Result (AL_0033 0129 e1, full R²=0.865):** top 1% (focal hotspot, 810 px) → R²=0.29; top 5% → 0.69; top 20% → 0.77; **~50% of the hemisphere needed for 90%**. So the coupling is SPATIALLY DISTRIBUTED — a focal high-weight region alone predicts poorly. Reconciles with the component recon (1 canonical component ≈ 90%): that dominant component is a broad bilateral mode, so in pixel terms it needs many pixels. Exports `cp_pxrecon.png` (R²-vs-region-size + weight map + example trace + top 1/5/20% masks). User decision: REPLACE (not keep both).
**Why:** the original `[CP-KRECON]` ranked canonical components (low-rank story); user wanted the spatial question — how large a contra region is actually needed. The distributed answer is a distinct, citable result (global vs focal homotopic coupling) and matches the S09 observation that the weight hotspot is focal but the predictive info is spread.
**Next:** none. Optionally quantify the distributed-vs-focal contrast as a supplementary panel; replicate on AL_0041. (Stale `paper/cp_krecon_*.png` from the old component version are orphaned; paper/ is gitignored.)

### 2026-07-01 — [CP-HEMI] prediction-quality diagnosis: model sound, poor segs = local signal + low-var artifact
**Changed/Found:** diagnosed the poor-prediction held-out segments in `[CP-HEMI]` (S08), AL_0033 0129 e1, laser-center fit (rank 17, primary-pixel raw R²=0.847 / DC-anchored 0.862 / shape 0.894). (1) Confirmed the predictor is fit AND scored on NON-STIM data — frames are inter-stim gaps, dropping `h_settle_s=1.0 s` after each onset (`h_win_mode='interstim'`); 60k spont frames, 40k train / 20k test. (2) Per-3-s-segment shape R² (n=190): median 0.894, only 1 seg (<0.5%) negative. What predicts a low shape R²: **segment variance ρ=+0.72 p≈0** (dominant — R²-metric artifact: low SST punishes tiny errors), motion ρ=−0.12 p=0.11 (n.s.), since-preceding-stim ρ=−0.006 p=0.93 (null → 1 s settle adequate, no leakage). (3) The genuine failures (seg 48 only-negative; seg 14 high-var underperformer) are LOCAL ipsi dynamics the contra hemisphere cannot see — i.e. the residual "signal", not a bug; seg 30's early error tracks a motion spike (z=1.45) at onset.
**Why:** user wanted to debug poor prediction and change the model if needed. Diagnosis says DON'T change it: worst cases are (a) low-variance metric artifact, (b) genuine local activity that the residual pipeline is designed to isolate — a model tuned to predict these would absorb local signal into the global term. Lags already tested worse (pX=0 R²=0.905 vs pX=10 0.880); motion regressor deliberately excluded to keep motion testable as a state.
**Next:** none required. Optional: `h_K`/rank sweep to quantify the spontaneous-R² ceiling if a reviewer asks; keep model as-is otherwise.

Rationale: after A2 retarget to the laser center, template-gain collapses (borderline) but L1-dev is the robust effect. **Verified end-to-end at the laser center:** PRIMARY L1-dev partial(state|pre) — Motion −0.046 (p=0.21, null), PreVar **+0.213** (p=9.5e-9), PreDelta **+0.166** (p=8e-6); clean monotonic dose-response; decomposition Actual +0.75/Global +0.73/Local +0.36 (Local retains an independent chunk). ### 2026-07-01 — A2 retarget to laser center MATERIALLY shifts the state finding (gain→L1-dev)
**Changed/Found:** `cp_residual_core.m` — new `opts.use_data_site` (default TRUE): retarget the primary pixel from the flip-prone `params.pixel` (inhibition RIM) to the DATA-DERIVED laser center [row 373 col 353] (`cp_stim_site` cache) and re-extract `y_full` there (recording-kernel mean %dF/F), BEFORE the ROI mask + HEMI kernel readout so everything (predictor footprint, residual) is at the true site. Reverses the earlier "do not retarget" decision at user request. **Impact — the rim pixel was inflating the directional effect.** Held-out R² 0.896→0.890 (pixel 0.885→0.865, rank 43→19; dip deeper, residual recovers 72% vs 65%). Template-gain (signed) partial(state|pre) **collapses**: PreVar −0.215→**−0.080** (p=0.031, borderline), PreDelta −0.217→**−0.115** (p=0.002). But L1-dev (unsigned trial-to-trial deviation) is **ROBUST**: PreVar +0.208→**+0.213** (p=9.5e-9), PreDelta +0.196→**+0.166** (p=8e-6). Bleed control at center: PreDelta confound rejected; PreVar A1 bleed~state now +0.104 (p=0.005, mildly state-dep) but the (weak) gain effect still survives bleed control (A3 −0.085); catch null clean.
**Why:** the recording rim pixel sits ~60 px off the focus where inhibition is weaker and its SIZE is more state-modulable → a strong directional gain effect. At the saturating focus the response SIZE is state-robust (weak gain) but its trial-to-trial CONSISTENCY tracks state (strong L1). ⇒ the defensible headline at the correct site is the PREDICTABILITY/variability (L1-dev) effect, not the directional size (template-gain) effect.
**Next:** DECISION for user — swap DV primacy to L1-dev (primary) given it's the robust effect at the true site? Re-run `cp_bleed_control` around L1 (currently built on `R.gain`). Re-cut `[CP-VAR]/[CP-DELTA]/[CP-LOCAL]` panels at the new site. Localize AL_0041 sites so it inherits the retarget too.

### 2026-07-01 — A4 pre-onset state window: state effect is NOT onset-span circularity
**Changed/Found:** `cp_residual_core.m` — new `opts.state_win` ('peri' default = var/delta [−1,+0.5]s spanning onset; 'pre' = strictly [−1,0]s ending at onset, motion likewise [−2,0]s). Rerun on the retargeted (laser-center) pipeline: peri vs pre partials are essentially identical — gain PreVar −0.080 vs −0.075, PreDelta −0.115 vs −0.119; L1 PreVar +0.213 vs +0.175, PreDelta +0.166 vs +0.127 (all still p<1e-3). The `dev_pre`-partial safeguard was doing its job; removing the +0.5 s onset span does not change the conclusion.
**Why:** review flagged mild circularity — the peri-stim var/delta windows extend 0.5 s past onset into the stim response, overlapping the DV window. The pre-onset rerun confirms the state-dependence is driven by genuine PRE-stim state, not by the window peeking at the response.
**Next:** none (robustness confirmed). Keep 'peri' as default (more samples, matches the 2026-06-26 window decision); cite 'pre' as the robustness check.

### 2026-07-01 — Bleed state-dependence control: residual state-dep is NOT a bleed artifact
**Changed/Found:** `cp_residual_core.m` (new per-trial `R.bleed` + `R.catch`), new `cp_bleed_control.m`, `contra_prediction.m` new `[CP-BLEEDCTRL]` section. Confound tested: stim leaks ipsi→contra, contra is the predictor, so a STATE-MODULATED bleed could let the predictor absorb a state-varying share of the local response and fake the headline (pre-stim var/delta → weaker local gain). `R.bleed` = per-trial projection of the contra dip-window response (mode space, 0-200 ms vs 500 ms pre) onto the amplitude-graded contra bleed axis, z-within-amp, aligned to `R.gain`. `R.catch` = same state windows + residual dip energy + bleed proj on amp-0 (no-stim) trials. **Results (AL_0033 0129 e1):** (A1) bleed itself is NOT state-dependent — PreVar ρ=+0.075 p=0.046 (borderline), PreDelta ρ=−0.013 p=0.72 (null). (A2→A3) the gain-vs-state partial SURVIVES/strengthens when per-trial bleed is added as a control: PreVar −0.215→**−0.229** (p=6e-10), PreDelta −0.217→**−0.221** (p=2.6e-9). (A4) a state×bleed interaction is significant (var β=−0.079 p=0.013; delta β=−0.107 p=5e-4) = MODERATION, not mediation (main effect undiminished). (B) catch-trial null is clean: residual dip energy ⟂ pre-stim var/delta (ρ=−0.010/−0.002, p=0.89/0.98). Figure `cp_bleed_control.png` (bleed~state | partial ±bleed | catch null).
**Why:** validity requirement for claiming the method catches a state-dependent LOCAL stim response rather than state-dependent ipsi→contra bleed. Bleed being ~state-independent + effect surviving bleed control + catch null (needs an actual stim) together rule out the confound. User request 2026-06-28: "if the bleed itself from ipsi to contra is state dependent how can we add a control... so that we can claim that this method actually catches state dependent stim."
**Next:** Interpret the A4 moderation (state modulates trial-to-trial bleed↔residual coupling — a secondary effect, not an artifact) — decide if it warrants a panel. Replicate `[CP-BLEEDCTRL]` on AL_0041 e1/e2. Bleed axis is amp-defined in mode space — sanity-check vs the `[CP-BLEED]` FDR-sig pixel set if a reviewer wants a pixel-space bleed measure.

### 2026-07-01 — Residual state DV switched to template-gain (primary) + L1-dev (secondary)
**Changed/Found:** `cp_residual_core.m`, `cp_agl.m`, `cp_cont_state.m`, `contra_prediction.m` `[CP-SETUP/LOCAL/MOTION/VAR/DELTA]` — replaced the per-trial DV from `dev_stim` (unsigned deviation from amp-mean) to **template-gain** `g = <r,μ>/<μ,μ>` (signed/directional projection of each trial dip onto the amplitude-mean dip template; =1 for an average response), z-scored within amp, as the PRIMARY DV. **L1-dev** `mean|r−μ|` kept as SECONDARY. Both computed in `cp_residual_core` (per-trial loop, nan-safe `gd` mask, `den=max(Σμ²,eps)`) and mirrored in `cp_agl` (new signature `[dA,dG,dL,dA1,dG1,dL1,trA,trG,trL]` + `local_gain` subfunction). `S` struct: `r_partial/p_partial` now = template-gain partial(state|dev_pre); added `r_L1/p_L1`, `r_devSq/p_devSq` (old DV ref), `dv_primary/secondary`. `R.gain`, `R.devL1` added. Verified end-to-end on AL_0033 0129 e1 (site = data-derived rim pixel A, NOT retargeted per prior decision): held-out R²=0.896. **Result (partial, control dev_pre):** Motion +0.048 p=0.19 (NULL, headline-consistent); PreVar **−0.215** p=6.7e-9; PreDelta **−0.217** p=4.2e-9. Decomposition: the negative effect lives in the LOCAL residual (PreVar Local ρ=−0.224 vs Global +0.134, Actual +0.078; PreDelta Local −0.226) with a clean monotone dose-response. L1-dev mirrors (+0.208/+0.196); old dev_sq (+0.192/+0.183) agrees in magnitude.
**Why:** template-gain is directional (a shallower-than-template trial → gain<1, signed), giving a physically interpretable "response size" axis (high pre-stim variance/delta → WEAKER local inhibitory response) and it detects the state-dependence MORE strongly (bigger |ρ|, smaller p) than the unsigned dev DV. User decision 2026-06-28: "switch the DV to template-gain primary + L1-dev secondary" + "do not retarget" the site.
**Next:** `cp_motion_amp.m` (`[CP-MOTION-AMP]`, fig-3 replication) still uses the old |dev| metric — decide whether to switch it too. Then build the bleed state-dependence controls (catch-trial state null + bleed×state interaction). Replicate DV on AL_0041 e1/e2.
